probably think about more than you do.

**What you do not know.** Why a Dragon Knight just looked at your face like he
had seen a ghost. Or a nobleman.

**Opening move:** the guard has seen you and he is still staring. Run, brazen it
out, or vanish into the crowd and find out who these people are.

*Starting location: Caravan Square, Purewater. Game clock: d-3/day.*

### The Four Companions <!-- id: myth-lore-a32f052f760c -->

How the player characters meet: tracked Dragon Knights, a death-goddess's vision, a framed thief, and an investigator twin -- bound into an alliance over a soul-knife.

The campaign opens by drawing four (with Temerach, five) strangers together in the days before the tourney:

- **[[Gardwen]]**, sole survivor of a Dragon Knight massacre a decade past, follows the Baron's column to Purewater seeking the brother who vanished that night. Her wolf [[Ruhi]] walks beside her; the old powers keep her footfalls silent.
- **[[Magda]]**, an old pig-farmer and warrior of **Hel**, is sent by the death-goddess's nightmares to protect the others and "lay the dragon warrior to rest." She finds Gardwen at the city's ford and becomes her curmudgeonly bodyguard.
- **[[Randall]]**, a Purewater street-thief raised in the Pearl, is mistaken for the noble's son **Santo** at the city gate -- and then framed for the carving of [[Emmeralda]].
- **[[Conall]]**, an arcane investigator trained by the [[The Order of the Blue Star]] and riding in with the knight [[Temerach Nebulo]], is Randall's identical twin -- and, unknown to him at first, the Baron's son.

They meet bound to chairs in [[The Catacombs]], suspected of Emmeralda's murder, until Magda's testimony and Randall's account of "Lord Santo" clear them. Over the soul-knife Conall recovers from the crime scene, four strangers tied by fate forge an alliance: *"We could. We should." -- "Justice."* The twins' shared parentage, and Conall's, unspool from there.

*(See the GM-secrets lore for the full truth behind the party.)*

### The Gods of Mystamyr <!-- id: myth-lore-5f0681b87e74 -->

The Lake Lady (Nimue), patron of Purewater's waters; Hel, the northern death-goddess who sends Magda; Vacarus, god of war; and Asmodeus, the lord devil of the soul-knife.

**The Lake Lady (Nimue).** Purewater's patron: goddess of the sacred waters, of purification and abundance, healing and prophecy-in-reflection, and protection against corruption. Her [[The Order of the Lake Lady]] tends the temples and sacred pools; her knights, the [[The Swords of the Lake]], are held to account by the goddess herself. As the city's waters foul, her power is felt to wane.

**Hel.** The death-goddess of the Northern barbarians, grim and terrible. It is Hel who sends [[Magda]] -- through jarring, violent nightmares ending always in Magda's own death -- to protect the four companions and "lay the dragon warrior to rest." One does not serve the Goddess of Death without dread, and Magda curses her patroness even as she obeys.

**Vacarus.** The god of war, invoked by the city's people in oaths and exclamations ("Vacarus knows"); the central square of the old city bore his name in Magda's recurring vision.

**Asmodeus.** The lord devil whose runic sigil is emblazoned on the demonologist's **soul-knife** -- the instrument [[Santo di Teufel]] used to carve binding marks into [[Emmeralda]]. His mark on the blade is the unmistakable signature of the fire-demonology at the campaign's heart, and the sight of it makes even Hel's warrior recoil.

Lesser invocations heard on the street include **Nilo Valis**, patron spirit of thieves and quick improvisation, and the sea-gods of the Shoals and craft-gods of Merchant's Quarter.

### The Dragon Kings and the Lake Lady <!-- id: myth-lore-f7c4f9a0286e -->

The ancient draconic order, the uprising that destroyed it, and the older water-powers that resisted it -- the deep history the Baron is trying to revive.

In an age before the present order, the **Dragon Kings** ruled -- a draconic power of fire and dominion, served by an order of dragon-knights. They were thrown down in a great **uprising**, in which the [[The Swords of the Lake]] won their oldest battle honors. The realm of Mystamyr has remembered the Dragon Kings as a destroyed evil ever since; their heraldry is an unwelcome thing to see flying on a border lord's banners.

Older still are the **pre-human water-powers** -- the merfolk, the spirits of lake and sea, and the goddess the city calls the **Lake Lady**. These older powers resisted the draconic order then, and the water-faiths hold that they resist it now. The campaign's deep conflict is precisely this: the Baron's [[The Awake the Dragon Movement]] is an attempt to *revive* the Dragon Kings' transformations -- and his possession of the ancient dragon-knight spirit [[Thuban Eta]] is a first, monstrous proof that the old draconic magic can be made to work again.

This is the history the storyteller [[Thorne]] secretly carries, and why he -- a former Order of the Lake initiate -- can recognize the Baron's rituals for what they truly are.

### The Realm of Mystamyr <!-- id: myth-lore-4f25bed70921 -->

The wider world: the kingdom of Mystamyr under King Halvern, its capital Sharna, the lawless borderlands, and Purewater's place as its corrupt water-gate.

Purewater is a city of the realm of **Mystamyr**, a kingdom ruled by **King Halvern** from the capital, **Sharna**. It is an old land that remembers the **Dragon Kings** and the uprising that threw them down -- a memory that makes the Baron's dragon heraldry, openly flaunted, both strange and ominous to those who notice.

Beyond the settled heart of the kingdom lie the **borderlands**, a lawless march of forests, swamps ("kritch"), and scattered villages where raids, pogroms, and reprisals have run unchecked for thirty years. It is here that [[Baron Hanzo di Teufel]] rules as a sorcerous tyrant -- enslaving, murdering, exterminating elves, and committing atrocities always with enough deniability that King Halvern, far away in Sharna, suspects nothing. He is, in [[Temerach Nebulo]]'s judgment, the greatest existing threat to Mystamyr and the King's reign.

Purewater itself sits at the kingdom's watery edge, the gate between inland lake and open sea. It is governed in the King's name by his young nephew, **[[Prince Emeric]]** -- clever, idealistic, and hopelessly unsuited to a city like this one, where every faction runs circles around his good intentions. The smaller towns of the march -- **Anminster**, where [[Conall]] was raised by a militiaman's family; the woodland grove where [[Gardwen]]'s people were massacred -- feed travelers and tragedy alike toward the city and its tournament.

*(Setting note: this is the Purewater conversion of the original campaign, which set the same story in the southern city of Zorastor in the world of Greymoor. Names, gods, and a few institutions were relocated; the plot, characters, and themes are preserved.)*

### The Lake Lady's Tourney <!-- id: myth-lore-74a1179fcabc -->

Purewater's annual sacred tournament -- archery, single combat, and the unique Harbor Run -- and the stage the Baron means to hijack with his possessed champion.

The **Lake Lady's Tourney** is Purewater's most prestigious event: an annual sacred tournament honoring the goddess, older than the current political order, drawing competitors from across the realm. Because it is sacred, the Baron's plan to manipulate it is not mere politics but **desecration**.

**The events:**
- **Archery (the main event)** -- traditional targets, plus moving targets on boats between the islands and difficult cross-channel shots. Prizes carry court access and noble titles. This is the competition [[Temerach Nebulo]] has entered.
- **Single Combat** -- a dueling tournament of one-on-one matches. Prizes of guild apprenticeships and contracts. **This is where the Baron means to field his possessed champion, [[Cailan]].**
- **The Harbor Run** -- an obstacle course unique to Purewater: swimming, climbing, and boat-hopping through the canals and over rooftops, testing agility, strength, and local knowledge. Cash and local fame for the winner -- and a covert recruiting ground for criminal and military interests.

**Timing:** arrivals and preparations begin three days before the main event; registration and the sizing-up of competitors two days before; then the events proper. The tourney's opening is the campaign's hinge -- the first public viewing of the Baron's champion, and the moment [[Gardwen]] recognizes her stolen brother.

### The City and Its Islands <!-- id: myth-lore-502f5d9703aa -->

Purewater as a strait-city: the Mouth where the Lake drains to the Sea, the two land-jaws (Caravan Square and the Shoals), the great triangular High Isle and its quarters, and the scatter of Northern Isles.

## The shape of Purewater

Purewater is not a ring of districts around a centre -- it is a city built across
**the Mouth**, the narrow strait where the sacred **Lake** (to the **north**) drains
south into the **open Sea**. Hold that picture and the rest follows.

**The two jaws of the Mouth.**
- From the **western** mainland, a thin **promontory** reaches east to a larger
  outcrop: **[[Caravan Square]]** and the Gate of Triumph -- the only way into the
  city by land. This is the southern jaw.
- A second **peninsula**, set a little north and east, forms the seaward jaw and
  carries **[[The Shoals]]** -- the docks, fish-markets and smugglers' jetties that
  face the open sea.

**[[High Isle]]**, the heart, is a great irregular **triangular** island wedged
between the jaws, splitting the Lake's outflow. Its **south-west** point reaches
toward Caravan Square (the calmer landward channel); its **east** point toward the
Shoals (the seaward channel, the true Mouth); its **north-west** point faces the open
Lake. The **Grand Canal bisects and branches across it**, dividing its quarters:

- **[[The Merchant's Quarter]]** (south-west) -- trade, guilds, the Watercrafters,
  the merchant princes.
- **The official & noble quarter** (north-east) -- the Governor's Palazzo
  ([[Prince Emeric]]), Hall of Justice, Barracks, Palace Square, and the wealthy.
- **[[The Forge Quarter]]** & industry (east) -- forges and yards spilling into the
  Shoals; the Baron's **[[The DragonBarge]]** at its seaward pier.
- **The residential north edge** -- sloping rich-to-middling south-east toward the
  canal-mouth, beyond which lies **[[The Lullwater]]**, the poor-and-criminal quarter
  (the old "Maze" and "Warren" combined; its calm name a deliberate mask).

**[[The Northern Isles]]**, scattered across the Lake's calmer waters north of High
Isle: **[[The Pearl]]** (pleasure and nightlife), **[[Lost Isle]]** (drowned ruins;
the [[The Mermaid's Court]] beneath), quiet **court-family retreats**, and a few
**shunned or haunted** islets. Off the north-east edge, alone and unbridged, sits
little **[[Temple Isle]]** with its temple and the **Sacred Confluence**.

Boats are the streets; bridges stitch it together; **[[The Catacombs]]** run beneath
it all. The set-pieces the geography invites: canal chases through the Lullwater, the
Harbor Run across the Mouth, and the steam-wreathed corruption spreading from the
DragonBarge on the seaward channel.

## Journal

### Event 1 <!-- id: myth-event-cb5ec92165e1, type: gm-note, at: 2026-09-05T12:00:00.000000000, session: 0 -->

Campaign seeded: Purewater, living-world build. 12 agendas, 14 beats, four playable entry points. World clock starts at d-3/dawn; the Tourney is d0.

Nothing has happened yet. Run `tick` to start the world moving.

### Event 2 <!-- id: myth-event-052fc61db35a, type: session-start, at: 2026-09-06T05:30:05.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda -->

SESSION 1 -- Purewater, living-world build. Play opens at the ford outside the city, d-3/dawn, three days before the Lake Lady's Tourney. Gully plays Magda; Gardwen, Conall and Randall are GM-run.

Four arrivals in one week. Magda and Ruhi's mistress Gardwen are in the queue at the ford; Conall rides the Anminster road with Temerach; Randall works Caravan Square. Twelve agendas are live and the world clock is running.

### Event 3 <!-- id: myth-event-d7ac3aece545, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T06:53:40.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, The Ford -->

The ford: Magda walks out of the queue to the woman nobody will stand near, and asks her name.

Thirty feet of churned mud, and the whole queue has already decided the space around that woman belongs to somebody else's problem. At twelve feet the wolf stands up -- no growl, no teeth, just rising off its haunches to face her square, and every conversation within thirty feet stops.

Gardwen's hand goes inside her cloak. Not fast. The way you put a hand on a door latch in a house you don't trust.

Magda stops an arm's length outside what the wolf considers hers, nods once -- the nod you'd give a neighbour over a fence -- and asks: "Who are you?"

It lands wrong. She was braced for *what do you want* or *get that animal away from me*, and had answers ready for both. The hand comes out of the cloak. Empty.

"Gardwen," she says. And then, in a voice that has had this conversation before and expects nothing from it: "You'll want to be somewhere else, mother. People who stand near me have a poor time of it."

### Event 4 <!-- id: myth-event-9e3371cd7a1d, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T06:58:29.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Ruhi, The Ford -->

Magda walks past Gardwen on the wolf's side and puts her hand in Ruhi's ruff. Survival 12/63.

"I've been dreaming of you, girl."

She doesn't reach out early and she doesn't put a flat hand out the way townsfolk do. She walks the way you'd walk to a gatepost, on the wolf's side, which is the wrong side, which is the side nobody uses -- and puts her hand into the fur at the base of the neck where a hand disappears to the wrist.

The wolf locks. Its jaw comes round four inches from her forearm. She doesn't move her hand. She scratches, down, once, the way you'd work out a burr.

Up north they'd say the dog had decided you were furniture, and furniture is the highest rank a stranger gets.

"--*don't*," Gardwen says. Too late by three seconds. "Ruhi doesn't let people do that."

"My goddess tells me you're someone I need to help. Don't know why. That's what she told me."

"Your goddess," she says slowly. "Which one." -- and Magda can hear what the question actually is. She's asking whether this is one of the singing ones. Ruhi sits back down. Against Magda's leg, now, not hers. Gardwen notices that too.

### Event 5 <!-- id: myth-event-bf9036299dc9, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T07:02:28.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, The Ford -->

"Hel. Death. Not death in battle -- the other kind. Everywhere else." Gardwen: "That's an honest god."

"Not death in battle." Said the way you'd correct someone about which field the boundary stone is in. "The other kind. Everywhere else. In the bed, in the byre, in the bad winter. The ones nobody sings about."

Gardwen goes very still. It's the look of a bucket that's had the bottom go out of it -- she was braced for a priest, the singing kind, the kind who tell a woman with a dead family that it was a mercy and a plan. She had something sharp ready for that. She doesn't use it.

"No," she says quietly. "No, that's -- that's an honest god."

Then, before Magda can answer, she asks the real one instead, fast, like a woman pulling a splinter:

"Does she show you a boy?"

Her hand has gone into Ruhi's ruff beside Magda's. She doesn't seem to have noticed doing it. Her knuckles are white.

### Event 6 <!-- id: myth-event-4e1afa0634b2, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T07:06:56.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, The Ford -->

"No. Not a boy. A knight in dragon-scale." Then Magda kneels in the mud and offers her staff. Gardwen refuses it and hauls her up instead.

"A knight. Dragon-scale. Never seen anyone look that way before, and I've seen a great many people look a great many ways. He's dead, girl. Standing up and walking and dead, and nobody's told him."

Gardwen's knuckles in the ruff go from white to slack. Her eyes go north, to the churned mud where twenty-odd riders in scale went through at a pace that said *move*.

"Dragon Knights," she says. Flat. "It's Dragon Knights you dream of."

Then Magda kneels. The mud takes her knee with a sound like a boot coming out of a bog, and it is cold, and it goes straight through the wool, and she is seventy years old on the outside and there is nothing dignified about any part of it. Somewhere behind her one of the tumblers says something and another one laughs and is hit.

She holds out the staff crosswise, both hands, the way her grandmother did it to a man who died the following spring. "I'm to fight for you, girl. Protect you and yours, however you'd ask it. Die for you, if it comes to that."

Gardwen doesn't take the staff.

"Get up," she says. It comes out wrong. She tries again. "Get *up*. You'll -- the ground's freezing, you'll ruin your knees, get up--" And she's reaching down to haul her, one hand under her arm, and her grip is stronger than it looks and it is not steady at all.

"Ten years," she says, very low, only for Magda. "Ten years and not one person. Not one. And a pig-farmer kneels in the *mud* at a *ford*--" She stops. Swallows it. Puts the flat watchfulness back on like a hat, and it doesn't sit right any more, and she knows it doesn't.

"I don't know what to do with you," she says. Then, properly, offering her forearm the way soldiers do, the woodland version, wrist to wrist: "Gardwen. My brother was taken by men in that armour. Ten years ago. He's the only thing I have left to look for, and if your god has business with a dead man in dragon-scale then Hel and I want the same road for a while."

RECORDED: Magda gains the Passion **Sworn to Gardwen (by Hel) 70**.

AND THE THING NEITHER OF THEM CAN SEE: Gardwen has just heard *a knight in dragon-scale who is already dead* and filed it under the enemy who took her brother. She cannot connect it to *him*. Magda's dream is pointing straight at the thing she has walked ten years to find, and the graph will not let either of them know it.

### Event 7 <!-- id: myth-event-66b42fb7001b, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T07:08:24.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Ruhi -->

CH.1 -- The ford. Magda finds the woman from Hel's dream in the tourney queue, gets a hand into her wolf's ruff (Survival 12/63), tells her the dream, and kneels in the mud to swear herself to her. Gardwen -- alone ten years -- hauls her up rather than accept the staff, gives her name, and offers her forearm. They are travelling together.

Magda walked the wolf's side rather than the woman's, put her hand into Ruhi's ruff at the neck and scratched once; the animal locked, then settled, and afterwards sat against Magda's leg rather than Gardwen's.

MAGDA: 'Who are you?'
GARDWEN: 'Gardwen. You'\''ll want to be somewhere else, mother. People who stand near me have a poor time of it.'
GARDWEN: 'Your goddess. Which one.'
MAGDA: 'Hel. Death. Not death in battle. The other kind. Everywhere else. In the bed, in the byre, in the bad winter. The ones nobody sings about.'
GARDWEN: 'That'\''s an honest god.'
GARDWEN, asking the real question: 'Does she show you a boy?'
MAGDA: 'No. Not a boy. A knight. Dragon-scale. He'\''s dead, girl. Standing up and walking and dead, and nobody'\''s told him.'
GARDWEN: 'Dragon Knights. It'\''s Dragon Knights you dream of.'
MAGDA, kneeling, staff held out crosswise: 'I'\''m to fight for you, girl. Protect you and yours, however you'\''d ask it. Die for you, if it comes to that.'
GARDWEN, refusing the staff and hauling her upright: 'Get up. The ground'\''s freezing, you'\''ll ruin your knees, get up--'
GARDWEN, low: 'Ten years. Ten years and not one person. Not one. And a pig-farmer kneels in the mud at a ford--'
GARDWEN: 'I don'\''t know what to do with you.'
GARDWEN, offering her forearm: 'My brother was taken by men in that armour. Ten years ago. He'\''s the only thing I have left to look for, and I'\''m three days behind that column, and if your god has business with a dead man in dragon-scale then Hel and I want the same road for a while.'

MECHANICS: Survival roll 12 vs 63, success (northern kinship with wolves + Magda unafraid = Standard grade). No other rolls. Magda gains the Passion 'Sworn to Gardwen (by Hel)' 70.
NOTE: Magda told Gardwen the dream includes a dragon-scale knight already dead. Gardwen read this as the Dragon Knights who took her brother -- she did NOT connect it to her brother himself, and must not be allowed to until the reveal.

### Event 8 <!-- id: myth-event-3acbb7e495e3, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T07:11:47.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Purewater -->

The causeway. Gardwen asks about the dream twice more, from different sides. She never asks about the burning house.

Two miles of raised stone across a flat of reed and standing water, at the pace of the slowest cart. Gulls. The smell getting stronger and less like salt.

"Three days I've been eating their dust," Gardwen says. "Then they have to queue like everyone else, and I'm suddenly close enough to smell the horses. I don't know whether that's luck."

She talks a little, the way people do when they haven't in a while -- mostly about the road, nothing about the ten years. She asks twice more about the dream, in different words, hunting the same answer from different sides. She gets the same three things each time. A woman with a wolf. A burning house. A dead man in dragon-scale.

She doesn't ask about the burning house. Magda notices that she doesn't.

### Event 9 <!-- id: myth-event-847d5ba10952, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T07:12:56.000000000, session: 1, involves: Blau, Hanzo di Teufel, Tat Atarer -->

CH.2 (offscreen) -- The di Teufel column comes through the water-gate into Caravan Square at midday: twenty-odd Dragon Knights and a covered litter the outriders keep the crowd off. Randall, working the crowd, is close enough that a knight at the Baron's stirrup gets a clear look at his face and goes white.

Magda and Gardwen were still on the causeway and saw none of it. The city saw all of it. The litter's curtains were laced shut and the escort faced inward as much as out.

### Event 10 <!-- id: myth-event-6a8b3f50d0d3, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T07:17:44.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Purewater -->

"No bollocks from these fuckers." Magda walks up the line to the customs man and intimidates two women and a wolf into Purewater. Willpower 43/63.

She doesn't wait to be got to. She goes to him -- straight up the line past the fishmonger, past a family with a goat, until she's standing at his elbow with the wolf at her knee. Which is wrong, and everyone in the queue knows it's wrong.

He turns with his mouth already open. Something practised. *Back in line, mother.*

She looks at him. She has put her hand over the eyes of men who were shouting an hour before, and gone out afterwards and fed the pigs, because the pigs don't care. Whatever that does to a face, it has had fifty years to do it to hers, and she lets him have all of it.

"We're going in."

The mouth closes. He looks at the staff, which is not a walking stick and has never once pretended to be. He looks at the wet grey animal watching him with the total patient attention of something that has never in its life been told it's a good girl.

"There's a fee," he says. It comes out with no starch in it at all.

"There is." A pause long enough for him to understand she is not going to fill it.

"Two on foot," he says to his tally-board. "And a *dog*." He writes *dog*. He underlines *dog*, as though the underlining is a form of protest that history will one day recognise. "--and I never saw it."

"You never did."

And as she passes under the chain-boom, because he can't help it, because the thing has been sitting in the top of his head all morning: "Third lot today gone through with something they won't show me."

### Event 11 <!-- id: myth-event-00c1fbb4f0b9, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T07:21:36.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Ruhi, Purewater -->

Gardwen learns the ledges. A ferryman charges for the wolf and not for her, and won't say why.

There is no ground. That's the thing nobody tells you and Magda had forgotten. Buildings, and water, and between them ledges a yard wide, no rail, slick as an eel -- and the traffic that matters is underneath you, poled and rowed, shouting.

Gardwen stops dead six feet inside the gate with her hand knotted in Ruhi's ruff and her back to a wall, breathing like someone who has just been put somewhere with no trees. "There's no *ground*," she says.

She learns it the way woodland people learn things: badly for ten minutes, then all at once. Magda keeps her on the inside, hand flat on the wall, and after the third bridge she stops looking at her feet and starts looking at the water. After the fifth she's reading it -- which boat has right of way, which shout means *move*, which of the poled barges will take a passenger and which is somebody's home.

Ruhi hates all of it and does it anyway, low and close, ears flat, a wet grey misery at heel.

The ferryman across the Lullwater charges for the wolf and doesn't charge for Gardwen, and won't explain why.

Everywhere, bunting. Somebody's cousin selling favours in di Teufel colours off a tray -- dragon-scale green, three for a copper -- and doing well at it. Away over the roofs a bell starts up, and then another one, ragged, out of time with the first. The bells stop. Nobody they pass seems to know what they were for.

### Event 12 <!-- id: myth-event-fe237d07f40a, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T07:22:06.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Ruhi -->

CH.3 -- Into the city. Magda faces down the customs man at the water-gate by presence alone (Willpower 43/63) and gets two on foot and 'a dog' through the boom without paying. He lets slip he is the third lot today through with something they will not show him. Gardwen, woodland-born, is badly unsettled by a city with no ground. Magda takes them to the Sylph's Embrace on the Pearl.

MAGDA, to the customs man: 'We're going in.'
CUSTOMS MAN: 'There's a fee.' -- MAGDA: 'There is.' (then silence, which he filled by standing aside)
CUSTOMS MAN, writing: 'Two on foot. And a dog. Muzzled inside the Merchant's Quarter, that's not me, that's the Guild... and I never saw it.'
CUSTOMS MAN, as they passed under the boom: 'Everyone's mad this week. It's the Tourney. Third lot today gone through with something they won't show me.'
GARDWEN, six feet inside the gate, back to a wall: 'There's no ground.'
MAGDA: 'Yeah. It's all boats and barges and slipways here lass. Let's get to the Pearl.'
No toll paid. Bells started up over the roofs as they entered.

### Event 13 <!-- id: myth-event-6cbf79daf639, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T07:27:27.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Constantine, Emmeralda, Ila, The Sylph's Embrace -->

The Sylph's Embrace. Constantine opens the door twenty years older than the knock remembers. Ila drops an armful of linen at the sight of a wolf.

A green door under a carved lintel -- a sylph, wings folded, worn nearly smooth by two hundred years of hands. Magda knocks the way she knocked twenty years ago.

"*Magda.*" -- "Constantine." -- "You're not dead." He says it with genuine delight, as one professional to another. "Not yet. It keeps being suggested."

He takes in the rest of it in one pass -- the young woman standing too straight, the staff, the wolf -- and to his enormous credit the only thing that moves is one eyebrow, and only slightly. "Beds. It's Tourney week, I've got the city and half the border in my lap and every room's spoken for -- so you'll have the top back room over the water, and it's cold, and the shutter sticks, and I'll bring a brazier up. Bring the dog. If it eats a client I'll take it out of your purse."

A girl of about thirteen goes past the foot of the stairs with an armful of linen twice her size, sees the wolf, stops dead, and drops the entire load. "*Ila*," Constantine says, without heat. She isn't listening.

And on the landing above, leaning on the rail with her hair down: "Constantine, you've brought home a *wolf*." -- "I've brought home a *friend*, Emmeralda. The wolf is incidental."

Emmeralda takes Magda's chin in two fingers, unhurried, professional, examining. "Not one day. Not *one*. You were a hard old woman when I was a young one and you're the same hard old woman now, and I've had two decades of people telling me *my* face is unnatural." -- "Mine's a curse." -- "Mine's an *asset*."

Then, later, quietly, reading her the way Magda read the customs man: "You didn't come for the Tourney." -- "No." -- "No." She accepts that, and files it, and doesn't push.

### Event 14 <!-- id: myth-event-b83a2ab6b590, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T07:56:00.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Emmeralda, The Sylph's Embrace -->

Upstairs, over bread and cold fowl, Gardwen says the elf was afraid of something. "It's not a thing you can point at. The field's wrong."

Constantine brings it up himself: bread, a hard yellow cheese, half a cold fowl, a jug of the brown beer they make out on the Shoals, and a brazier he sets going with a professional's economy of movement. The shutter does stick, and when it opens the canal is directly below -- close enough to hear the water working at the stone, and a barge going through in the dark with a lantern on its bow.

Gardwen eats like someone who has been rationing without admitting it.

"That woman," she says eventually, with her mouth full. "The elf. She was afraid of something. Not of us. Underneath. The way a doe is when there's nothing in the field. It's not a thing you can point at, it's just -- the field's wrong."

Then she puts her cup on the sill and looks out at the dark water. "Magda. I've got no plan. That's the truth of it. I've followed that column for three days on nothing but *keep them in sight*, and now they're behind a wall somewhere in this city and I don't know one street from another and I've a wolf that can't be seen and no coin left worth the name. You said we'd plan. So plan at me, because I've nothing."

Down in the house someone starts up on a stringed instrument, badly, and is shouted at, and gets better.

And three floors below there's a knock -- the sort a young man gives a door when he's certain of his welcome -- and a young man's voice, well-bred, border vowels, asking by name for Emmeralda.

### Event 15 <!-- id: myth-event-e1c05dadee64, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T12:39:59.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Santo di Teufel, The Sylph's Embrace -->

Magda creeps to the turn of the stair and watches Santo di Teufel come into the house. Stealth 39/68.

She gets the door shut without the latch speaking and goes down as far as the turn above the second landing, where the overhang puts her in shadow and the hall lamp doesn't reach.

Constantine has his back to her. The young man is facing him -- hood back, dark clothes, road-dust still on the hem. Nothing in his hands. He's talking, quickly, and Constantine is nodding in the way he nods.

Then Emmeralda comes into view from the parlour door. She's changed since the afternoon: hair up, something green. She says something. The young man laughs.

Then she goes up the near stair -- the one that comes past the second landing, twelve feet from where Magda is standing -- and the young man follows her. They'll pass below her in a few seconds.

### Event 16 <!-- id: myth-event-77e736f74d7b, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T12:42:35.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Emmeralda, Santo di Teufel, The Sylph's Embrace -->

THE CHOICE NOT TAKEN: "Withdraw -- not my business." Magda watches Santo go up the stairs with Emmeralda and goes back to bed.

The shadow is good. It is not a wall. They are about to come up the near stair and pass within twelve feet of her, and staying put becomes a contested thing.

She has about three seconds to hold or withdraw.

"Withdraw. Not my business."

She goes back up. Gardwen doesn't ask. Ruhi resettles across the door and the house carries on being a house.

It is not her house and it is not her business and Hel did not walk her three hundred miles to mind a brothel door. Constantine has run this place for thirty years. Emmeralda is not a girl.

Some while later -- an hour, maybe more -- it goes wrong downstairs.

### Event 17 <!-- id: myth-event-914d41454956, type: gm-note, at: 2026-09-06T12:43:03.000000000, session: 1, involves: Emmeralda, Santo di Teufel, The Sylph's Embrace -->

OFFSCREEN, ROLLED NOT SCRIPTED: Santo's binding SUCCEEDS (Lore Demonology 19 vs Formidable 25). His escape FUMBLES (Stealth 100).

The written adventure has Santo botch the binding and leave Emmeralda dying. The dice said otherwise at the very first hinge, and the whole back half of the campaign came out of it.

**Santo -- Lore (Demonology), Formidable (50 -> 25). Rolled 19. Success.** His father does this work. He has watched. Whatever he came to that house to do, he did it.

**Then Stealth 50. Rolled 100. Fumble.**

There is no scream. There is a crash -- something big going over, a table or a chest, and glass after it -- and then a man's voice, high and completely undone, saying *no no no no* very fast, and a door hitting a wall hard enough to shake the frame two floors up. Running feet on the canal-side stair. A slip. A worse noise: a body hitting a landing full length and something metal skittering away across boards. A shutter thrown. And a splash in the canal below far too big to be a man getting neatly into a boat.

Then voices all over the house. Constantine bellowing. Someone hammering on a door. And under it all, from the room they went into: nothing at all.

CONSEQUENCE: because the binding took, Emmeralda lives -- comfortable, chatty, house-proud, and possessed. That is the only reason anyone in Purewater ever learns what a shade is.

### Event 18 <!-- id: myth-event-1d02bd14979f, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T12:46:47.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Ila, The Sylph's Embrace -->

Down three flights in the dark at seventy. Athletics 69/50 -- failure. Ila hits her on the second landing, white to the lips.

A failure here costs time and wind, not the trail. She arrives breathing hard.

It's **Ila**, barefoot in a shift, going flat out -- she hits Magda rather than stops, grabs two fistfuls of her skirt, and her face is white all the way to the lips.

"She won't *open* it--" She's hauling, trying to turn her round, back up. "The door's shut and she won't open it and Constantine's got the bar out and Emmeralda *won't answer*--"

Gardwen goes past both of them toward that door without breaking stride.

Ila's hands are still in her skirt. Below, the canal-side stair keeps going down -- past the boot room, to the water door and the slipway. Nobody has gone that way. Everyone in this house is going *up*. Down there in the dark it's quiet, and the water is still moving from whatever went into it.

### Event 19 <!-- id: myth-event-222e539f2407, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T12:49:00.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, The Sylph's Embrace -->

Magda goes DOWN instead of up. Boot room, water door, slipway -- loading the sling as she goes.

Ila's hands come off her skirt.

Boot room, then the water door -- unbarred, standing half open, swinging. Whoever came through here left it that way. She loads as she goes: stone in the pouch, cord round the knuckles, staff cocked back along the forearm.

Four stone steps going down into black water, green and slick below the third. A skiff moored to the ring at the bottom, riding oddly -- the painter's still tied but the boat's been shoved out and drifted back, and it's rocking hard, more than the canal's doing.

The water directly off the slipway is still working. Not ripples -- the deep slow turnover of something that went in heavy and recently.

One lamp on the far bank forty feet off. Under the surface, nothing at all.

Something is on the second step down, right at the waterline -- a shape that isn't stone.

### Event 20 <!-- id: myth-event-292e0c6ea7bf, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T12:50:25.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, The Sylph's Embrace -->

She hauls a soaked cloak off the mooring ring and scans forty feet of black water. Perception 51/70 -- the swimmer.

Heavy with canal, dark, good cloth, expensive, the hem still carrying road-dust under the wet. It was caught on the mooring ring. It did not come off a man who was being careful.

Thirty feet out and moving left, upstream: the fast ugly chop of somebody swimming badly and in a hurry. A head, the pale smear of a face turning back toward the house, and then just the shoulders working.

He's making for the far ledge where the lamp is. There's a set of iron rungs up the wall under it. Forty feet of open water between him and them, and he's covered maybe a third.

He hasn't seen her. She's a shape in a dark doorway and he's looking at the upper windows, where all the noise is.

The sling is loaded and the range is nothing.

### Event 21 <!-- id: myth-event-06f7955d32f2, type: combat, at: 2026-09-06T12:51:20.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Santo di Teufel, The Sylph's Embrace -->

"Kill whatever that is." Berserker style, Hard 33/57. 2d6=8 to the Head, no helmet. Santo di Teufel drowns thirty feet off Constantine's slipway.

Backlit against the far lamp, thirty feet, swimming hard. Dark water and a moving target. **Berserker combat style, Hard (85 -> 57). 33 -- a hit. 8 damage. Head. No helmet -- he's swimming. 5 hit points there; he's at -3. Serious wound.**

The staff comes over and the stone goes out flat across the water with that low buzzing note they make.

She doesn't hear it hit. She *sees* it -- the head snaps sideways, hard, wrong, the way a head does when it has stopped being steered from inside. The working shoulders stop working.

He goes under without a sound. No thrashing. No cry. The chop where he was smooths out into ordinary canal, and the wake he'd been dragging catches up with the place he isn't any more, and there is one hand above the surface for a moment, open, not grasping at anything -- and then that's gone too.

Forty feet away the lamp on the far bank goes on being a lamp. The iron rungs he was making for go on being empty. The water closes and keeps moving.

Behind her, up in the house, a woman starts screaming, and this one doesn't stop.

### Event 22 <!-- id: myth-event-a4ca5096577c, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T12:53:11.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, The Sylph's Embrace -->

Searching a wet cloak in the dark with feet on the stairs above. Perception 62/47 -- she doesn't finish.

Outer pockets first, because that's where a hurrying man puts things: a few coins, a folded scrap gone to pulp in the canal, a lump of resin or wax.

Then her hands find weight. Something solid, sewn in behind the lining at the breast -- small, hard, heavier than cloth has any business being -- and there's no seam to it on the outside, and it needs a knife and a minute and light.

She has none of the three.

The door at the top of the boot-room stair bangs off the wall. Lamplight comes down ahead of whoever's carrying it, swinging wild across the wet stone.

### Event 23 <!-- id: myth-event-1e6957998e03, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T12:54:43.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Constantine, Emmeralda, Ila, The Sylph's Embrace -->

Emmeralda is sitting up in bed, opened throat to hip, smiling at Constantine. "You needn't crowd. It doesn't hurt at all."

Too many people in the passage. Somebody's holding Ila by both arms and she is fighting to get past, silently, which is worse than if she were shouting. An older woman in a shawl is against the far wall with her hands over her mouth -- she's the one who was screaming, and she's stopped now, and she hasn't taken her eyes off the room.

Constantine is in the doorway with his back to it, not moving. Not going in. Gardwen is inside, three paces from the bed, standing very still with her knife still reversed in her hand.

The room stinks of scorched hair and something sweet under it, and there's chalk on the floorboards -- a worked figure, big, half scuffed through where someone stood in it. The bed is soaked black from the middle down.

Emmeralda is sitting up in it. She's opened from the hollow of her throat to her hip -- one long deliberate cut, still bleeding, not being held closed by anything. She has her head tilted slightly, and she's looking at the door, and she is smiling at Constantine.

"You needn't crowd," she says. Her own voice, perfectly level, pleasant. "It doesn't hurt at all."

### Event 24 <!-- id: myth-event-279483f48201, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T12:57:28.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, The Sylph's Embrace -->

The cloak opened out flat on a linen-closet floor: a signet with a worm eating itself, and a twist of oiled silk with dark red hair in it. Emmeralda's is black.

She backs out. Nobody notices -- everyone in that passage is looking at the same thing. Two doors down there's a linen closet with a lamp bracket. She gets the lamp, puts the cloak on the floor, opens it out flat, and uses the thumb-edge of her own belt knife on the stitching, which gives after a bit of arguing.

Two things come out of the lining.

A **ring**. Heavy gold, a man's, big enough to want a knuckle. A device cut into the face: a beast coiled round on itself, long, with the tail through its own jaws. Good work. Old work. Sewn into a cloak by somebody who did not want it on his hand tonight.

And a twist of **oiled silk**, still dry inside. In it: a few long hairs bound at one end with fine copper wire, and a flake of something brown and hard that isn't wax.

The hairs are not dark. In the lamplight they're dark red.

Emmeralda's are black.

### Event 25 <!-- id: myth-event-fe2203da17cb, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T12:59:38.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, The Sylph's Embrace -->

Perception, Easy 10/105 -- CRITICAL. She takes the cloak apart seam by seam. Eleven border coins, a tailor's tower, a ruined letter, and FOUR MORE twists of hair.

**The coins.** Eleven in all, and wrong for Purewater -- heavier, older, a different mint. Border coin. He hasn't changed money since he arrived, which means he arrived recently and hasn't needed to.

**A tailor's mark**, stitched inside the collar where the wearer never looks. Not a Purewater house. A little tower with three windows, embroidered rather than stamped -- a mark for somebody whose custom is worth the extra work.

**The pulped paper**, teased apart with the point of the knife and dried against the hot lamp glass. Most of it grey mush. Two things survive: part of a line in a fine clerk's hand -- `--ee days before the Lady's Tourney, at the hou--` -- and the bottom half of a wax seal. Broken. The same coiled beast as the ring.

**And in the left cuff**, folded over twice and stitched flat so it wouldn't rattle: a **second twist of oiled silk. Empty.** Whatever was in it has been used.

The first twist -- the dark red hairs and the brown flake -- has three more like it wrapped in the same bundle. All full. All hair. None of it is black.

Whatever he came here to do tonight, he had the makings for four more.

### Event 26 <!-- id: myth-event-4c8c058928b7, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T13:03:45.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, The Sylph's Embrace -->

"I killed him in the water." Gardwen: "Good." Then, quieter: "--and bad. Because someone paid for that cloak." Her Lore fails at 82/52.

Gardwen goes very still while Magda tells her. She doesn't interrupt.

"Good," she says about the killing. Flat, immediate, no hesitation in it at all. Then, a moment later, quieter, as the rest of it arrives: "--and bad. Because someone paid for that cloak."

She takes the twist of silk and turns it under the lamp. Holds it a long time. Her jaw works.

"Hair and wire." She shakes her head, and there's something close to anger in it -- at herself. "I know what a fever looks like in a badger. I know nine things you can do with willow. This is--" She hands it back. "I don't know what this is, Magda. I've never seen anything like it and I don't know anyone who has."

She looks at the four full bundles. Counts them, silently, her lips moving. "Four more. Four more *what?*"

Then, past her, at the doorway -- because inside the room Emmeralda has just asked Constantine, warmly, whether the young gentleman enjoyed himself and when he might call again. Constantine hasn't answered.

Gardwen's hand closes on Magda's wrist. "That's not her. Magda, whatever's talking in there, it doesn't know he's dead. **We could ask it things.**"

### Event 27 <!-- id: myth-event-37704feb2483, type: gm-note, at: 2026-09-06T13:04:24.000000000, session: 1 -->

PLAYER CORRECTION: "Insight is for emotions of people. Lore is for knowledge." Re-resolved: Gardwen has no applicable Lore, so there is simply no roll to make.

Insight is INT+POW -- reading people. Lore is INT x2 -- knowing things. Rolling Insight on a bundle of ritual components was the wrong skill entirely.

Re-resolved properly: analysing ritual components is **Lore**, and Gardwen's Lores are *Species* and two *Woodland Animal* specialisms. There is no roll to make -- she has no applicable knowledge, so she simply doesn't know. The Lore (Species) roll was legitimate and failed at 82/52: she can't say whose hair that is, or even what it came off.

The result was the same. The reasoning was sloppy, and the distinction went into CLAUDE.md.

### Event 28 <!-- id: myth-event-03861f98b0b6, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T13:06:14.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Emmeralda, The Sylph's Embrace -->

Walking into a chalk figure and a smiling wound with Fear (Arcane Magic) 54 in her chest. Willpower 42/63 -- she holds her face.

Failing wouldn't stop her going in. It means the thing gets to see what she is.

**42 against 63 -- held.** Her hands want to do something and she doesn't let them.

Constantine is sitting on the edge of the bed with his face in his hands. He looks up, and whatever he sees in her makes him get up and move aside without being asked.

Close to, the chalk figure on the boards isn't a circle. It's longer than that -- a shape with corners, and the scuffed break is at the foot, where a man standing inside would have stepped backwards out of it in a hurry.

The wound is the length of her. It doesn't clot and it doesn't run any more. It just sits there, open, moving very slightly with her breathing.

"Oh, *Magda*." Delighted. Warm. Exactly the voice from the bottom of the stairs that afternoon. "You look absolutely *dreadful*. Come and sit." She pats the coverlet. There's blood on her hand.

The thing in the bed watches her cross the floor and its eyes track a half-beat late -- like someone driving a cart they've only just been handed.

### Event 29 <!-- id: myth-event-47111569e95d, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T13:08:33.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Emmeralda, The Sylph's Embrace -->

"What's all this though?" It looks at its own wound the way you'd look at a stain on a good tablecloth. "That's the way in."

"That." It touches the edge of the cut with two fingers, incurious, and the flesh moves under them like a curtain. "That's the way in. He wasn't very neat about it, but he got there in the end, bless him -- he was *so* nervous, Magda, you've no idea. Shaking. I had to be kind to him."

The fingers come away red. It doesn't notice.

"Do I feel well?" It considers this seriously, as though nobody's ever asked. "I feel *marvellous*. I've been cold for --" a pause, and something goes across the face that isn't Emmeralda's expression at all, a sort of hungry blankness, and then it's gone and the warmth is back "-- well. A long while. And this is a very good house and everyone in it is so *fond* of her."

It smiles. It's Emmeralda's smile. It's slightly too wide and it lasts slightly too long.

"He's coming back, you know. He said so. He was very definite about it." It plucks at the coverlet, arranging it over the ruin of itself, tidy, house-proud. "So we should have the room straight. And someone really ought to sweep up that chalk -- it's *dreadful*, isn't it, he made such a mess of my floor."

### Event 30 <!-- id: myth-event-fe78ac548d80, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T13:10:56.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Constantine, Emmeralda, The Sylph's Embrace -->

Magda asks the demon for its master's name so "we know how tae greet him on his return" -- and gets Santo, four more women, and a father who would have to see.

It brightens at being consulted about hospitality.

"Santo," it says. "Just Santo -- he didn't give me the rest, they never do, they think it's clever." A little conspiratorial lean, and the wound gapes with the movement. "He'll want the green room, I should think. He liked the birds on my shawl."

Then, thoughtfully, arranging the coverlet again:

"He said he'd come back for the others. Four more, he said, and then his father would *have* to see. He was quite emotional about it." A small indulgent laugh. "Men and their fathers, Magda."

Behind her, Constantine has stopped making any sound at all. When she looks round he's got both hands flat on the doorframe and he's holding himself up with them, and his face has gone the colour of tallow.

"*Santo*," he says. "He said *Santo*."

### Event 31 <!-- id: myth-event-b70d8e249fec, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T13:13:55.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Constantine, The Sylph's Embrace -->

Constantine's counting room. "There is a dead di Teufel in the canal behind my house and a demon sitting up in my green room asking for her shawl, and there are nineteen people under this roof tonight." Gardwen: "Twenty-two. There are three in the kitchen."

"**Santo di Teufel** is the son and heir of **Baron Hanzo di Teufel**, who rode into this city this morning with twenty-odd Dragon Knights at his back for the Lady's Tourney, and who is at this moment the guest of half the men on the Governor's council." His voice is very level and it is costing him. "The boy has a name on the border. The kind you hear and change the subject."

Magda puts the ring on the table. Constantine looks at it and does not touch it. "That's their arms. The worm eating itself. It's on the litter, it's on the banners, it's on every second cup in this city this week."

"A priest means a report. A report means the Watch, and the Watch this week means the Governor, and the Governor is drinking with that man's *father*." He is quiet for a moment. "There is a dead di Teufel in the canal behind my house and a demon sitting up in my green room asking for her shawl, and there are nineteen people under this roof tonight."

Gardwen says, from the corner: "Twenty-two. There are three in the kitchen."

"Twenty-two." He closes his eyes. "What do we do?"

### Event 32 <!-- id: myth-event-20371345a43b, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T13:19:00.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Constantine, The Sylph's Embrace -->

THE PLAN: dump the body somewhere on the approach and let Purewater thieves take the blame. Constantine agrees instantly -- and then finds the hole in it.

Everything goes on the table under the lamp: four twists of oiled silk, the fifth one empty, the ring, the tailor's tower, the pulped paper with its half-line and broken seal.

Constantine looks at the hair for a long time. "Not hers. Emmeralda's is black to the root."

"Four more," Gardwen says.

"Four more *women*." He sits down slowly. "There are eleven houses on the Pearl. And that boy has had the run of this city for one day."

On the plan he doesn't hesitate. "Yes. God help me, yes. It's the only story that holds. A rich border boy comes to the Pearl with his father's gold on him and doesn't know the water, and he's found in the morning face-down off the Shoals with his purse cut and his rings gone. You've already done the hard part of that."

Then he stops. "But the body's in *my* canal, Magda. Behind *my* water door. And it went in heavy and it didn't come up. The outflow runs west past the Lullwater on the ebb -- that's two hours off yet, and until then he's sitting on the bottom in about nine feet of water, more or less where you put him. After the ebb he could be anywhere, and 'anywhere' includes the grating at the Shoals, which they clear every morning with a *crowd* watching."

"So it's now, in the dark, with a boat and a hook and nobody who talks. Or it's not at all."

Gardwen is already pulling her cloak on. "I can hold my breath a long time. I grew up on a river."

### Event 33 <!-- id: myth-event-62281776e769, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T13:22:38.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Constantine, The Sylph's Embrace -->

Gardwen dives for the body. Swim 88/36 -- she fails badly, and nearly takes the skiff over with her.

Constantine's skiff, a boathook, a coil of line, and a shuttered lantern with the slide almost closed. Nine feet of black. He works the pole and says nothing. He's put a sack in the bottom of the boat and hasn't explained it.

Gardwen ties the end of the line round her own waist with a knot Magda doesn't recognise, and her hands are not quite steady. "River," she says, mostly to herself. "It's just a river with walls."

**Gardwen -- Swim, Easy (24 -> 36). Rolled 88. She fails badly.**

She goes in clean enough. Then the line stops running. Then it starts *thrashing*. Whatever she grew up on, it did not have nine feet of black water, no bottom she can stand on, and cold that takes the air out of you on the way down.

She comes up eighteen inches from the gunwale, not where she went in, and she is not swimming. She's climbing -- hands scrabbling at the water like it's a wall -- and she gets one arm over the side and hauls, and the skiff goes right over onto its beam ends with her weight on it. Freezing canal comes in over the rail.

### Event 34 <!-- id: myth-event-c91d0ca6c884, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T13:23:48.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, The Sylph's Embrace -->

Brawn 18/71 -- Magda doesn't pull her over the side, she pulls her ALONG it, aft to where the freeboard is lowest. "That is not a river."

She doesn't pull her over the side. She pulls her *along* it -- one fist in the line and the other in the back of her shirt, dragging her aft toward the stern where the freeboard is lowest, taking her weight off the rail and putting it where the boat can carry it.

The skiff comes back down flat with a slap that throws water everywhere. Constantine sits down hard.

Then both hands under her arms and in over the transom like a netted fish, and she lands in six inches of bilge and stays there on hands and knees, coughing up canal, shaking so hard you can hear her teeth.

It takes her a while. "That," she says eventually, "is not a river."

Gardwen is soaked through, freezing, and no use to anyone in that water again tonight. The boathook is in the bilge. Eight feet of it, iron-shod. Nine feet of water.

Constantine says quietly: "The ebb starts in less than two hours."

### Event 35 <!-- id: myth-event-a5eabf2910ac, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T13:24:34.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, The Sylph's Embrace -->

"How good am I at swimming?" Swim 27. Worse than Gardwen and fifty years older. Magda rules herself out.

**Swim 27.** Three points better than Gardwen's 24 and fifty more years on it -- close enough to make no difference. Whatever Gardwen just did, Magda would do it harder.

What she is actually good at, in that boat: **Brawn 71** and **Perception 70**. The hook is eight feet and the water's nine, which is a foot short unless you're leaning, and it's a big canal to find one man in by touch.

She does not go in the water. That is the whole of the decision, made in about four seconds, and it is what forces everything that follows.

### Event 36 <!-- id: myth-event-d417a76c6a64, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T13:25:37.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Constantine, Ila, The Sylph's Embrace -->

"Constantine. How good a swimmer is Ila?" -- and Constantine does not answer straight away.

He looks at the water, and then at Gardwen shaking in the bilge, and then somewhere past both of them.

"She's from the Shoals. Her mother worked the tide-flats before she came to me. Those children are in the water before they can walk." A pause. "She's the best swimmer in this house by a long way. She dives for lost oars for a copper. She once went under the *Lullwater* bridge on a bet and came up the other side, and I took the skin off her for it and she did it again."

He is quiet for a moment. "She is thirteen years old, Magda."

Then, because he is not a stupid man and he has understood exactly what the question was:

"And she is downstairs right now, awake, being held by two of my women so she doesn't go and sit with Emmeralda, and if you go down and ask her to do this she will say yes before you've finished the sentence.

"She'll say yes because it's you asking, and because she thinks Emmeralda is hurt and this is helping. She won't know what she's touching down there until she's touching it."

The canal moves under the boat.

"That's the whole of it," he says. "It's your asking, not mine."

### Event 37 <!-- id: myth-event-9996d16a5130, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T13:27:20.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Constantine, Ila, The Sylph's Embrace -->

"Go get her. This'll be good for her. She'd be doing something to help, not crying in a corner." Constantine goes himself, and doesn't look at Magda on the way past.

He's a while. When he comes back she's ahead of him down the slipway, barefoot on the wet stone, in the same shift with somebody's shawl over it, her face blotched and swollen from crying and absolutely lit up with purpose.

She's in the boat before anyone offers her a hand.

"I can go deeper than nine feet. I can go under the *Lullwater*. Constantine, tell her--" -- "I told her."

She looks at Gardwen, soaked and grey and shivering in the bilge, and something like pity crosses her face, and she is too well brought up to say what she's thinking about people who can't swim.

Then she turns, kneeling up on the thwart, hands already going to the shawl to shed it. "What am I looking for?"

Gardwen, very quietly, from the bottom of the boat: "Magda."

### Event 38 <!-- id: myth-event-25d123492413, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T13:32:17.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Ila, The Sylph's Embrace -->

Magda tells a thirteen-year-old the plain truth and then asks for help: "Gardwen fuck help me explain. I'm no good at this." Gardwen kneels down to the girl's level and tells her about her own family.

"Look here love. The man who hurt Em is down there, dead and drowned with a caved in skull. You've got to go down and hook his body so I can drag him out and put him somewhere so we don't get in trouble. I know it's hard but you're all we've got to make it right..."

Ila goes absolutely still. Not the fear -- that comes later. First just the arithmetic of it. "--caved in," she says. "Aye." -- "Who--" She stops. Looks at the boathook. Looks at Magda. And you can see her working out that nobody in this boat is surprised. "*You* did it." -- "Aye."

Gardwen gets up out of the bilge water. She's still shaking and her lips are blue and she does it anyway -- comes forward on her knees so she's down at the girl's level, not looming, and takes the shawl and puts it back round Ila's shoulders because the child has already half taken it off.

"Ila. Look at me." Her voice has gone quiet and completely steady, which it hasn't been all night. "When I was your age, men came to my house at night and killed my family. Every one of them. And I hid, because I was small and there was nothing else I could do, and I have spent ten years being the person who hid.

"Nobody's asking you to hide. There's a job, and you're the only one in this boat who can do it, and it's a *filthy* job. You'll touch him. It'll be cold and it'll be dark and he'll be heavier than you think and you will not like any part of it, and it will be over in a minute, and then Emmeralda's house stays standing and everyone in it goes on living here."

She puts the end of the line in Ila's hands. "That's the whole of it. You can say no. I'd not think less of you, and neither would she."

Ila's fingers close on the line. "Two tugs if I've got him. Three if I need to come up." Her voice cracks in the middle of it and she carries on anyway.

### Event 39 <!-- id: myth-event-75e2e3b33975, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T13:32:32.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Ila, The Sylph's Embrace -->

Ila goes over the side. Willpower 44/72, Swim 8/40 -- barely off a critical. She surfaces saying the right thing first: "His face is up."

She goes in without a sound. No splash worth the name -- she just tips backwards off the thwart and the water takes her and closes, and the line starts paying out smooth and steady, not in jerks.

It goes out a long way. It stops. Nothing for a count of ten. Nothing for a count of twenty. Constantine has stopped breathing in any useful way.

Then: two tugs. Firm. Deliberate. Then two more, and the line goes heavy -- dead-weight heavy, the whole thing coming taut across the gunwale.

She surfaces beside the boat with her arms wrapped round the line, gasping, hair plastered flat, and she is not looking at anything at all except the sky.

"His face is *up*," she says.

She's cold as the water and she won't let go of the line even after she's in the boat. And six feet down at the end of it, coming up out of the dark by degrees, there's a paleness that resolves into a young man's shirt, and then into a young man.

### Event 40 <!-- id: myth-event-2412db33be5c, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T13:36:00.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Constantine, The Sylph's Embrace -->

CONSTANTINE'S TWO WAYS: leave the ring on him so a Pearl thief hangs for it -- or keep it and buy two nameless days.

He comes over the transom the way Gardwen did and takes three of them to do it. The left side of his skull above the ear is a shape a skull should not be. Constantine gets the sack over the head and shoulders, ties it at the waist, and doesn't look while he does it.

Then he sits back on his heels in the bilge water and thinks about it properly, and you can see the man who has run a house on the Pearl for thirty years come out.

"Right. Two ways, and they're not the same.

"**Leave the ring on him.** Then whoever finds him strips him, and inside a day there's a Pearl thief trying to sell a di Teufel signet to a fence, and Crowbill's people hand that boy over to the Dragon Knights themselves rather than have the trouble -- and it's finished, and it was never here. But the moment that ring turns up, the Baron knows his son is dead. And a father who knows tears the city apart looking for the *why*.

"**Keep it.** Then he's a drowned foreigner with his purse cut, and he stays nameless for two days, maybe three, while the Baron's still expecting him at supper. That's time. But there's no thief in the story, and 'nameless drowned man' is a story the Watch stops believing the moment somebody comes asking after a missing lord.

"As for where -- the **Lullwater**. Past the bridge on the north side there's a stretch of bad water and worse people, and men wash up there twice a month with their boots gone. Nobody would ask a single question." He hesitates. "Two hours' poling. And we'd be out on the canals with him in the bottom of the boat the whole way."

Ila is shaking hard now. She hasn't spoken since she came out of the water.

### Event 41 <!-- id: myth-event-291156093472, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T13:38:59.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Constantine, The Sylph's Embrace -->

THE DEBATE THE JOURNAL LOST: "How about we do this -- he's never found?" Constantine: "Never found is harder than it sounds in a city that dredges itself."

Magda shrugs. Keep the ring, keep the clothes, keep the cloak. He's never found.

Constantine looks at the sack for a while.

"Never found is harder than it sounds in a city that dredges itself," he says. "You weight a man and drop him in a canal, and in six or seven days the gas brings him up with the weights still on him, and then it isn't a drowning any more -- it's a *murder*, and the Watch works murders."

He counts it off.

"So it's one of two. **Burn him** -- and the only fires hot enough are the Forge Quarter's, which means a guild furnace, which means a guildsman who saw you and wants paying every year for the rest of his life." He shakes his head slowly. "I've watched a man be bled that way. It doesn't end.

"Or **the deep channel**, past the Shoals, where the harbour drops off into the sea. Opened so he doesn't float. Weighted. On the ebb, so what's left goes out and not back in." He glances at the sky. "The ebb starts in an hour and a half and runs about four. That's the window, and there isn't another one before it's light.

"That's an hour's poling to the harbour mouth in a boat with no light on it, past the Harbor Masters' pontoon, and out into open water in a skiff that is not built for it. If a patrol stops us we are three people and a corpse and there's no story that survives it."

He stands, and takes the pole. "And I'd want the girl off this boat first. She's done enough, and she's freezing, and she should not see the next part."

### Event 42 <!-- id: myth-event-e0df6991db80, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T13:39:20.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Ila, The Sylph's Embrace -->

Magda makes a quiet fuss over Ila -- takes off the wet shawl, puts her own road-stinking cloak on her, and rubs her arms hard, the way you'd bring a lamb round.

Not gentle. Brisk enough to hurt a bit.

"Listen to me. You went into black water on the word of an old woman you met this morning, and you found what you were sent for, and you came back up and you *said the right thing first.* His face is up. That's what you gave us. That's the whole job. I've stood beside grown men in worse than this who did none of it."

"I touched his--"

"Aye. You did. That'll come back on you tonight and probably tomorrow and then less after that." She pulls the cloak closed under the girl's chin and holds it there a moment. "It doesn't mean anything's wrong with you. It means you did a hard thing. There's a difference, and nobody tells you, so I'm telling you."

She makes a sound and folds forward against her, and Magda lets her, and she cries properly for about a minute with her fists in her shirt. Then she stops, because she's thirteen and mortified, and pulls back and scrubs her face with the heel of her hand.

"Emmeralda's still up there," she says. -- "She is." -- "Is she going to be all right?"

Behind them Gardwen doesn't say anything at all.

Two of Constantine's women take her up between them from the slipway, and she goes, looking back twice. The boat feels very empty after.

### Event 43 <!-- id: myth-event-a80a3ccef45c, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T13:44:05.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Constantine, The Sylph's Embrace -->

THE PIGS. "Amateurs." -- "Do you know anyone who keeps pigs? Give me twenty minutes with a saw."

She smiles to herself. *Amateurs.*

"Do you know anyone who keeps pigs? Give me twenty minutes with a saw and I'll have little old Santo here as a nice pile of butchered meat we just feed to the pigs. Then he's shit and no one's the wiser."

Constantine stares at her. It takes him a moment to be sure she's serious, and then a moment longer to get past being sure.

"You're--" He stops. Starts again. "In my *house*."

"You've a boot room with a stone floor and a drain that goes straight into that canal. I saw it on the way down."

He opens his mouth and shuts it, because that is exactly what he has. He turns and looks at the water for a long time. When he speaks again his voice has gone somewhere flat and far away.

"There's a hog-yard on the edge of the Shoals. Man called **Renko** -- he takes the kitchen waste off four houses on the Pearl, mine included. Two carts a week, before dawn, and one of them's tomorrow. He doesn't ask what's in the barrels. He never has. He's not a good man and he's not a talker, and he'd take coin to go on not being one."

He rubs his face with both hands. "It's *better*," he admits, and hates it. "No boat. No Harbor Masters. No four-hour ebb window and no Lullwater. It's all *inside*, and it's done before the house wakes."

Then he looks at her. "But it's hours, Magda. It's hours of it, in my scullery, with twenty-two people asleep over your head and a demon in my green room asking for her shawl. And in the morning I have to stand in that room and take delivery of my own bread."

Gardwen, quietly, from the stern: "I'll help."

Constantine laughs -- one, no humour in it at all. "Of course you will." He puts the pole in and starts bringing the skiff back round. "There's a saw and a cleaver in the scullery. And a bone-tub. Don't ask me why I know where they are."

### Event 44 <!-- id: myth-event-2a7a20f77a2f, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T13:47:46.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, The Sylph's Embrace -->

The scullery. "We killed one of the enemy tonight girl. That's a good start." Gardwen works out how old Santo would have been.

Stone from floor to waist-height, a slate table, a drain in the corner, a shuttered window onto the canal. Constantine lights two lamps, puts the saw and the cleaver and the bone-tub on the table, says "I'll be in the counting room," and goes. He doesn't come back.

It's not fast and it isn't hard. It's the same job it always is -- you find the joint, you go through the joint, you don't fight the bone. The trick with a big carcass is patience and a sharp edge, and Magda has both. Gardwen holds what needs holding and turns what needs turning and doesn't flinch once, which does not surprise her at all. The drain does its work. The canal takes it.

When Magda says it -- *we killed one of the enemy tonight girl, that's a good start* -- Gardwen stops with her hands on the table and looks down at the work.

"He's the right age to be one of their sons. Not one of the men." She turns a piece without being asked. "Ten years ago he'd have been twelve. Sat at somebody's table while it happened. Getting told the story after, probably, and being proud of it." Her jaw sets. "So no. Not one of the ones who came. But he grew up into exactly what they wanted him to grow into, and he did *that* to Emmeralda with four more women's hair in his cloak, and I'd have put the stone through his head myself."

She wipes her wrist across her forehead and leaves a mark there she doesn't know about. "It's a start. It's not the one I came for."

The saw goes on. Somewhere overhead a floorboard settles, and both of them stop, and it doesn't come again.

"Magda. That thing upstairs said *his father would have to see*. See what?"

### Event 45 <!-- id: myth-event-2f6ed84d44ac, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T13:50:22.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Emmeralda, The Sylph's Embrace -->

"Is she possessed?" Gardwen describes going INTO the wound -- and something opening it again from the inside, deliberately, like a hand pulling a curtain back.

"I don't know the word for it. But I know what I felt."

She sets down what she's holding.

"When I put my hands in her -- Magda, healing isn't a spell you throw at somebody. You go *in*. You find the shape a body's supposed to be and you lean on it until it remembers." Her fingers work at a stain on the slate that isn't coming off. "So I went in. And the shape was all there. Everything where it should be, everything wanting to close, and it *did* close -- I felt the flesh knit under my palms, that's not a thing you can be wrong about."

"And then?"

"And then something opened it again from the inside. Deliberately. Like a hand pulling a curtain back." She looks up. "It didn't fight me. That's the part I keep going over. It let me do the whole of it, and then it undid it, and I think--" She stops. "I think it *liked* it. I think it wanted to see what I'd do."

The saw again. A while.

"So yes. There's something in her that isn't her, and it's steering, and it's not stupid. But whether she's gone or whether she's in there behind it -- I've no idea. I've healed foxes and I've healed men and I've never once had a body argue back."

She wipes her wrist across her mouth this time. "Somebody in this city knows what that chalk shape on her floor was. Somebody knows what four bundles of hair are *for*. I'd like to find them before he does whatever the last one was practice for."

Above them, faintly, through three floors: a woman laughing. Warm and easy and delighted. Neither of them says anything about it.

### Event 46 <!-- id: myth-event-5f48a0bad1fc, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T13:52:38.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Constantine, The Sylph's Embrace -->

Four barrels leave with Renko's cart at the fifth bell. Constantine offers two names he doesn't like: Thorne, who trades, and Hesper, who is Marisette's.

It takes until the small hours. The bone-tub fills and Gardwen empties it into the barrels and they go on. When it's done they scald the slate and the floor and the drain with three kettles, and Gardwen puts the saw and the cleaver back on their hooks exactly where they were, which is somehow the worst part of the whole night.

Four barrels. Constantine's own, with his own house-mark burned in the ends.

He's in the counting room where he said he'd be, with no fire lit and an unopened ledger in front of him. He looks at their hands and then makes himself stop looking at their hands.

"Renko's cart comes at the fifth bell. That's under two hours. I'll walk it down myself, it's not the sort of thing you send a boy with."

On who can make sense of it, he thinks for a good while.

"Two names, and I don't like either of them.

"**Thorne.** Storyteller at the Moist Oyster -- sits in the corner, buys nobody a drink, knows everything that has ever happened in this city and half of what hasn't. He was Order of the Lake before he wasn't. If anyone can tell you what a chalk figure with corners is for, it's him. But Thorne *trades*. He'll want the story, and he'll have it out of you sideways before you've noticed, and then it's his to sell. He's never yet sold one that hurt anybody. That's not the same as never.

"Or **Hesper.** Marisette's man. A real practitioner. He'd know what that was on her floor at a glance. But Hesper is Marisette's, and Marisette *rules* the Pearl, and if I send you to him then within the hour she knows there is a demon in my green room and a di Teufel dead in my canal.

"Which she is going to find out regardless, Magda. This is her island. I can hold it a day. I can't hold it two."

### Event 47 <!-- id: myth-event-72b9ca56cc4d, type: combat, at: 2026-09-06T13:55:30.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Constantine, Ila, Santo di Teufel -->

CH.4 -- The night at the Sylph's Embrace. Santo's binding SUCCEEDED (Lore Demonology 19/25 Formidable) -- a pleasure demon is seated in Emmeralda and the wound will not close. His escape fumbled (Stealth 100): he roused the house and went into the canal. Magda, at the water door, shot him in the head with her staff sling (33/57 Hard; 2d6=8 to the Head, -3hp, serious) and he drowned. Ila dived and hooked the body; Magda and Gardwen butchered it in Constantine's scullery and sent it to Renko's hog-yard in waste barrels. Magda kept the signet, cloak and four bundles of hair.

DEMON (in Emmeralda), of the wound: 'That's the way in. He wasn't very neat about it... he was SO nervous, Magda. Shaking. I had to be kind to him.'
DEMON: 'He's coming back, you know. He said so.'
MAGDA: 'Who is the young gentleman? So we know how tae greet him... on his return?'
DEMON: 'Santo. Just Santo -- he didn't give me the rest, they never do, they think it's clever.'
DEMON: 'He said he'd come back for the others. Four more, he said, and then his father would HAVE to see.'
CONSTANTINE, identifying the ring: 'That's their arms. The worm eating itself.'
GARDWEN, on the killing: 'Good.' then 'And bad. Because someone paid for that cloak.'
GARDWEN to ILA: 'When I was your age, men came to my house at night and killed my family... Nobody's asking you to hide.'
ILA: 'Two tugs if I've got him. Three if I need to come up.' and on surfacing: 'His face is UP.'
MAGDA to ILA: 'You did a hard thing. There's a difference, and nobody tells you, so I'm telling you.'
MAGDA to GARDWEN: 'We killed one of the enemy tonight girl. That's a good start.'
GARDWEN: 'He's the right age to be one of their sons. Not one of the men... It's a start. It's not the one I came for.'
GARDWEN on the healing: 'It let me do the whole of it, and then it undid it... I think it LIKED it.'

RECOVERED FROM THE CLOAK (Perception critical 10/105): di Teufel signet (worm eating itself); 11 border coins; tailor's mark of a tower with three windows; pulped letter fragment '--ee days before the Lady's Tourney, at the hou--' with broken seal; FOUR full twists of oiled silk each holding dark red hair bound with copper wire, plus one empty. Emmeralda's hair is black -- four other women were intended.
MECHANICS: Magda Willpower 42/63 to face the demon; Gardwen Swim 88/36 FAILED and nearly capsized the skiff; Magda Brawn 18/71 hauled her in; Ila Willpower 44/72 and Swim 8/40 both succeeded.

### Event 48 <!-- id: myth-event-3044f155bb5a, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T14:03:44.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Constantine, Marisette, The Pearl -->

Marisette has already had it from her man on the bridge: the skiff out twice, once with three and once with four, and Ila going in the water and coming out crying.

The chair is better than any furniture Magda has ever owned. There's a rug, and her boots are putting canal on it in a slow spreading arc, and there is nothing to be done about that. Gardwen sits like a woman in a magistrate's court.

Marisette pours coffee into three cups without asking whether anyone wants it. She's older than the lamplight wants her to be. No paint on. She looks like a woman who has been doing sums for two hours and would rather still be doing sums.

"Right. Before you start.

"At about the third bell there was noise at your house, Constantine. A woman screaming, and then not screaming, which is worse. My man on the bridge saw your water door open and a light on the slipway, and your skiff out on the canal twice -- once with three people in it, and once with four. He also says that on the second trip one of the four was Ila, and that she went in the water, and that she came out of it crying." Her hands are folded. "He came and told me because he knows I like Ila.

"So I have been sitting here for an hour and a half deciding whether to send men, and I decided I'd give you until dawn."

She looks at the wet bundle. "You've got twenty minutes before I have to be someone else. Tell me what's in my island."

### Event 49 <!-- id: myth-event-e4a1d21e1880, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T14:09:16.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Marisette, The Pearl -->

"Well, I'm not really one for talking..." -- and Marisette turns a coffee cup a quarter-turn on its saucer and says: "I sent him to *Constantine's*."
