Marisette does not move at all while Magda talks -- the carving, the demon, the sling, the pigs, and then the name.

When she stops, Marisette reaches out and turns one of the coffee cups a quarter-turn on its saucer, and that's the only thing that happens for about five seconds.

"Say the last part again." -- "Hanzo di Teufel's son."

"Santo." She says it flatly, like checking a number. "Twenty-two, black hair, very handsome, laughs before the end of the joke." -- "That's him."

"He came to my house three days ago asking after Asphodel and I turned him away because I didn't like his hands." She is completely still. "I sent him to *Constantine's*."

Constantine says, "Marisette--" -- "Don't." Not sharp. Just closed.

She goes through the evidence like a woman counting stock. The ring she barely glances at. The letter she reads twice. The coins she pushes aside.

Then she comes to the four twists of oiled silk, and opens the first with her thumbnail, and the copper wire and the long dark red hairs come out into the lamplight. And Marisette stops.

"Constantine. Who at your house has red hair?" -- "Nobody." -- "No. Nobody at mine either."

She lays the hairs flat on the desk and separates them with one fingernail, four or five strands, precisely, the way you'd lay out evidence.

"There are two women on this island with hair that colour, and I know them both, and neither of them works. One of them is a Watercrafter's daughter. The other is the High Priestess of the Lake Lady's own niece."

### Event 50 <!-- id: myth-event-6bdadf34dfc8, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T14:11:54.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Marisette, The Pearl -->

Magda nods Gardwen into telling her tale. Marisette hears "guards facing in" and stops: "you don't face inward to keep people out."

Gardwen puts her cup down and doesn't touch it again, and tells it the way she'd give a report -- no shape to it, nothing arranged, which is somehow the worst way to hear it.

"Ten years ago men in dragon-scale came to my family's steading at night. There were nine of us. I was eleven. They fired the house and they killed everyone in it and I lived because I was small and the woods took me. In the morning I went back and I counted them. I counted them all. My brother wasn't there.

"He was seven. They'd taken him alive, and they'd taken *only* him, out of nine people, and they burned the rest so nobody would count."

She finally looks up. "That boy in the canal said his father would *have to see*. See what he'd made. That means his father makes things too. And his father has had my brother for ten years."

Marisette sits back. "A litter. Curtains sewn shut. Guards facing inward." -- "Aye."

"He entered a champion for the Single Combat. Nobody has seen the man. It's been the joke of the week -- the Baron's mystery swordsman. Not one soul in this city has laid eyes on him."

She picks the ring up, finally, and turns it over. "But a champion nobody has seen is not a champion. It's a *thing being kept*. Men who are proud of a swordsman show him off -- that is the entire point of entering one. Hanzo di Teufel has not let a living soul look at his. He has him behind a wall and a litter with the curtains stitched, and his guards face inward -- **you don't face inward to keep people out, you face inward to keep something in.**"

"How old would your brother be now?"

### Event 51 <!-- id: myth-event-293806146dd6, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T14:13:36.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Marisette, The Pearl -->

"Seventeen. Santo was twenty-two. It's not him." -- "No. It isn't." Marisette lets it sit exactly long enough.

It comes out of Gardwen flat and immediate, the way a number does when you have counted it every year for ten years.

"He'd be seventeen. Santo was twenty-two." Her hands have gone white on her knees. "It's not him."

"No," Marisette agrees. "It isn't." She lets that sit exactly long enough.

"Seventeen is young for the Single Combat. It is not too young. I have seen younger die in it."

The clock behind them goes on being expensive.

"I'm not telling you it's your brother, girl. I've no idea and neither have you and I'd not have you break yourself on a hope." She says it almost gently, which from her seems to cost something. "I'm telling you that the thing in that estate is being hidden, and the boy who came out of it was carving women up to impress his father, and that his father is at this moment three streets from the Governor with a demon-worker's blood in him and an entry in the Lady's own Tourney."

She stands. "Which makes it my island's business now, and I don't have twenty minutes any more."

### Event 52 <!-- id: myth-event-73f6fbad4ab4, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T14:17:52.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Marisette, The Pearl -->

"Who are you?" -- "Magda. I keep pigs." Marisette gives them the job nobody else in Purewater can do: nobody knows their names.

"You killed the Baron's heir with a sling from a doorway in the dark, at what -- thirty feet? -- and then took him apart in a scullery and had him in barrels before the fifth bell." A beat. "You keep pigs."

"Aye. And other things."

She accepts that. She writes one line in the ledger and blots it.

"Right. Three things, and then you're going to sleep, because you're no use to me grey.

"**Emmeralda.** I'll have Hesper at Constantine's within the hour. He'll come quietly and he'll come as a physician, because I'm not having the word *demon* on this island before I've decided who gets to say it.

"**The two women.** That's mine as well. I can get word to both without explaining why, and I will, this morning. Whoever they were for, they're not getting them now.

"**And the Baron.**" She comes round the desk. Up close she is not a large woman at all.

"You asked if there's fuckery to be had. There is, and I can't do it. Every face I own is known on this island -- my people can't get within a street of that estate without it being reported to somebody by noon. **You two walked through the water-gate yesterday morning. Nobody in this city knows your names. That is worth more than anything else you've brought me tonight.**

"So: sleep. Eat. And this afternoon I want to know what is behind that wall. Not a rescue. Not a fight. *Looking.* You come back and tell me, and then we decide."

Then, without looking up: "And when you find out whether that boy in the litter is seventeen, Gardwen -- you come to me before you do anything about it. Not after."

### Event 53 <!-- id: myth-event-7e615073890e, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T15:05:35.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, The DragonBarge, The Forge Quarter -->

An hour in the lee of a cooperage watching what a ship actually needs. Perception 49/70 -- nothing goes ON. Things come off.

You can see it from the second bridge: above the roofs of the Forge Quarter a column of dark smoke from the furnaces, and standing separate from it, lower down at the waterline, a long flat streak of white. Steam. It comes off the water in a continuous sheet for a hundred feet along a private pier, and it does not stop, and there is no fire under it.

Where the hull meets the water, the water is boiling. Gulls won't go near it. There's a dead-water slick round the stern with things floating in it belly-up.

Not much goes *on*. That's the first thing. No provisioning carts, no chandler's boy, no water butts -- until you notice the covered gangway and understand they're bringing everything aboard where nobody can see it, in their own hands, from their own boat.

But things do come **off**. **Slops** -- twice, a knight carries a covered pail down the ramp and empties it into the dead water at the stern. Nobody's collecting it. **Laundry** -- a woman came at the second bell with a barrow, took a bagged load from the *pier head*, handed down by a knight who didn't let her past the bollards, and went away. **Ash and clinker**, in barrows, from somewhere below decks, far more than a galley fire makes. And a **cooper's boy** delivered four barrels to the pier head, left them, and was waved off.

Nobody who is not a Dragon Knight has been past the bollards all afternoon.

### Event 54 <!-- id: myth-event-ab2f9d217e3c, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T15:08:12.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, The DragonBarge, The Forge Quarter -->

"How do they eat? Any food supplies?" -- the ship's boat, twice, from north-west round the point. Two boatloads, carried off the thwarts in a few armloads. Food for a handful.

Nothing goes aboard by cart. Not a sack, not a crate, not a water butt.

But there's a **ship's boat**. It came round the point from the north-west, four men rowing, and went in under the covered gangway aft where you can't see what happened. It left again riding higher than it arrived. Then it came back an hour later, the same way.

North-west round that point is High Isle proper -- the good streets, the leased houses, where a border baron with money would take a place for the tourney and stable his horses and put his knights.

So the ship isn't provisioning itself off this yard because it's being fed from a house. Their own boat, their own men, in under the canvas.

And here's the thing you can count. Two boatloads. Four men rowing each time, and what they carried came off the thwarts in a few armloads.

That is not victualling for twenty-odd Dragon Knights. That's food for a handful.

Whatever's on that ship, there aren't many of them aboard it.

### Event 55 <!-- id: myth-event-70d32926d5f5, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T15:09:47.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, The Forge Quarter -->

"Go over there and help. I know all about hogs." Survival 78/63 -- FAILURE. The horse is not impressed by her either, and the cart goes into the spoil heap.

She goes to its head talking the way you'd talk to any bad-tempered old nag, and it lays its ears flat and swings its quarters, and the offside wheel goes into the spoil heap and the cart tips enough to put one of the barrels over on its side. The lid stays on. Mostly.

The lad says a word she'd have clipped him for.

Then they both spend a couple of minutes getting the wheel out, and she gets her shoulder under the barrel while he rights it, and by the end of it they're both filthy and he's stopped being embarrassed.

"She does that," he says, glaring at the horse. "Every time. Every single time, right at the end." -- "She's testing whether you've noticed her." -- "I've *noticed* her--" -- "No, lad. She wants you to go to her head *before* she starts, not after."

He considers this with the deep suspicion of a fifteen-year-old being told something obviously true.

His name's **Pell**. He does the Forge Quarter yards on alternate days for Renko out on the Shoals -- which Magda keeps her face entirely still about.

A failed roll bought a friend. It just cost a barrel first.

### Event 56 <!-- id: myth-event-ccd5d709b8fc, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T15:11:19.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, The DragonBarge, The Forge Quarter -->

"Tight bunch, are they?" -- "They're *unnatural*, is what they are." Pell hands over the whole shape of it as a complaint about waste.

"Every yard on this side gives us their waste. Cooperage, rope-walk, chandler, even the furnace lads give us their bones. That's how it *works*. That lot tip it in the water. In the *water*, right off the stern. Good scraps, meat and all, I've watched them do it. Straight in."

He shakes his head at the criminality of it.

"Renko went down there himself first day. Offered to take it for nothing -- for *nothing*, mind, just to have it -- and they walked him back up the pier by his elbows and told him not to come again.

"And the ash. They've been carting ash off that ship since the day it tied up, barrows of it, and you show me the galley fire that makes that much clinker in a week. Furnace lads have been laughing about it. Reckon they're burning green timber down there.

"Only one that gets anything off them is the laundry woman. **Yannick's wife** -- does the rope-walk and the chandler and now them. Bag down at the bollards, coin in her hand, off you go. She's not allowed past neither. She won't talk about it, though. Went right off, she has. Won't say a word about that ship. Which is a shame, 'cause she'll talk about anything."

### Event 57 <!-- id: myth-event-59c5132a9bf6, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T15:13:20.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, The DragonBarge, The Forge Quarter -->

THE QUESTION THAT CRACKS IT: "How big is the bag? Is there a lot of laundry?" Pell has never thought about it before -- and thinks about it now.

"Not big," he says. "One bag. She carries it on the barrow but she could carry it on her hip if she wanted -- I've seen her lift it one-handed off the bollard."

He frowns.

"Every day, though. That's the thing, now you say it. Rope-walk's forty men and they send out twice a week. That ship sends out *every day* and it's one bag."

He clicks at the horse.

"And it's all the same, she said once before she went quiet on it. Not shirts and hose and what-have-you like a normal house. All the one thing. Sheets, I suppose. Bedding."

He's got the nag moving now, and he says the rest over his shoulder, cheerfully, with no idea what he's handing her:

"She had a right go at Renko about it in the tavern, first week. Said she'd take their coin but she'd not take it long, and if they wanted that much *boiling* every day they could get a woman with a stronger stomach."

"Alternate days, missus! Tell Renko I said you know your horses!"

### Event 58 <!-- id: myth-event-5aba4517a9ca, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T15:15:37.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, The DragonBarge -->

Counting properly. Perception 70 against 70 -- exactly on the number. EIGHT. No banner. No Baron. And the shutters amidships are barred from OUTSIDE.

**Eight.** That's all of them. Two at the pier head. Two at the top of the ramp. Three working on deck -- coiling, carrying, one on the quarterdeck who doesn't do anything but walk the same twenty feet over and over and look at the shore. And one who came up out of the aft hatch with a barrow of clinker, went down the ramp, tipped it on the spoil heap, and went back.

Eight men. In scale, all of them, on a hot afternoon, aboard a moored ship. Nobody in shirtsleeves. Nobody idle. Nobody talking.

**No banner at the masthead.** For a nobleman in residence there'd be colours flying. That mast is bare.

**No Baron.** No servants, no clerk, no valet, nothing that looks like a household. The good clothes and the gold are somewhere else -- north-west round that point, where the boat goes.

And the shutters amidships have not opened once all afternoon. It has been a warm bright day, every port on every hull on this water is open to air the ship out, and those are closed and -- now she looks properly -- they're closed from the *outside*. There's a bar across.

Gardwen has gone very quiet. "Magda," she says. "Sheets. Every day. Boiled."

### Event 59 <!-- id: myth-event-a4a4e773d953, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T15:21:44.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, The Forge Quarter -->

The wash-house. Magda sits like an old woman sits and asks nothing at all. Ann Yannick's Willpower 66/55 -- she fails to keep it in.

She comes in out of the sun, takes the bench by the door where the draught is, and sits down the way a woman of seventy sits down -- all at once, with a noise. Then she doesn't say anything. She looks at her boots. She rubs her knee. The steam is actually pleasant on the joints and she says so, once, to nobody.

Gardwen, who has understood, stays outside in the yard with her back to the wall.

The paddle goes on. Copper, lift, turn, copper. Ann Yannick has been holding this for four days with a husband who doesn't want to hear it and no one else to tell.

It takes a long while. Magda gets most of the way through not-asking before it happens.

She lifts a load, drops it, wipes her forehead with the back of her wrist, and says -- to the copper, not to her:

"Do you know what boiled blood smells like?"

Magda says nothing.

"'Course you do. You've a face like you've buried people. Four days I've had that. Every morning. One bag, and it's all sheets, and every one of them's the same.

"It's not a wound. I've done a house with a consumptive in it, I've done a birth-bed, I've done a man who lost a hand at the rope-walk. You get a shape to it -- where it came from, how it lay, whether he thrashed. This is the *same shape* every day. Same place on the sheet. Same size. Like it's been let out of him careful, in the same spot, and mopped, and put back.

"And there's something else in it that doesn't come out. Not blood. It goes *grey*, and it smells like the forge, and my hands were numb to the elbow the first day and I've worn gloves since.

"They pay double. My man says take it. And I lie there at night thinking -- whoever's in that ship, he's still bleeding tomorrow. Four days. Somebody's keeping him alive to do it again."

### Event 60 <!-- id: myth-event-49cc3aac39b1, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T15:24:52.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, The Forge Quarter -->

THE JOKE THAT DIES. Influence, Easy 54 against 27 -- no. "It lands in the steam and dies there." And Ann Yannick's face closes like a door.

Magda arranges her face into something sympathetic. She's seen other people do it. It is not a thing her face knows how to be, and what it produces is closer to indigestion.

And the joke -- *at least it's honest work, most folk pay double to keep blood off their sheets, not on* -- is the sort of thing that's funny in a byre at four in the morning with people who've been up all night with a bad calving.

It is not funny in a wash-house. It lands in the steam and dies there.

Yannick's wife's face closes like a door. She picks the paddle back up. The rhythm changes -- shorter, harder, the movements of a woman who has remembered she's working.

"You're not from the yards," she says. Not hostile. Worse: careful.

"You came in with a young one who's stood outside my door instead of coming in, and you sat down and let me talk, and you've asked me nothing, which is how the Watch does it and how Crowbill's do it and how a certain sort of woman from the Pearl does it." She doesn't look up. "And now you've made a joke about blood.

"So I'll ask, since you won't. Who are you, and why do you care what's in my coppers?"

### Event 61 <!-- id: myth-event-ce5909d50d29, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T15:32:27.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, The DragonBarge, The Forge Quarter -->

Not a retry -- a different move. Magda tells her the plain truth. Ann Yannick's Insight 44 against 45. By ONE she reads her as honest, and gives up three things.

"No. Not from here. I'm from the north, where the winds are cold and the ice is sharp. I joke about blood because I've seen and cleaned up my fair share and I'm interested because the men on that boat are up to some evil shit that might need stopping. Sounds like you're thinking the same way... But other than that, I'm just a tired old woman stretching my limbs here."

She looks at her a long moment through the steam. Whatever she's checking for -- and she's a woman who has been lied to by better liars -- she doesn't find it.

"North," she says. "Aye. I thought there was something in the vowels."

She works for a bit. "Evil shit that might need stopping," she repeats, and something that isn't quite a laugh comes out of her. "My man says I'm being a woman about it."

"Three things, then. And I'll deny every one of them if you ever say I said it.

"**One.** It's not a sick lad. Sick men sweat, and they piss the bed, and they stink of the sickness. There's none of that. Whoever's in that bed isn't ill and isn't dying and isn't getting better. He's being kept.

"**Two.** Second morning, the bag came down heavier and there was a shirt in it. Not linen -- a fighting man's under-shirt, quilted, for wearing under scale. The arming-points were *cut*. Not untied. Cut, all of them, front and back, the way you do when you're getting something off a man who isn't helping you.

"**Three.** The knight who hands the bag down. Big fellow, white eyebrows, no colour in him at all -- he's the one that walked Renko off the pier. Yesterday he asks me, all friendly, whether I ever wash for the houses on the Pearl. Whether I know anyone that does."

She looks straight at her. "I said no. But he wasn't asking about laundry, was he."

### Event 62 <!-- id: myth-event-49980921e38c, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T15:35:00.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Marisette, The Forge Quarter -->

"You know Marisette? What do you think of her?" -- "She's a hard woman who looks after women. There's worse to be."

She snorts. "Everyone knows Marisette. She's not on this side of the water but she doesn't need to be."

She turns a load. "What do I think of her." She considers it properly, which Magda is beginning to realise is how she does everything. "She's not *kind*. Don't let anyone tell you she is. She'll take a girl's whole life apart to keep her house standing and she'll do the sums out loud while she does it.

"But she's straight. That's rarer. She says a price and it's the price. And when Ganny Corliss's daughter got herself in trouble with a Watercrafter's son two winters back -- a Forge Quarter girl, mind, nothing of hers, no reason at all -- Marisette had the Guild sat down and settled inside a fortnight, and the girl kept the child and the money, and the boy's father still can't say her name without going purple."

She wrings out the sheet. "So. She's a hard woman who looks after women. There's worse to be."

Then she stops, and her hands go still in the water, and you can see her arriving at it. "You *are* off the Pearl." She looks at the door where Gardwen is standing in the yard. "And that white-haired bastard was asking me yesterday about women who do laundry for the Pearl houses. He wasn't hunting a washerwoman. He's hunting something that went *from* here *to* there. Or from there to here."

She pulls her hands out of the copper and dries them on her apron, and she isn't calm any more. "What did you people *do?*"

### Event 63 <!-- id: myth-event-87ee441af3c2, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T15:45:45.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, The DragonBarge, The Forge Quarter -->

"At some point, you have to choose sides. Which part of the boy are they bleeding?" She shakes a clean sheet out flat across the bench and puts her hand on it.

"I got here yesterday -- my loyalty's to Hel and that girl standing outside and no one else. These men are stone killers. The albino worse than most... so be careful what you tell that one. At some point, you have to choose sides."

She's quiet for a moment. Then she does what she does with everything: she thinks about it properly.

"I can tell you that. I've been reading it for four days."

She takes a clean sheet off the folded stack and shakes it out flat across the bench and puts her hand down on it. "Sheet goes on the bed this way -- hem to the head, you can tell from the wear where a man's heels go. So." She lays her palm high on it, a little left of the middle. "It's *here*. Every morning, four days. About the size of my two hands together."

She spreads her fingers. "Upper body. Left side. Chest, or just under the arm -- high enough that if he was on his back it ran sideways, because the edge of it's always feathered toward the outside. Same spot, same size, and it's *sharp*-edged, not spread. That's not a wound that's healing and breaking open. A healing wound weeps different every day, wider then narrower, and the shape wanders. This doesn't wander.

"Somebody opens the same place, the same way, every morning. And then somebody else changes the bed.

"And the grey. That's not in the blood. It's *round* it -- you get the red out and the grey stays behind in a ring, like a scorch. That's what numbs your hands."

Then she squares the sheet off on the stack and stands there with both hands on it. "You said choose sides."

She goes to the end copper and lifts the lid and starts fishing the load out. "They're due at the sixth bell for that. They'll get it. I'll smile at the white-haired bastard and take his coin.

"But you tell Marisette that Ann Yannick washes for that ship every morning, and she'll go on doing it, and she'll go on *looking*. And if that boy's still bleeding on the day of the Tourney, I want somebody to have done something about it."

### Event 64 <!-- id: myth-event-fa4361781c2a, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T15:49:51.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda -->

CH.5 -- Reconnaissance at the DragonBarge. Magda and Gardwen scout the Baron's steaming black ship at High Isle's seaward pier. Count: EIGHT Dragon Knights aboard, no banner, no Baron, no household -- he is lodged ashore NW round the point and the ship is fed twice daily by its own boat in armloads, food for a handful. One shuttered port amidships, barred from OUTSIDE. Nothing is collected from the ship; slops go in the water and barrows of clinker come off daily. Magda befriends Pell (Renko's waste lad), then gets Ann Yannick, the ship's laundress, to talk.

PELL: 'They tip it in the water. Good scraps, meat and all... Renko offered to take it for nothing and they walked him back up the pier by his elbows.' On the laundry: 'One bag. Every day. And it's all the one thing.'
ANN YANNICK: 'Do you know what boiled blood smells like?'
ANN: 'It's the same shape every day. Same place on the sheet. Same size. Like it's been let out of him careful, in the same spot, and mopped, and put back.'
ANN: 'It goes grey, and it smells like the forge, and my hands were numb to the elbow.'
ANN: 'Second morning there was a fighting man's quilted under-shirt in it. The arming-points were CUT, front and back.'
ANN: 'The white-haired one asked me yesterday whether I ever wash for the houses on the Pearl.' (= Blau hunting the Pearl connection)
ANN, locating the wound on a sheet: 'Upper body. Left side. Chest, or just under the arm... Somebody opens the same place, the same way, every morning.'
MAGDA: 'These men are stone killers. The albino worse than most... At some point, you have to choose sides.'
ANN: 'You tell Marisette that Ann Yannick washes for that ship every morning, and she'll go on LOOKING. And if that boy's still bleeding on the day of the Tourney, I want somebody to have done something about it.'
MECHANICS: Magda Perception 49/70 (pier traffic) and 70/70 (count of eight); Survival 78/63 FAILED with the cart-horse (which opened the conversation with Pell anyway); Influence 54/27 FAILED -- the sympathetic face and the blood joke misfired and Ann went wary; Ann Yannick Willpower 66/55 failed to keep it in; Ann Insight 44/45 read Magda as sincere by one point.
OFFSCREEN: Blau rolled Streetwise 82/66 and FAILED to trace Santo on the Pearl today; di Teufel servants worked the island house to house. New agenda for Blau: 'Find the young master'.

### Event 65 <!-- id: myth-event-dc22f46df7da, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T15:50:06.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, The Forge Quarter -->

Magda grips her shoulder woman to woman, warrior to warrior. Ann Yannick puts her wet hand over it and holds it there, and neither of them says anything.

Not a pat. She takes the weight of it, once, the way you'd grip a man's arm before he goes over a wall -- and Ann Yannick reaches up and puts her wet hand over hers and holds it there a moment, and neither of them says anything, because there's nothing that would improve it.

Then Magda creaks upright, and her knee makes its opinion known, and she goes out into the yard where Gardwen is waiting.

### Event 66 <!-- id: myth-event-b09ab0ca4ed9, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T15:51:00.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Marisette, The Pearl -->

Marisette hears the whole recon without interrupting once. At the end she says "Eight," and then "*Eight*," differently.

Eight men, no banner, no Baron, the boat from the north-west, the barred shutter, the barrows of clinker, the one bag a day, the same place on the sheet, the cut arming-points, and Ann Yannick's name and Ann Yannick's message.

At the end she says, "Eight," and then, "*Eight*," differently.

And then: "Ann Yannick will not lose that work, and she will not be alone in that wash-house again after tomorrow. I'll see to it."

### Event 67 <!-- id: myth-event-9f0be6455cda, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T15:53:20.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Hesper, Marisette, The Pearl -->

THE TIMELINE. The ship came five days ago; the laundry started four days ago; the Baron was still three days up the road. "Nobody started anything here. That is a *routine*."

"No," Marisette says. "The Baron rode in through Caravan Square yesterday at midday. I had it from three people within the hour. But the *ship* came before him. It came by sea, round the Mouth, and tied up at that pier five days ago. I know because the Harbor Masters made a noise about the berth and were made to stop making a noise about it."

"Five days," Hesper says. "And the laundress has been washing that bed for four."

He sits forward. "Then it started the day after the ship tied up, and the Baron was still three days up the road when it did. Do you see what that means? Nobody started anything here. That is a *routine*. That is a thing that has been done to somebody every morning for however long, in whatever house it was done in before, and they carried on doing it at sea, and they carried on doing it here."

He picks the cup up, finds it cold, and puts it down again.

"And they brought him by *water*. Twenty-odd knights ride three days up the road in the open, and the thing in the litter goes round by sea in a hull that steams. You don't do that for a champion. You do that because a road has villages on it, and villages have people in them, and whatever's in that cabin cannot be taken past people for three days."

Marisette says quietly: "Hesper." -- "I know." -- "Tell them what it said."

"The thing in Emmeralda spent the afternoon complaining. It's bored, it's fond of the girl's memories, and it will talk about anything to anyone who'll sit with it. And what it complained about, at some length, was that it had been promised something better than a courtesan.

"It said its master's father keeps a *proper* one. And that his is **'the old kind, the kind that remembers being a person.'**"

### Event 68 <!-- id: myth-event-2ae36c261001, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T15:56:13.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Hesper, The Pearl -->

Magda tells them Hel's dream, and Hesper seizes on one word. "You don't lay a demon to rest. You lay a *dead man* to rest."

She tells them. The eleven nights, the woman with the wolf, the burning house, and the knight in dragon-scale who is dead and hasn't been told. And what Hel asked: *lay the dragon warrior to rest.*

Hesper does not say anything for a long time. When he does, his voice has changed -- the unremarkableness has gone out of it.

"Say the last part again." -- "Lay the dragon warrior to rest."

"*Rest*," he repeats. "Not destroy. Not banish. **Rest.**" He puts both hands flat on his knees. "Madam, you don't lay a demon to rest. There's nothing in a demon to rest. You bind it or you break it and either way you may as well be arguing with a fire.

"You lay a *dead man* to rest."

He stands and takes two steps and stops, because it's a small room. "'The old kind. The kind that remembers being a person.' That is a **shade**. A person who died and did not go where the dead go -- kept, and bound, and put somewhere. And you cannot keep a shade in a jar or a stone for long, it wears through. You keep it in a *living body*, because a living body renews itself, and the wearing-through goes into the flesh instead of the vessel.

"Which is why somebody opens the same place on that boy's chest every single morning. It's not torture and it's not a ritual scar. It's *maintenance*. You bleed the host to keep the seat clean. The grey your laundress can't boil out is what comes off the seam -- that's the shade's wear coming out of him with the blood, and it's numbing her hands through a copper of lye."

Gardwen has not moved at all. "How long," she says, "can they keep a person like that?"

"Years," says Hesper. "If they start young enough that the body grows around it."

Gardwen sits down.

### Event 69 <!-- id: myth-event-a7dc0ddf0c96, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T16:01:42.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, The Pearl -->

Magda puts a hand on the back of Gardwen's neck. "He was seven, Magda. I've spent ten years hoping he was alive. I never once thought about what for."

She's gone rigid all the way up.

"Look at me." She does, eventually. Her eyes are dry and that's worse.

"Seventeen," she says. "*If they start young enough.*" -- "Aye."

"He was seven, Magda." Her voice is very steady and very small. "I've spent ten years hoping he was alive. I never once thought about what for."

She takes a breath and puts it away -- Magda can see her do it, the way she did at the ford -- and it doesn't go all the way in this time.

"I'm all right." -- "You're not." -- "No." A pause. "But I'll do."

### Event 70 <!-- id: myth-event-b7c0b0373f50, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T16:01:43.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa, Marisette, The Pearl -->

MAGDA'S OWN QUESTION: "Didn't they ring some bells around noon? Were the two things related?" They were. It was the fouling alarm, and Prince Emeric silenced it in writing.

"The bells." Marisette sets her cup down. "Yes. Yesterday, just after midday. Everyone assumed it was for the Baron's entry, because it started while the column was in Caravan Square and this city will assume anything."

"It wasn't?"

"It was the **Lady's temple on Temple Isle**. And that wasn't a welcome peal, it was ragged and out of order and it came from the water-tower, not the campanile. That's the *fouling* alarm. They ring it when something goes wrong with the sacred channel -- a dead beast in the intake, a spill, a body. It's rung four times in my life."

Hesper says quietly: "And they stopped it inside a quarter of an hour."

"They did. Which they don't do unless somebody senior tells them to. **The High Priestess Nerissa rang the alarm on the day Hanzo di Teufel entered this city, and then somebody had it silenced before the third peal, and nobody has said a word about it since.**"

She picks the pen up and puts it down again.

"And her niece has red hair."

### Event 71 <!-- id: myth-event-ec626a39a8d7, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T16:06:59.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Hesper, Marisette, The Pearl -->

"There's only 8 of them on that boat. With enough men we could just go and get him." Marisette: "It's not that it's stupid. It's that it's *last*." Hesper: "You'd be stealing a lit lamp."

Marisette doesn't flinch and she doesn't laugh.

"Eight is eight. I can put twenty men at that pier before the sixth bell tomorrow. Crowbill can put forty, and he'd do it for what's in your purse, because he'd rather do it than be asked twice."

She lets that sit.

"And then I have twenty men against eight Dragon Knights in scale, on a deck, at night, and I lose eleven of them and it's still even money. And by dawn every one of them has a name and a mother on this island, and Hanzo di Teufel -- who is at this moment eating supper with the Governor's brother -- knows to the hour who came for his ship."

She spreads her hands. "It's not that it's stupid, Magda. It's that it's *last*. You only get to do it once, and after you've done it there is nothing else you can do."

Hesper cuts in, and he's not being diplomatic. "There's a worse problem. **You'd be stealing a lit lamp.**

"Whatever is seated in that boy is held there by something, and it is not the ship and it is not those eight men. Take the body out and you have not freed anyone -- you have carried a bound shade into the middle of the Pearl with its keepers coming after it, and the first thing that happens when the maintenance stops is that the wearing-through goes somewhere else. You cannot rescue him until you know what the anchor is. Otherwise you're not rescuing a boy. You're moving a problem into a house full of women."

Marisette nods slowly. "So. My play. **Nerissa.** She rang that alarm and somebody silenced her, and she has been sitting on it for a day and a half, and her sister's daughter's hair is in your pocket. She has the only authority in this city that can stop that Tourney, and she has just been given a personal reason to want to. That is the strongest ally available and nobody has gone to her, because nobody knows what you know."

### Event 72 <!-- id: myth-event-8e255a5815f2, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T16:10:21.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Ruhi, Temple Isle -->

"The wolf can come too?" -- "Take the wolf. Don't explain the wolf to anybody. Let them explain it to themselves."

Marisette actually smiles -- the first one all night, and it's brief. She's already writing the note.

"A grey wolf walking to heel beside a woman in woodland leathers, at the Lady's own gate, at night." She blots it. "Nerissa's people will not see two strangers with a message. They'll see something that came out of the wild to the water, which is the oldest shape their faith has, and half of them will make a sign at it and the other half will fetch somebody senior *very quickly*.

"That's worth more than my seal. Don't explain the wolf to anybody. Let them explain it to themselves."

Ruhi comes off the scullery step reluctantly, and Ila watches from the doorway with her arms wrapped round herself and doesn't ask to come.

Temple Isle after dark is a different city. No bunting. The canals run quieter here and the water in them is *clear* -- you can see the bottom by lamplight, which you have not been able to do anywhere else in Purewater.

At the gate, two of the Swords of the Lake see the wolf first. One of them makes a sign against his breast. The other says, without taking his eyes off Ruhi: "It's the middle of the night."

### Event 73 <!-- id: myth-event-ecccc200d09c, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T16:22:16.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa, Temple Isle -->

"Say the worst of it first and we'll work backwards." Magda does -- pigs and all, with a sweet smile. Nerissa's Insight 56/80: not a single lie.

"The worst of it? That'd be when I killed Hanzo's evil little shit of a son after he bound a demon into one of the girls at the Sylph's Embrace... and then fed his body to pigs. Working backwards from there, we think he was planning on doing the same thing to your niece. Oh, and we think the Dragon Knights kidnapped her brother ten years ago and is hosting a shade in him so that he can fight in the tourney in a few days. That's about all of it."

Blue eyes gleaming with a sweet smile.

**Nerissa -- Insight (80). Rolled 56.** She has spent thirty years listening to people lie in that room. She reads Magda all the way through, and what she finds is that she has not told her a single lie.

Nerissa doesn't move for a while. Her feet stay in the water.

"You smiled," she says at last, "when you said *pigs*." -- "Aye."

"Mm." She takes her feet out of the tank and dries them on the hem of her robe like a farmer's wife, and gets up. "Good. I'd not trust a person who could say that flatly."

### Event 74 <!-- id: myth-event-ba0fec91fd01, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T16:22:30.000000000, session: 1, involves: High Priestess Nerissa, Temple Isle -->

NERISSA'S HALF: the channel went THIN four minutes after noon, and Prince Emeric silenced her alarm in writing before the third peal.

"Now I'll tell you mine, and then we'll be even.

"Yesterday, four minutes after noon, this channel went **wrong**. Not fouled -- I've had fouled, fouled is a dead ox and a week of prayers. This went *thin*. The Lady's presence in her own water went thin, the way a voice goes thin at a distance, in the space of about ten heartbeats." Her hands have closed. "I have served here since I was nineteen and it has never once done that.

"So I rang the tower. And by the third peal there was a boy from the Governor's household at my gate with a written instruction to *cease*, on the grounds that the city was welcoming a distinguished guest and the bells were causing alarm during a formal entry."

She turns round. "Written and sealed by **Prince Emeric**. Who is twenty-four years old, and well-meaning, and who has been told by three separate men on his council this fortnight that his uncle would like the Tourney to go smoothly."

"And you stopped."

"I stopped." Something flat and furious goes across her face. "Because I am the High Priestess of a temple that is paid for by this city's Guilds, and because I had *nothing*. A thin feeling in the water and no cause I could name in front of a magistrate. If I had rung on I would have been removed by Thursday and replaced by somebody who wouldn't ring at all."

She comes back and sits down on the step again, closer. "And now an old woman walks in at midnight and tells me there is a ship at the Forge Quarter pier that steams where it sits, that arrived five days ago, and that has a dead man bound into a living boy aboard it.

"Say the part about my niece again. Slowly."

### Event 75 <!-- id: myth-event-1150f366ec1b, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T16:24:16.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa, Temple Isle -->

Nerissa asks one question about the hair that nobody else thought to ask: is there a knot in it, or is it cut clean at both ends?

Magda lays it all out on the low table by the lamp and takes it apart for her the way you'd show someone a bad joint of meat. The cloak, the tailor's tower, the ring, the letter, the eleven coins. And then the oiled silk.

"Five of these. Four still full. Hair, bound at one end with copper wire. And a bit of something brown that isn't wax. That one's empty. He used it." -- "On the courtesan." -- "Aye." -- "Whose hair is black." -- "Aye."

Nerissa picks up the first twist. She doesn't open it. She holds it, and her thumb moves once across the silk.

"So he came into this city carrying five women in his coat," she says. "And he spent one of them on a girl at Constantine's, and he had four left."

She opens it then, and lets the hair out into the lamplight, and looks at it a long moment. Then, in a voice that has gone completely level:

"Beneath the wire. Is there a knot in it, or is it cut clean at both ends?"

Magda turns it. The wire's at one end. The other end of the hank is blunt, square, all the strands the same length. "Cut clean."

"Then it was taken with shears, off a head, by somebody standing behind her." Nerissa lays it down very carefully. "It was not gathered off a comb or a pillow. **Somebody got close enough to my sister's daughter to put shears in her hair, and she did not scream, and she has not told me.**

"Her name is **Sana**. She's nineteen. And she has been at the temple school on Lost Isle for six weeks and I have not seen her in three."

### Event 76 <!-- id: myth-event-0e60610845ac, type: gm-note, at: 2026-09-06T16:27:20.000000000 -->

Nerissa now has a cause she can name

### Event 77 <!-- id: myth-event-dc683f48d8cc, type: gm-note, at: 2026-09-06T16:27:20.000000000 -->

Marisette had the culprit's name inside a day -- Magda walked it to her before dawn

### Event 78 <!-- id: myth-event-0175ea0878ec, type: session-end, at: 2026-09-06T16:27:55.000000000, session: 1 -->

SESSION 1 ends at the Temple of the Lake Lady, midnight of d-2. Magda arrived at the ford a stranger and by the end of two days has sworn herself to Gardwen, killed the Baron's heir and destroyed the body, allied with the ruler of the Pearl, and brought the High Priestess of the Lake Lady into the story. Santo's binding SUCCEEDED (a divergence -- Emmeralda is possessed, not dying). Blau is hunting the missing heir and has not yet found the trail.

### Event 79 <!-- id: myth-event-2a8086a95197, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T16:27:55.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Hesper, Marisette -->

CH.6 -- Marisette's counting room, dusk. Magda reports the DragonBarge recon. Hesper, who has spent the afternoon with the demon in Emmeralda, identifies what is aboard: not a demon but a SHADE -- a bound dead man in a living host, maintained by the daily bleeding. Magda tells them Hel's dream ('lay the dragon warrior to rest'); Hesper seizes on 'rest' as proof it is a dead man, not a demon. Marisette refuses a frontal assault on the ship and sends them to High Priestess Nerissa instead.

HESPER on the demon in Emmeralda: 'It's bored, it's fond of the girl's memories... it complained that it had been promised something better than a courtesan. It said its master's father keeps a PROPER one. The old kind, the kind that remembers being a person.'
MAGDA: 'Like a ghost? Like a dead knight?' -- then told them Hel's dream.
HESPER: 'You do not lay a demon to rest. There is nothing in a demon to rest... You lay a DEAD MAN to rest.'
HESPER: 'It is not torture and it is not a ritual scar. It is MAINTENANCE. You bleed the host to keep the seat clean.'
GARDWEN: 'How long can they keep a person like that?' HESPER: 'Years. If they start young enough that the body grows around it.'
GARDWEN, afterwards: 'He was seven, Magda. I have spent ten years hoping he was alive. I never once thought about what for.'
MAGDA proposing a direct assault: 'There's only 8 of them on that boat. With enough men we could just go and get him.'
MARISETTE: 'It is not that it is stupid. It is that it is LAST. You only get to do it once.'
HESPER: 'You would be stealing a lit lamp... You cannot rescue him until you know what the anchor is.'
MARISETTE on the bells: 'That was not a welcome peal. That is the FOULING alarm... The High Priestess rang it on the day Hanzo di Teufel entered this city, and somebody had it silenced before the third peal. And her niece has red hair.'

### Event 80 <!-- id: myth-event-abf6c1e1b096, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T16:27:55.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, High Priestess Nerissa, Lilura Deepcurrent -->

CH.7 -- The Temple of the Lake Lady, midnight. Marisette's note and a grey wolf at the gate get them to Nerissa, who has not slept since noon the day before. Magda confesses the killing and the pigs outright; Nerissa reads her as truthful (Insight 56/80) and trades her own secret: the sacred channel went THIN four minutes after noon as the Baron entered, and Prince Emeric silenced her alarm in writing. Shown Santo's five twists of hair, Nerissa determines they were cut with shears from behind -- and names her niece Sana, nineteen, unseen for three weeks.

NERISSA: 'Say the worst of it first and we will work backwards.'
MAGDA: 'That'd be when I killed Hanzo's evil little shit of a son after he bound a demon into one of the girls at the Sylph's Embrace... and then fed his body to pigs.'
NERISSA: 'You smiled when you said pigs.' MAGDA: 'Aye.' NERISSA: 'Good. I'd not trust a person who could say that flatly.'
NERISSA: 'This went THIN. The Lady's presence in her own water went thin, the way a voice goes thin at a distance, in the space of about ten heartbeats. I have served here since I was nineteen and it has never once done that.'

NERISSA, examining the hair: 'Beneath the wire. Is there a knot in it, or is it cut clean at both ends?' ... 'Then it was taken with shears, off a head, by somebody standing behind her. Somebody got close enough to my sister's daughter to put shears in her hair, and she did not scream, and she has not told me.'
NERISSA: 'Her name is Sana. She's nineteen. She has been at the temple school on Lost Isle for six weeks and I have not seen her in three.'
MECHANICS: Nerissa Insight 56/80 success -- read Magda as entirely truthful.

### Event 81 <!-- id: myth-event-48cc981a3a75, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T17:06:49.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Temple Isle -->

"What does Gardwen want to do?" -- "I want to go to that pier tonight. So I'm not to be trusted about this. I know that about myself."

Gardwen doesn't answer straight away. She's looking at the water going through the tank.

"I want to go to that pier tonight." Flat. No performance in it. "I want to walk down there and kill eight men and take him off that ship, and I've been standing here for the last ten minutes working out how I'd do it, and the answer is I couldn't, and I'd have gone anyway. So I'm not to be trusted about this. I know that about myself. I've known it since about the third hour after we left that wall."

She puts her back against the tank-step and slides down to sit on the stone.

"Here's the sense of it, and I hate every word.

"My brother has been in that condition for ten years. He was in it yesterday and he'll be in it tomorrow, and whatever they do to him at dawn they've done four hundred times. One more night doesn't change what he is -- it just changes whether I ruin the only chance of getting him *out*. Hesper's right. Take him now and I'm carrying a lit lamp into a house full of women, and the first thing that goes wrong goes wrong on Ila.

"Sana is different. Sana's a girl who's had shears in her hair by someone who got behind her, and there are three days of Tourney left and four twists in your pocket and one of them's hers. She can still be got to *first*.

"So. Sana. Tonight, if the priestess will move."

Then, quieter: "But Magda -- when we do go for him. When we know the anchor and it's time." Her jaw sets. "**I'll not be talked into waiting a second time.** You should know that now, so it's not a surprise later."

### Event 82 <!-- id: myth-event-8c896feeed03, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T17:15:13.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Temple Isle -->

"I'm pledged to you girl. Where you go, I go." Gardwen doesn't say anything. She takes Magda's wrist and holds it for three seconds and then goes and checks her knife.

That's the whole of it. It's enough.

And Nerissa, on the letters: "My sister has had two letters from Sana in six weeks. The first came at the end of the fourth week. The second came eight days ago and it was three lines and it was *fine*, and my sister showed it to me and said 'she's settling,' and I said 'good.'

"Three lines. In a hand I'd know anywhere, from a girl who once wrote me eleven pages about a heron."

She goes past them toward the colonnade and the water-stair, and she does not walk like a woman of sixty. "Bring the wolf."

### Event 83 <!-- id: myth-event-57697615c043, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T17:19:05.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, The Northern Isles -->

Corbie's at one in the morning, with a light burning over the door. "We sent a boat at dusk, Mother -- didn't it come?" Nothing came. And there is a light moving on Lost Isle.

Magda limbers as she walks. Neck first -- it goes with a crack that makes one of the Swords glance round. Then the shoulders, then two slow swings of the staff at half-length in the colonnade, feeling where the joints are tonight, which is: not good, but adequate. She counts the sling bullets by touch in the pouch. **Eleven.** She'd have liked twenty.

Gardwen watches her do it and doesn't say anything, and then starts checking her own kit, which is thinner than hers.

Twenty minutes on black water with two Swords rowing. Nobody talks. Nerissa sits in the bow with the lamp shuttered and her hand trailing over the side in the water the whole way, which is not idleness -- you can see her listening to it.

The woman who opens the door is the mistress of the school and she has been crying. She sees Nerissa and her face does something terrible.

"We sent to you at *dusk*," she says. "We sent a boat at dusk, Mother -- didn't it come?"

Nerissa has gone very still on the step. "No," she says. "Nothing came."

"Sana's gone. Since yesterday morning. We've had every girl out looking and we've dragged the channel and I sent Bettan to the temple *myself*--"

And across the water, four hundred yards off, Lost Isle sits low and black and weed-choked with nothing on it at all. Except that there's a light moving on it. Not a lamp -- too low, and the colour's wrong. A dull red, close to the waterline, that doesn't swing the way a carried light swings.

### Event 84 <!-- id: myth-event-1b2fbc2f7a23, type: gm-note, at: 2026-09-06T17:19:07.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda -->

PLAYER PROMPT: "Don't forget about Gardwen's magic. She can do stuff." She had been run as a healer with hands for two days. She is a Cleric with nine spells.

Piety 87, Channel 85, 18 magic points. Rank 1: Animal Friendship, Barkskin, Command, Cure Minor Wounds, **Detect Evil**, **Know Passions**, Entangle, Slow Poison, Remove Fear. Rank 2: Cure Serious Wounds, Cure Disease, Hold Person.

Detect Evil and Know Passions had been going to waste for two days. She could have read Ann Yannick, or Nerissa, or the thing in Emmeralda.

The correction turns the entire Lost Isle operation from a search into a reconnaissance -- and it came from the player, not the GM.

### Event 85 <!-- id: myth-event-05c1205e2f42, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T17:21:55.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, High Priestess Nerissa, Lost Isle -->

Detect Evil across four hundred yards of open channel. Piety 46 against 44 -- she misses by two. "I got about halfway across and it went out like a candle in a draught."

She goes down on one knee at the water's edge and puts both hands flat in the channel, and says something under her breath that has the shape of a name in it. And you *feel* the room she makes -- the way the air goes when a door opens somewhere else in a house. Then it closes. Nothing comes.

She sits back on her heels, breathing like she ran here. "Too far. It's not the distance, it's -- the water's all *hers* here, and I'm not hers, and I was asking a favour in someone else's house."

Nerissa has been watching her the whole time with an expression Magda can't read at all.

"You reached," she says. It isn't a question. "From the bank. Across running water that isn't yours, at a rank you shouldn't manage."

"I didn't reach far enough."

"No." Nerissa is already unpinning her other sleeve. "You reached *at all*. Girl -- **you were asking a favour in someone else's house because you were standing on the doorstep shouting.**"

She steps down into the channel until she's shin-deep, and holds out her hand. "So come inside and ask properly. Take my hand and do it again, and this time the Lady will hear where it came from."

Across the water, the dull red light stops moving. Then it goes out.

### Event 86 <!-- id: myth-event-f6b21527d8f0, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T17:24:29.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, High Priestess Nerissa, Lost Isle -->

PLAYER FINDS THE MECHANIC: "Could she use a passion to augment it?" Love (Brother + Family) 80 -> +16. 87+16 = 103, Hard -> 69. Rolled 46. She's through.

A woman who has spent ten years looking for a taken child, being asked to find a taken girl. That is squarely inside the passion, and Nerissa channelling it removes the problem that beat her the first time.

She takes Nerissa's hand and goes into the channel up to her knees, and the second time is not like the first. Her fingers lock on Nerissa's hard enough to hurt and her breath goes out of her all at once, and Nerissa stands there in the water and takes it without moving, the way you'd hold a rope.

Then Gardwen starts talking, fast, flat, describing as she goes:

"There's -- the whole island's *humming*. Underneath. Not evil, just old, there's a great deal of something old down under the weed and it doesn't care about us at all. Above that. On the stone. There's a *working*."

"Say what you see," Nerissa says quietly.

"Same flavour as the chalk on her floor. Same hand. It's not finished -- it's *laid out*, like a table before dinner. It's cold. It's been cold a while. Whoever set it hasn't come back to it in a day or more."

"Living things."

"Two moving." Her head turns, slowly, tracking. "Both of them wrong. Not demons -- *men*, but wrong, the way milk's wrong. They're low down and they're together and they've stopped, they've gone still since the light went out--"

She stops. Her whole face changes.

"There's a third. Not moving. Not wrong. She's *small* in it, Magda, she's gone right down small the way you do when you're hiding -- and she's above the other two, up high, in the stone."

"Is she alive?" -- "She's *frightened*," Gardwen says. "You can't be frightened if you're not alive."

Then she opens her eyes and looks straight across four hundred yards of black water. "She's been there since yesterday morning and they haven't found her yet."

### Event 87 <!-- id: myth-event-9e01a2041e39, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T17:32:24.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Lost Isle -->

GARDWEN'S PLAN, drawn in the mud of the bank: their boat first, then the girl, then the men. "That's the whole game. Do that and they're on a two-hundred-yard rock with us."

"South side. Land in the weed, not on stone -- weed's quiet and it'll take the boat's noise. Then we don't go *at* them."

She crouches and draws it in the mud with her finger.

"**One -- their boat.** They came out here somehow and it'll be on the north side with them. If they're spooked they run for it, and then we've got nothing and they've got a day's start. Someone takes it or holes it before anything else happens. That's the whole game. Do that and they're on a two-hundred-yard rock with us.

"**Two -- Sana.** She's been in that stone since yesterday morning with no water and she's hiding from *anything that moves*. If a Sword in armour comes round a corner at her she'll bolt, and if she bolts she goes into the weed in the dark and we lose her for good. So it's me and Ruhi that go up, and it's me that calls to her, because I'm the only one here who isn't wearing anything.

"**Three -- then your two men.** Not before. Once the boat's gone and the girl's out of the tower they've nowhere to be and nothing to bargain with, and we can take them at our leisure, from three sides. Alive if they'll have it. Because whoever laid that working knows what the anchor is."

She wipes the mud off her finger. "That's my call. You said kill or capture -- I'd say **capture hard**. But Magda, they're wrong. I don't know what that means yet and neither does she. So don't let anybody close with them alone."

### Event 88 <!-- id: myth-event-cba4250caa32, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T17:34:25.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Lost Isle -->

The approach: 38/60 clean, Magda's Stealth 20/68. Lost Isle up close is a STREET -- doorways and window-holes at chest height, standing in four feet of black water.

Eleven minutes across, oars muffled in somebody's shawl, short strokes, no feathering. The weed takes the boat with a long wet sigh and nothing more.

She goes over the side into thigh-deep water and mud that wants her boots, and makes less noise doing it than the young Sword does.

Lost Isle up close is a street. That's the thing that gets you. You can see the line of it -- doorways and window-holes at chest height, a gutter, the stub of a wellhead -- and it's all standing in four feet of black water with weed growing out of the windows. You are walking up somebody's road with the water at your thighs.

Nerissa goes past and touches a doorframe as she passes, briefly, the way you'd touch a coffin.

### Event 89 <!-- id: myth-event-a05bbf397196, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T17:38:06.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Lost Isle -->

"Say what she calls my mother." Magda can't -- so she uses the eleven-page letter about a heron instead. Sana 54/60. She believes it.

Gardwen calls up the stairwell, pitched to carry that far and no further: "**Sana.** Your aunt sent me. Nerissa sent me. She's stood in the water at the corner and she's got her mace and she is *extremely* angry."

Nothing. Ruhi's ears are forward and locked on something above.

Then, from three floors up, very small and very hoarse: "*Say what she calls my mother.*"

Magda doesn't know. So she says the other thing.

"I don't know but I know you sent her an eleven page letter about a heron. The three line one note you sent raised an alarm for her and that's why we're here."

A long silence. Long enough that Gardwen looks at her.

Then: "I don't-- " The voice breaks and comes back. "I don't remember writing eleven pages."

"You did. She's told it to me twice tonight and she's not a woman who repeats herself."

Something moves up there. Stone grit comes down and lands in the water. "Is she *really* here?"

"Aye. Stood in the channel at the corner with a mace in her belt and no robe on."

A sound that might be a laugh and is mostly not.

### Event 90 <!-- id: myth-event-fd1950aed015, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T17:39:00.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Lost Isle -->

Sana comes down -- nineteen, barefoot, hair chopped ragged on the left side, looking about twelve. "They didn't come for me. They came for the *place*."

It takes a long time, because she has been folded into a gap between two floor-joists for a day and a half and her legs have stopped being reliable. Twice she stops and just breathes.

Gardwen goes up the half-stair to meet her and brings her the last of it, and she comes out into the weed-light barefoot in a school shift. Dark red hair. What's left of it.

She grips Gardwen's forearm and won't let go and talks in a rush, hoarse, too fast:

"There were three of them yesterday. Three. There's only been two since this morning -- the other one went off in the boat before dawn and hasn't come back--"

"Slow down, lass."

"You don't understand." She's shaking. "**They didn't come for me. They came for the *place*.** I only ran because I *saw* -- I came out to look at the ruin like everyone does and they were *already here*, and they had a girl from the school with them, and she was walking with them like she knew them."

She looks between them.

"Bettan. Her name's Bettan. She's fourteen. She's the one they sent to the temple last night with the message."

### Event 91 <!-- id: myth-event-3690d2062f62, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T17:42:06.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Lost Isle -->

MAGDA'S QUESTION: "How well do you know this girl? Did she change recently?" And Sana, clever, makes herself stop shaking to answer it properly.

"About a month ago.

"She was -- Bettan's a *baby*. Fourteen and homesick and cries at everything, and she'd got herself half-drowned in the misery of it, and none of us were kind enough about it. Then she went quiet for about a week. Properly quiet, wouldn't come down to meals, and Mistress Corliss had her in and we all thought she'd be sent home.

"And then she came back *cheerful*."

She looks up. "That's the bit. Not better -- *cheerful*. And she started being lovely to everyone. Girls she'd never spoken to. She sat with Ana Vell for a whole week and Ana Vell is *horrible*. We all said it was nice. Mistress Corliss said it was nice. Everyone was so pleased."

Then, and her voice drops: "**She stopped wearing her shawl. In *February*.** She'd sit in the window bay with the shutter open at night doing her letters and the rest of us are three to a bed with chilblains, and she'd say she was warm.

"And she started asking about people's families. All the time. Who's your mother, where's your house, have you got sisters. And we all told her, because she was being *nice*."

She looks at the ragged side of her own head.

"She did my hair. Three weeks ago. She said she could do the plait my aunt does and she stood behind me with the scissors and she did it *beautifully*, and I said thank you."

### Event 92 <!-- id: myth-event-b928650073de, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T17:44:18.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa, Lost Isle -->

Corvin and Ash find the punt fast (14/60). Two men sitting side by side in the dark, not talking, for twenty minutes. Nerissa: "are waiting for something."

"Found it, Mother. Not a boat -- a **punt**, flat, no keel, tied off in a doorway about sixty yards up. Corvin's sat on it. It's poled, not rowed. And it's the *only* one -- we went the length of the north side."

Which means the third man took the other boat before dawn and hasn't brought it back, and these two have been on this rock all day with one flat punt between them.

"And them?"

"Forty yards past it, in what's left of a big room -- half a roof on, no wall on the water side. There's a fire *in* there, laid on a slab, but it's not lit now. They've a lantern with a red slide. And Mother -- they've been *digging*. There's a heap of weed and muck pulled up out of the floor and stacked, and something under it they've uncovered. Stone. Cut stone with work on it.

"They're just sitting. Both of them, on the edge of the slab, side by side, not talking. Been like that since the light went out."

Gardwen has gone very still at *not talking*. "How long is that now?" -- "Near on twenty minutes."

Nerissa lifts the mace slightly. "Two men who have sat in the dark without speaking for twenty minutes are waiting for something."

### Event 93 <!-- id: myth-event-fd992c98fa00, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T17:46:03.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Lost Isle -->

Magda goes up the drowned street alone. Both men turn their heads at once -- "not together like men who heard the same thing. *At once*, like two doors on one hinge."

Sixty yards. The punt in its doorway with Corvin folded down beside it, who doesn't look up as she passes.

Half a roof, no wall on the water side, so you can see straight in from thirty feet: a stone floor above the waterline, a raised slab, and the heap of dragged-up weed and muck stacked against the standing wall. Where they've cleared it there's cut stone showing -- deep-carved, and the carving goes on down under the water at the edge, and it is a great deal older than anything else on this island.

The red-slide lantern sits on the slab, shut. And the two of them are sitting side by side on the edge of it, facing the water, facing *her*, in the dark.

She is twenty feet away before either moves.

Then both of them turn their heads at once. Not together like men who heard the same thing -- *at once*, the same speed, the same distance, like two doors on one hinge.

Neither stands.

One of them -- sandy, thirties, a boatman's forearms -- says in an entirely ordinary voice: "You're not her."

And the other one, without any pause at all, in the same voice, finishes it: "--she was meant to bring the girl."

### Event 94 <!-- id: myth-event-97471f1842fd, type: combat, at: 2026-09-06T17:49:07.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Hollow man (sandy) -->

"No girl, just little old me. All alone surrounded by big scary warriors" -- and the stone goes out on the last word. 56/128, right arm, serious. He doesn't look at it.

It takes the sandy one high on the right arm, on the outside, and you *hear* the bone go from twenty feet.

The arm drops. It doesn't swing -- it just stops being part of him and hangs.

He doesn't make a sound. He doesn't look at it. He doesn't even look at *her*.

Both of them stand up at the same moment, the same speed, like the slab tipped them.

And the sandy one says, conversationally, with his arm hanging off him: "That's going to make the poling difficult."

And the other one finishes: "--we'll manage."

They come off the slab into the water together. The billhook comes up off the wall into the second one's hands.

### Event 95 <!-- id: myth-event-4feef8a8ef5c, type: combat, at: 2026-09-06T17:51:39.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Hollow man (sandy), Hollow man (billhook) -->

Parry and disarm on the differential, then everything into the sandy one's already-broken arm. Bone through the sleeve. Neither of them has bled a drop.

The billhook comes in at her shins and she doesn't step back -- she drops the staff-end into its path and rolls her wrists, and the hooked head skids off the shaft and goes *past*, and while his weight is still following it she carries the same motion up and round and takes the haft out of his hands with the top third of the staff. It goes into the black water. He watches it go.

Then she pivots off the back foot and puts everything into the sandy one. Aiming for the same arm. She gets it.

It's already broken high up and this takes it below the elbow, and there is a wet crack and the whole limb comes apart at two places at once, and what's hanging off his shoulder now is not a limb. Bone through the sleeve. Six inches of it.

He goes back a step from the force of it. Only one. Then he straightens up.

He looks at the ruin of his own arm -- and this time he *does* look at it, curiously, turning slightly to see it better, the way you'd inspect a torn coat.

And the other one, standing empty-handed in the water beside him, says in that same flat conversational voice: "That's the arm gone, then."

"Mm," says the sandy one.

**Neither of them has bled a drop.**

### Event 96 <!-- id: myth-event-cf6e185cb5eb, type: gm-note, at: 2026-09-06T17:55:38.000000000, session: 1 -->

PLAYER CATCHES A RULES ERROR TWICE: "One of them already attacked this round." Then: "Check the rules. How many active attacks per round?" ONE proactive action. AP above that buys defences only.

> "On their turn a combatant spends 1 Action Point on ONE Proactive Action -- one per round, no more (Mythras Imperative has no extra action cycles). Remaining AP may only be spent on Reactive Actions (Parry, Evade, Interrupt)."

Two of the attacks run in Round 1 shouldn't have happened: the billhook-man's follow-up dagger, and Sandy's final undefended lunge. Both were second proactive actions in the same round. Both missed, so nothing had to be un-wounded.

Which also means Magda never actually ran out of defences. The grim moment where a hollow man was a foot away and she had nothing left was bookkeeping, not fiction.

The rule went into CLAUDE.md.

### Event 97 <!-- id: myth-event-3d8415564822, type: combat, at: 2026-09-06T17:59:41.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Hollow man (sandy) -->

Gardwen's Hold Person takes -- and it should not. "There shouldn't be enough person in him to grip."

She doesn't shout it. She puts one hand out flat toward him and says three words in the old tongue, and the water between them goes still in a ring.

Sandy stops. Not slumped -- *stopped*, mid-step, with his weight forward and his one good hand half-raised and the ruined arm still swinging gently from the momentum he had a second ago. It swings, and slows, and hangs.

His eyes go on moving. They track from Gardwen to Magda and back. Something in him is furious about this, and none of it reaches his face.

"Ohhh," he says. Just that. Perfectly pleasant.

Gardwen has gone white. "It shouldn't hold," she says. "Magda -- that's *Hold Person*. **There shouldn't be enough person in him to grip.**"

### Event 98 <!-- id: myth-event-7cadc3cb54f4, type: combat, at: 2026-09-06T18:03:08.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Ruhi, Hollow man (billhook) -->

The billhook-man takes hold of Magda's forearm and will not let go. Ruhi trips him and both of them go under. He isn't struggling. He doesn't need to breathe and he has all night.

He goes *through* the parry and takes hold. One hand closes on the staff. The other closes on her left forearm above the wrist, and it is like being gripped by a piece of machinery -- no squeeze, no threat, just a closing to a fixed position and then no further movement at all.

She has felt a great many hands in seventy years and none of them were this cold through wet wool.

He doesn't pull. He doesn't twist. He turns his head and calls, conversationally, to nobody in particular: "I've got this one." And behind her, frozen mid-step with his arm hanging in two pieces, Sandy says quite clearly: "--good."

Ruhi goes low, takes the leg at the back of the knee, and *pulls* -- forty stone of wolf against a man standing in three feet of water with both hands committed to holding an old woman.

He goes. And because he will not let go, she goes with him.

Under there it's silent, and cold, and absolutely black. He is not struggling. He is not trying to hold her down. He is simply lying on the bottom of a drowned street holding on, waiting, because he does not need to breathe and he has all night.

Her face is about eight inches under.

Gardwen's **Command** opens the hand -- all four fingers at once, like a catch releasing -- and Magda comes up off the bottom into air and lamplight coughing canal.

He sits up in the water beside her with his hands open in his lap, palms turned upward, and looks at them. "That wasn't me," he says, with what might almost be interest.

### Event 99 <!-- id: myth-event-9596d91e06f3, type: combat, at: 2026-09-06T18:10:18.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa, Hollow man (billhook) -->

THE KILLING THAT SHOULD NOT HAVE HAPPENED. He had stopped, put his hands down and stepped aside to show Nerissa the stone. Magda takes his head off anyway.

He gets up, doesn't come at her, and wades unhurried toward the slab with his hands still open at his sides. Nerissa is between him and it, and she doesn't move, and neither does he.

They look at each other across four feet of black water.

Then he says: "You're the Lady's, aren't you." -- "I am." -- "Good," he says. "She'll want to see this."

And he steps sideways, out of her path, and gestures with an open hand at the cut stone they've spent a day digging out of the weed.

**Berserker, Easy (85 -> 128), no defence. 75. Eight damage. Head: -4 of 5. Serious.**

She comes out of the water and doesn't say anything, and plants her back foot in the mud and swings the way you'd swing at a fence post that had offended you. Six feet of ash across the temple. Nothing between it and the bone.

His head goes sideways past where a head goes. He drops where he stands, straight down, no stagger. The body doesn't float.

Nerissa doesn't move. She's still standing exactly where she was, mace up, four feet away, with her mouth slightly open. She lowers the mace slowly.

"He had stopped," she says. Not accusing. Just stating it, like a woman reading a figure back to check she has it right. "**He had stopped, and he was showing me the stone.**"

### Event 100 <!-- id: myth-event-32ab2e144db9, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T18:15:02.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa, Lost Isle -->

"I'm a berserker. That means I fight until someone convinces me to stop." Nerissa's Insight: 4 against 80. CRITICAL. She reads Magda completely -- and gives her a reason instead of an order.

Whatever she was going to say next, she doesn't say. She changes her footing -- stops standing like a woman about to remonstrate and starts standing like a woman at the side of a horse that's still running.

"Ah," she says. Very quietly. "*Ah.* You're not deciding anything, are you."

She lowers the mace all the way and lets it hang.

"I've seen this twice. Once in a man at the Shoals after a fire, and once in my own father." She keeps her voice completely even -- not soothing, not raised, the pace of someone reading a list. "**You're not cruel. You've simply not been given a reason to stop yet, and nobody in this water has thought to give you one, they've only told you to.**"

She moves round until she's in front of her rather than beside her, slowly, so she can see her do it.

"So. Here's the reason.

"**Sana is in a boat forty yards behind us**, nineteen years old, with her hair cut off, watching. That girl has spent a day and a half in a hole in the dark being brave, and what she can see from there is an old woman breaking a man who can't move.

"That's it. That's the whole reason. Not the man -- he's nothing to me and I'd hang him tomorrow. **Her.**"

She holds out one open hand. Not to take the staff. Just open.

Magda doesn't answer and doesn't lower the staff. She just stops. Water running off her. Breathing like a bellows. Everything in her face still exactly where it was -- and standing entirely still in the middle of it, which is not the same as being calm, and everyone there knows the difference.

### Event 101 <!-- id: myth-event-84a8247cd13e, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T18:22:30.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Hollow man (sandy) -->

Sandy comes back to life mid-step, trussed, and smiles for the first time all night: "You're the one who did the boy in the canal. The face like a spade. He described you before he went."

Corvin and Ash work fast and say nothing. Elbows first, then the wrists behind, then a running loop from the wrists to the ankles so he can shuffle and not stride. They do it around the ruined arm rather than through it, which is more care than Magda would have taken.

Gardwen counts down under her breath -- "*--four, three--*" -- and on two the ring of stillness lets go and Sandy comes back to life mid-step and finds himself trussed and sitting. He doesn't fight it. He looks down at the rope with mild interest and settles.

Then he looks up, finds Magda, and smiles.

"You're the one who did the boy in the canal," he says. "The face like a spade. He described you before he went."

*He described you.* The one who left before dawn in the other boat. The one who hasn't come back.

### Event 102 <!-- id: myth-event-2f093ec90133, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T18:35:53.000000000, session: 1, involves: High Priestess Nerissa, Lost Isle -->

THE STAIR. Nerissa's Lore (Water spirits) 59/80 -- she has it. Lore (Theology) 96/88 -- she doesn't. "It's a door. Not for people. For the drowned."

She can tell you exactly what it *does*. She cannot tell you what it *means*, and you watch that bother her more than the corpse in the water.

"It's a door." She traces the carving with two fingers, following it down, and her hand goes under the surface and keeps going.

"Not for people. For the drowned. There are -- there are old stories about the Northern Isles going under. Not one flood, a slow one, over generations, and everything in them that died went down with the streets. And whoever cut this cut it so that what stayed in the water had somewhere to *go*. A way out. **You lay them to rest through a thing like this.**"

She looks up, and there's something urgent in it. "It isn't a Lady-stone. It's older than her presence in this channel and it isn't in any doctrine I've read in forty years, and I could not tell you who cut it or to what."

Then she stands, and holds the lamp out over the cleared patch. And you see that it isn't a slab at all.

It's the top of a **stair**. Four steps showing, cut into the same worked stone, going down at an angle and disappearing into black water that does not reflect the lamp the way the rest of the channel does. Weed hangs off the edges where they've hacked it away. The steps are *swept* -- there's no silt on them, and there should be a foot of it.

Something has been using them.

Behind them, roped and sitting in the shallows, Sandy says pleasantly: "That's what he went to fetch the boat for. You'd best hurry -- he's been gone since before dawn, and he'll not be coming back alone."

### Event 103 <!-- id: myth-event-b9ca121bdc30, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T19:00:59.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa -->
