"They're raising the drowned, just like our two chatty friends here." Nerissa: "They're not raising them, Magda. They're *shopping*."

"They don't bleed. They don't breathe. Ruhi had that one under for a good half-minute and it did nothing to him. They talk like one man with two mouths. And her *Hold Person* took. Gardwen said it shouldn't. There's enough person left in him to grip and not enough to bleed."

"And there is a door for the drowned dead under this island," Nerissa says slowly, "which they have been digging out for a day."

She looks back at the swept stair going down into water that doesn't hold the lamplight.

"They're not raising them, Magda. **They're *shopping*.**"

### Event 104 <!-- id: myth-event-5e5d40344fa9, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T19:04:32.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Hollow man (sandy) -->

ELLICK SARN. "They came to me in the winter... And then on the fourth time he said, would I like to not be cold any more."

He has to think about his own name. That's the unsettling part.

"Ellick," he says at last. "Ellick Sarn. That's -- yes. That's right, I'm fairly sure. I poled the Forge Quarter run. Timber mostly, sometimes charcoal, and a woman called Tam who I think was--" He stops. Frowns very slightly at the sky. "There was something about Tam. It's gone."

"They came to me in the winter. Not the young lord -- the *quiet* one, him that's gone for the boat. He asked did I know the Northern Isles and I said better than any man breathing, which was true, and he paid me eleven marks to take him out to the drowned streets four times and say nothing. I did say nothing. I want that noted. I kept my end.

"And then on the fourth time he said, would I like to not be cold any more."

He says it perfectly evenly.

"I've been cold on that water for thirty-one years, missus. Every winter. Hands gone the colour of the sky. And he said it like he was offering me a coat." His head tips a little. "And I said yes. **That's the part everyone wants to skip past. Nobody did anything to me that I didn't stand still for.**"

Sana has both hands over her mouth. "What went in you?"

"Something out of the stairs. It's not unkind. It doesn't want anything much. It likes the digging. It's got a lot more of me than it had at Candlemas. That's the trade -- it warms you and it *wears* you, and you don't notice the wearing because the part that would notice is the part it's wearing."

Then, conversationally: "You'll be wanting to know about the one on the ship. That's a different thing entirely. That one's *furnished*."

### Event 105 <!-- id: myth-event-c0fee886cdd4, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T19:04:59.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Hollow man (sandy) -->

"What are you fuckers up to?" -- the door is for taking something OUT. "You can't keep a thing in a boy for ever." And Sana: "The girls with the red hair aren't for the demon. They're spares."

"The quiet one wants the door open. That's the whole of the digging. Four days, and it wants to be open before the Tourney and not after -- he was very hard about that. There's a tide thing in it. He said something about the water being *thin* on the day and it having to be while it's thin."

Nerissa's head comes round. "He said *thin*?" -- "His word."

"And the door's for taking something *out*. That's the bit I did understand, because I asked him -- I said is this for putting things in, like you did with me, and he laughed. Only time I saw him laugh. He said no, Ellick, that's the easy way and any fool with a knife can do that. He said the hard thing is getting one *out* whole and putting it somewhere better.

"Because you can't keep a thing in a boy for ever. He said that too. They wear through. He said the one they've got has nearly used up what it was put in."

He turns his head and looks straight at Gardwen for the first time.

"**That's why they came here. It's not a rescue they're worried about, love. It's a *removal*. He's outgrown the boy and they've somewhere better to put him.**"

And Sana, very quietly, from the far thwart:

"...The girls with the red hair aren't for the demon. **They're spares.**"

### Event 106 <!-- id: myth-event-859688f2608b, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T19:07:36.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Thuban Eta -->

THE NAME. "That one *fights*." Two voices out of one throat, arguing for hours. "You bleed a man to keep him from arguing." Thuban. Thuban Eta.

"Mine's *content*, see. It got a boatman who was cold and it's warm now and it likes lifting stone. There's no fight in it. That's why I'm still able to sit here and talk to you about Tam and nearly remember her."

He nods north-west, toward where the black hull sits steaming at its pier.

"That one *fights*."

Ash has stopped rowing. Nobody tells him to start.

"I was aboard twice, carrying. You can hear it through the deck. Not screaming -- worse than that, it's *arguing*. Goes on for hours. Two voices out of one throat having a row, and one of them's a boy and one of them isn't, and the one that isn't sounds like a man who's been told to do something he thinks is dishonourable." He shakes his head slowly. "That's what the morning cut's for. It's not just the wear. It's to keep it *tired*. **You bleed a man to keep him from arguing.**

"They've a name for it. Old-sounding. The quiet one says it like a title, not a name." He works his jaw. "Thoo-- *Thuban*. Thuban something. Eta, maybe. He's a knight out of the dragon wars and he's been in one thing or another for a very long while, and he doesn't want to be, and he never has."

He looks around the boat at all of them.

"You keep saying *rescue the boy*. I'd think about that. **There's two in there, and only one of them's been asking to get out.**"

### Event 107 <!-- id: myth-event-ecd94ccc5806, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T19:11:16.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, High Priestess Nerissa -->

"That's the dead knight. That's Hel's command." Nerissa: "She showed you the *passenger*. It is not a rescue. It's a *funeral*."

Nobody rows. The boat drifts.

Nerissa says it first, slowly, the way you'd read something off a wall. "Eleven nights. A knight in dragon-scale who is already dead and does not know it." She turns to look at her. "And *lay the dragon warrior to rest*. Not the boy. She never once showed you the boy."

"No."

"She showed you the **passenger**." Nerissa's hand has gone to her breast. "Magda -- your goddess has been telling you the answer since before you left home and none of us could hear it because we were all looking at the child."

Gardwen has gone absolutely still in the bow. "Say the rest of it," she says. "Say it out loud so I've heard it."

Nerissa does.

"If Thuban Eta is laid to rest, he leaves. And whatever is left in that body is your brother, and only your brother." A pause. "**It is not a rescue. It's a *funeral*. And the boy walks out of it because the funeral wasn't for him.**"

Ellick, helpfully, into the silence: "That's what the door's for, mind. Same door. Only the quiet one wants him lifted *out and moved*, and you want him lifted out and let *go*. You'll be wanting the same night and the same stair as him, then. And the same thin water."

### Event 108 <!-- id: myth-event-8a8d48764e1f, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T19:23:25.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, High Priestess Nerissa -->

"What are you talking about? Makes no sense to me." Nerissa stops using her temple voice and explains it on three fingers.

"Right. Plainly.

"There is a dead man stuffed inside Gardwen's brother. Has been for years. He's the thing that makes the boy a champion -- he's the swordsman, not the child.

"He's worn the boy out. You can't keep meat in the same barrel for ever, it goes. So the Baron needs him **out** and into something fresher before he ruins the one he's got.

"There's exactly one way to get a dead man out of a living body whole. That stair on Lost Isle. It's a door. That's what those two have been digging for four days."

She drops her hand.

"So the Baron's man opens the door, takes the knight *out* of your girl's brother, and shifts him into a new body he's picked out. Probably one of the four girls whose hair was in that boy's coat. That's a **removal**, and it's the day after tomorrow because that's when the water goes thin enough to do it.

"Now. Your goddess wants the same door, the same night, the same knight -- and she wants him taken out and *put in the ground* instead of moved house.

"**It's the same job, Magda. Right up until the last moment, it's the *identical* job.** Get the dead man out. The only difference is what happens to him in the second after that, and whether there's a new girl standing there waiting to be filled."

Gardwen, from the bow, without turning round: "And either way my brother comes out of it empty. Alive, and empty, and his own."

Ellick nods along, agreeable as anything. "Told you it was a funeral."

### Event 109 <!-- id: myth-event-bb3422142927, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T20:24:19.000000000, session: 1, involves: High Priestess Nerissa -->

"Do we hold the door, or break it?" Nerissa consults the Lady. Lore (Theology) 38/88 -- she gets an answer, and it isn't the one she wanted. "She says the door isn't hers."

She puts both hands over the side into the channel and stops moving for a long moment, and the water round the boat goes glassy in a way that has nothing to do with the wind.

Then she takes her hands out and looks at them.

"She says the door isn't hers."

"Meaning?"

"Meaning she won't tell me to break it, and she won't tell me to hold it." Nerissa's voice is tight. "It was cut before her. It's not in her gift. What she *will* say -- is that the water goes thin tomorrow whether we like it or not, and that a thing left in the wrong place too long spoils what's around it. Which is not an instruction. **That is a woman telling me the milk's turning and leaving me to deal with it.**"

She snorts, and it's very like Magda's snort.

"Fine. Then we do it your way, and I'll answer for it later."

### Event 110 <!-- id: myth-event-5fba5060b6ff, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T20:24:41.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa, Prince Emeric -->

MAGDA'S BEST IDEA OF THE NIGHT: "Maybe you can ask the prince if he wouldn't mind." Nerissa: "I have been so angry with that boy for a day and a half that it did not once occur to me to *use* him."

She stops with one foot on the water-stair.

"...Say that again."

"Ask the prince. He's the one signed the paper. Ask him to unsign it."

She comes back down the step.

"Magda, I have been so angry with that boy for a day and a half that it did not once occur to me to *use* him." She says it like a woman finding a tool she'd thrown across the room. "And he's the only person in Purewater who can make any of this **lawful**.

"With a Governor's warrant, holding Lost Isle isn't twenty armed men on a rock -- it's the *Watch* posting a cordon, and nobody has to explain themselves. And going aboard that ship isn't a raid, it's a **search**, and eight Dragon Knights at a pier cannot lawfully refuse it in a treaty port without their master forfeiting the Tourney entry on the spot."

Gardwen: "And if he says no?"

"Then he's told the Baron by noon and we've lost surprise and the day. That's the wager. He's twenty-four, he's frightened of his council, and he signed that instruction because three grown men told him it was the polite thing to do. He is not wicked. He is *weak*, and weak men can be moved -- the question is who gets to him last.

"Although. I've a niece with shears-cut hair, a ring with a worm on it, and an old woman who'll say the word *pigs* to his face.

"He holds audience at the ninth bell. That's four hours. Do we spend them on him -- or on getting to that ship before the quiet one gets back?"

### Event 111 <!-- id: myth-event-027b704d6b75, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T20:28:39.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa, Lilura Deepcurrent -->

"Action. We go now. I'm not good at waiting or asking permission." Nerissa doesn't argue -- she splits it, and sends Lilura to the Prince with Sana and the ring.

"Then I'll do both. **Lilura** goes to the Prince -- she's temple, she's presentable, and she can hold a room for an hour without saying anything actionable. She'll carry the ring and Sana. If he moves, the warrant follows us out to the pier. If he doesn't, we were going anyway."

Sana starts to object about being used and Nerissa says "you *will*" and that's the end of it.

### Event 112 <!-- id: myth-event-a54fe117b401, type: gm-note, at: 2026-09-06T20:31:13.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda -->

PLAYER AUDITS HER OWN SHEET: "What's the heaviest armor I can wear and still use Artful Dodger?" ENC under 26. And the GM finally surfaces Berserk Rage, unused all night.

**Artful Dodger -- 1 extra action for parry/evade if ENC < 26.** Current load 12. Thirteen points of headroom. Hauberk, coif, kettle helm, axe and shield lands around 20-22 -- comfortably under. She can take the lot and keep the extra reaction.

And two things on that sheet that had not been used in a day and a half of play:

**Berserk Rage** -- 14 rounds. Damage modifier +1d2 and the weapon counts one size step larger. Half damage taken. Immune to serious wounds and fatigue. Endurance, Willpower and Brawn all one grade easier. Parry and evade one grade *harder*. Cannot retreat, must always engage. Ends with an automatic Fatigue level.

**Eyes in the Back of Your Head** -- attacked from behind, a Formidable Perception check lets her turn for 1 AP.

That's what a berserker *is*, mechanically, and she had fought two nights without it.

### Event 113 <!-- id: myth-event-9f2dbd5e1c3b, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T20:35:06.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa, Temple Isle -->

The temple armoury. Mail hauberk, coif, kettle helm, and Sword-Captain Vell's war axe. "She's twenty years dead and she'd have liked you."

"Take it. Argue with me about the cost afterwards."

The mail goes on over the wet wool and it is cold and it is heavy and it settles onto her shoulders like a hand. The coif flattens her hair. The kettle helm sits low and takes the top half of the world away, which is the trade you make for keeping the top half of your head.

Nerissa watches her do up the last buckle and says nothing at all until she picks the axe up.

Then: "That was Sword-Captain Vell's. She's twenty years dead and she'd have liked you."

*(And that is the battleaxe question from the very first hour of the session -- "can you see what the damage is of a battleaxe" -- finally answered on a character sheet rather than in a rules index. 1d8+1, size M, Bleed and Sunder. ENC 17. Artful Dodger holds.)*

### Event 114 <!-- id: myth-event-92d460432251, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T20:51:20.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa -->

"We used to use axes, in my tribe when I was a young woman." -- "Somebody's had that back to a smith for a new eye and kept the old head." -- "Twice. Her daughter has the receipts."

"Tell me about the tribe's axes."

"Plainer. Ash haft, a wedge of iron, and you sharpened it on the same stone you did the scythes." She turns Vell's over. The bearded edge catches the lamp. "Nothing like this. This has been *loved*. Somebody's had that back to a smith for a new eye and kept the old head."

"Twice. Her daughter has the receipts."

"Then I'd probably have liked her too."

Nerissa almost smiles, and doesn't quite, and picks up her mace. "Let's go greet the day, then."

### Event 115 <!-- id: myth-event-aea423ecf3de, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T21:03:39.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, The DragonBarge -->

Dawn at the pier. Perception 45/70: SIX aboard, four ashore. TEN. And the ship is being made ready to leave. "They're not waiting for tomorrow."

Two more than yesterday -- so nobody left with the quiet one; he went alone and they've brought men *down* from the house on High Isle.

And they are not going about a normal morning.

The **covered gangway** is open because they're bringing things *up* from below, not down. Chests. A long flat case, two men to it. Something wrapped in oiled canvas that takes four.

Two men are working at the **stern mooring**, taking turns off the bollard -- not casting off, but reducing it to what one man could throw clear in a hurry.

A knight on the quarterdeck keeps going to the rail and looking **north-west round the point**, the way the ship's boat went. Four times while she watches.

The **barred shutter amidships is still barred**. Whatever's behind it hasn't been moved yet.

And the **furnace smoke** is the wrong colour. Not from the yards. It's coming off the ship, from a hatch aft, black and greasy, and there's a great deal more of it than yesterday.

Ann Yannick has reached the bollards. A knight is coming down the ramp with the bag. She's holding out her hand for it exactly as she has every morning, and she has not looked once at the yards.

Nerissa, at her shoulder, very quietly: "They're not waiting for tomorrow."

### Event 116 <!-- id: myth-event-b461b08dc738, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T21:25:01.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa -->

"How many men do we have now? Not enough to take them, I'll wager." -- "Five and a dog. If we go up that ramp we die on it."

Nerissa does the sum out loud, flatly, because there's no point in dressing it.

"You. Gardwen. The wolf. Corvin, Ash, me. **Five and a dog** -- and of the five, two of us are past sixty and one of us is a priest."

"Against?"

"Ten. In scale. On a deck, above us, with a ramp they can hold two abreast. If we go up that ramp we die on it. All of us, in about a minute, and the ship leaves anyway with your girl's brother in the hold."

Corvin, who is twenty-two and brave and would go anyway, says nothing and grips his spear tighter.

"Reinforcement. Temple Isle is forty minutes there and forty back with a boat that isn't loaded -- and I'd get you a dozen Swords. Marisette can raise more than that but she's an hour away and it'd be another hour arming them. Crowbill's faster and he'd want paying in something I haven't got.

"And that ship is being got ready *now*. She could be off that pier inside the hour and out through the Mouth on the ebb, and then it's a border tower we can't reach and a boy we never see again."

She lets it sit exactly one beat. "So no. Not enough to take them. Not in time, not by force, not up that ramp."

Thirty yards away, Ann Yannick takes the bag off the knight at the bollards, hoists it onto the barrow, and turns it round. She has been past those men every morning for five days and not one of them has ever looked at her face.

### Event 117 <!-- id: myth-event-bb851aa299fc, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T22:58:25.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa -->

"Nah -- not today. Stupid to die for no reason." The berserker walks away. And asks for scouts instead.

The one decision in four days that is purely negative, and it is the reason there is a campaign left to run.

"Turns to Nerissa -- you got any scouts good enough to watch this monstrosity without getting killed?"

"Scouts." Nerissa considers it. "Not as such. The Swords are gate-guards and processional troops and I'd not send Corvin to follow a cat."

Then she looks at the steaming waterline, and something changes in her face. "But I don't need a scout to follow *that*."

She crouches at the edge of the yard and puts her hand flat in the harbour water, twenty yards from the slick.

"It hurts the channel wherever it sits. That's what the fouling alarm was for -- that's the only reason I knew anything was wrong in this city at all. It doesn't stop. It can't. **Wherever that hull goes it leaves a line of thin water behind it and I can feel the shape of it for as far as the Lady's water runs**, which is the whole harbour, the Mouth, and a good way out to sea.

"So: I cannot tell you what's on her deck. I *can* tell you where she is, at any hour, day or night, without a single person going near her. And I'll know the moment she moves."

Gardwen, without turning round from the barred shutter: "And if she sails, she still has to come back."

"She does. There's one door, and it's under a rock we're sitting on, and they cannot do their business without it. Wherever that ship runs to, it runs back to **Lost Isle** before tomorrow's water goes thin."

### Event 118 <!-- id: myth-event-0e344664fa43, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T23:07:40.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Blau -->

"Is that the albino?" Perception -- a 1. CRITICAL. It's Blau, and he isn't watching for the boat. He's sweeping the yards, and he is not going to stop doing it.

White through the eyebrows, white to the lashes, no colour anywhere in the face -- and even at forty yards through Forge Quarter smoke you can see the thing Ann Yannick meant, which is that he doesn't stand like the others. The rest of them are soldiers waiting. He's a man doing arithmetic.

And on a 1 you get more than the face.

He isn't watching for the boat. He's watching the **yards** -- sweeping them, left to right, in the unhurried way of a man who has been told that somebody killed his young master and is now looking at every working figure between the cooperage and the rope-walk.

His eye goes over her position and does not stop, because five people behind a stack of barrel staves are five people behind a stack of barrel staves.

But he does it again about forty seconds later. And again after that. He is not going to stop doing it.

Nerissa follows her look and swears in a way that would surprise her congregation. "Then he is not aboard that ship this morning by accident. That's the man they send when something has gone wrong."

### Event 119 <!-- id: myth-event-1d74bb43aac1, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T23:08:00.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa -->

Magda winks that the next part will be beneath a priestess. Nerissa does not smile. "I silenced my own alarm because a boy sent me a letter. Do not tell me what is beneath me. Tell me where to be."

She looks at Magda standing there in her dead captain's mail with her dead captain's axe, and doesn't answer for a second.

"Keep it," she says. "Bring it back when you leave the city, or don't."

And on the flippancy: "Magda. I silenced my own alarm because a boy sent me a letter." Her voice is perfectly level. "**Do not tell me what is beneath me. Tell me where to be.**"

"Fair point milady. Sorry for the flippancy."

She accepts it with a nod and no fuss -- which is its own kind of grace. "Then we understand each other."

She's already moving, and giving orders as she goes, and none of them are to Magda. Then at the boat she turns back.

"And Magda. When you know where to be, send to the temple. Not a message. A *name*. Whoever carries it should be able to say a name I'll recognise and nothing else, in case they're taken."

"What name?"

She thinks for a moment. "**Vell.**"

### Event 120 <!-- id: myth-event-828c41d2cea9, type: scene, at: 2026-09-06T23:12:07.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Thorne, The Moist Oyster -->

The Moist Oyster. Thorne reads two nights off her before she has said a word -- one of them wet -- from the weed dried on her boot.

He's about seventy, dressed like a man who was once dressed better, with a cup in front of him that he isn't drinking and a stub of chalk he's been turning over in his fingers.

He watches her cross the room. He watches the wolf. He watches Gardwen decide where to stand. Then he moves his cup six inches to the left, which is an invitation, and says:

"Sit down, mother. You've had a night." She hasn't said a word yet. "Two nights, at a guess." He looks at the mail under the cloak, and the mud on the hem, and the particular grey-green weed dried on her boot that grows in exactly one place in this city. "One of them wet. One of them worse.

"I'll tell you what I've had, since you're wondering. I've had a girl in here at seven this morning saying Constantine's house has a physician in it and won't say why. I've had a lad off Renko's cart telling anyone who'll listen that some old woman knows about horses. And about an hour ago I had two of the Baron's people asking whether anybody's seen a big northern woman with a face like a spade."

He finally looks up. "I said no. I'd like it noted that I said no *before* I knew what I was saying no about, which is the sort of thing I'll want paying for eventually."

### Event 121 <!-- id: myth-event-04aab1486d0d, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T23:23:25.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Thorne, The Moist Oyster -->

"How about I pay you a bit more and you tell him yes?" Thorne: "I don't sell lies, mother. I sell *stories*. A lie collapses. A story goes and gets *itself* believed."

"Not him." He is precise about it. "Two of his -- house servants in di Teufel livery, polite, one of them doing the talking and one of them looking at the room. No iron. Nobody walks into a dockside house in scale unless they want a riot, and that lot are being *careful*, which is more worrying than the alternative."

Then he sits back, and for the first time there's something sharp under the tiredness.

"Now. You've just offered to pay me to tell a man a lie. I want you to understand what you're buying, because people usually don't.

"I don't sell lies, mother. I sell *stories*, and the difference is that a story has to hold up when it's repeated by an idiot in a different tavern three days later. You can't just hand me a falsehood and a coin. You have to give me something that has the *shape* of true.

"**A lie collapses. A story goes and gets *itself* believed.**

"Two conditions, and they're not negotiable. **One** -- I'll not put a name on anybody who'd hang for it. If your story wants a body, it uses a dead one or a made-up one, never a living neighbour. **Two** -- I'm told what it's *for*. Not everything. But enough that if it goes wrong I know whether I ought to be sorry.

"Meet those and my price is small and strange: I want the *true* version. Afterwards. All of it, in order, told to me once and never written down.

"So. What do you want Blau to hear?"

### Event 122 <!-- id: myth-event-5733bd00bf8b, type: decision, at: 2026-09-06T23:37:16.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Thorne, The Moist Oyster -->

MAGDA HANDS HIM THE TRUE ONE FIRST. Thorne: "You've just done the thing nobody ever does." Then he shows her the craft: everything stays true, and the stone comes out of nowhere.

"Let's just you and me tell some tales. I have a feeling you'll figure out how you feel about the good Baron soon enough. I'm not good at lies but I love a good tale. How about this one -- a randy young nobleman carves up a whore to possess her with some kind of lust demon, gets the charm right but botches his escape only to fall in the canal and catch a slingshot to the temple..."

Thorne goes very still while she talks. When she finishes he doesn't say anything for a while. He turns the chalk over three times.

"You've just done the thing nobody ever does," he says at last. "You've handed me the *true* one first." He almost looks offended by how well it works.

"And it's better than anything I'd have built. Because every part of it is checkable. There *is* a physician at Constantine's. There *are* four houses on the Pearl that won't take di Teufel coin by tonight once the Pearl hears why. There *was* a young lord in that house on the night, and there are eleven people who saw him come in and none who saw him leave. That's not a story I have to sell. That's a story that sells itself house to house and comes back to me by Thursday with improvements."

Then he holds up a finger. "But you've a problem in it and you've walked straight past it. The truest version has an old northern woman with a sling in it. And there is a man on a ship asking after an old northern woman with a face like a spade.

"So here's the craft of it. Everything you told me stays *exactly as it is*. And **the stone comes out of nowhere.** He fell in the canal and *the water took him*, and the Lady's channel does not care for demon-work. That's not even a lie, is it. That's a *sermon*. Half this city will believe it before I've finished saying it, and the other half will believe it harder because the temple hasn't denied it."

He finally drinks. "As for how I feel about the good Baron. I was Order of the Lake for thirty-one years, mother. I know what a stair under Lost Isle is for. You didn't mention one. You didn't have to -- you've got weed on your boot that only grows in the drowned streets, and you came in here asking about the dead. Tell me I'm wrong."

### Event 123 <!-- id: myth-event-e3559722bdd5, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-06T23:47:16.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Thorne, Thuban Eta, The Moist Oyster -->

THUBAN ETA IS A MUSTER-ROLL NAME. Thorne's Lore (Dragon Kings era) 32/92 -- he has it cold. He wouldn't burn a town, so his own company drowned him for it.

At the name he stops. "Say that again." -- "Thuban Eta. The knight in the boy." He puts the chalk down.

"Thuban Eta was real. That's not a story name. That's a *muster roll* name. The Dragon Kings had knight-companies, and the Eta were one of them -- the letter's a company mark, not a family. And Thuban was the one they teach you about at the Order in your second year, because of how he died.

"He wouldn't burn a town. That's the whole of it. The company was ordered to fire a valley and he refused, and argued it in front of the others, and about a third of them came round to him. So the ones who hadn't took him out on the water at night to talk it over, and they put him over the side in his mail with his hands tied.

"**Drowned. By his own company. For refusing an order that even *they* thought was filthy** -- that's the part that damns them. They knew he was right and they killed him for making them know it."

He looks up. "And that is *exactly* the kind of dead man you can bind. You don't hold a shade with iron, mother. You hold it with the thing it can't put down. You keep him in the moment of it. Tied hands and cold water and his own men's faces looking down at him -- you keep him *there*, and he'll pull against it for a hundred years and never once get out, and every time he pulls you can use the pulling."

Gardwen, very low: "That's what's in my brother."

"That's what's in your brother. And it has been for ten years, and it has hated every hour of it, because a man who wouldn't fire a valley is not going to enjoy being made into a champion."

He pushes the chalk away. "Your goddess said *lay him to rest*, and everyone at this table has been thinking that means a door and a ritual. It might not. You don't lay that man down with a stair. **You lay him down by *undoing the betrayal*** -- by someone standing in front of him and taking it back on behalf of the ones who did it. The door gets him out of the boy. Something else has to make him *willing to go*."

### Event 124 <!-- id: myth-event-f0986cca34fc, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T00:01:37.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Thorne, The Moist Oyster -->

THE TURN OF THE WHOLE CAMPAIGN. "That was a thousand years ago. Who the fuck could do that now?" -- "It never wanted the men. What holds him is that *nobody ever came back for him*."

"Thorne... the kings themselves and all their armies were laid to rest. Every child knows that. Look at Magda -- what the fuck girl? I don't know what we do now. You know these ways -- what do you say?"

Thorne doesn't argue. He nods along. "You're right. There's no company left to answer for it and no descendants worth the name. If it wants the *actual men*, it's impossible and we may as well go home."

He holds up a finger. "But that's not how a debt like that works. It never wanted the men. It's been a thousand years -- Thuban knows they're dust. What holds him isn't a grudge against nine specific bastards. It's that **nobody ever came back for him.** He went over the side and the water closed and that was the end, and no company, no order, no god, no sister -- *nobody* has stood over that water since and said 'this was wrong, and he was ours, and we're taking him home.'"

Magda turns to Gardwen. She's been quiet a long while, and when she speaks it's slow, and it's not the voice she uses for people -- it's the one she used at the ford, with her hands in the water.

"There's a thing we do in the wood, for a beast that's died badly. In a snare, or under a fall, where it couldn't get free. You don't pray it away. You go and you *sit* with it, and you say out loud that it wasn't right, and then you carry it. That's the whole rite. **Somebody has to actually pick the thing up.**

"It doesn't need the ones who did it, Magda. It needs somebody willing to *stand where they stood* and answer for it. Not because it's theirs -- because they're the one who turned up."

Then Thorne, quietly, in the tone of a man laying a card down: "And who exactly do we know that serves the god of the ones nobody sings about?

"Not the battle-dead, you said. The other kind. A knight drowned in the dark by his own friends for refusing to burn a village, and left out of every song since, in every land, for a thousand years.

"**Mother -- whose *is* he, if he isn't hers?**"

### Event 125 <!-- id: myth-event-58d18203b7c7, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T00:07:15.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda -->

"Yeah!!!! I start to get it. That's what she'd want isn't it? Fuck me. Hel. The old lady has her ways. OK. I'll do it."

Nobody says anything for a moment. Thorne picks up his cup and finally drinks from it, properly, like a man closing a ledger.

"Well," he says. "There it is."

Gardwen has both hands flat on the table. "Then it's three things and we've got one of them.

"**The door.** We have that. **The boy at the door.** We don't have that -- he's behind a barred shutter on a ship that's getting ready to sail, with ten men and the albino on it. That's the whole problem and nothing about it has changed.

"**And you.** Stood over the water saying it out loud and meaning it. Which costs you something, Magda, and I don't know what, and neither do you."

Thorne, without looking up from his cup: "It'll cost her exactly what she's got. That's how those go."

### Event 126 <!-- id: myth-event-9e60ba85fa0d, type: scene, at: 2026-09-07T00:10:04.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Thorne, The Moist Oyster -->

THE BERSERKER SALUTE. Magda takes off her helm, leans across the cup, and headbutts a seventy-year-old storyteller. Gently. For her.

There's still an audible knock.

Thorne sits back with his eyes watering slightly and a hand going to his brow, and he does not look at all displeased.

"*Ohh*," he says. "That's a northern thing." -- "That's a northern thing." -- "I've read about that." He works his jaw. "It reads considerably better than it feels."

The girl mopping has stopped mopping. The dog under the table has reconsidered its position entirely. Gardwen has put her face in her hand.

Thorne straightens his collar with enormous dignity. "Thirty-one years in the Order, two of them at the Governor's table, and no one has ever done that to me. I've been given a chain of office and a commendation from a Prince and neither of them landed like that."

And as she's standing, without looking up: "Mother. When they ask me this morning -- and they will -- I'm going to say I've never seen you. That's on the house."

### Event 127 <!-- id: myth-event-7baf545eb6d6, type: scene, at: 2026-09-07T00:13:35.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Marisette, Prince Emeric -->

Lilura has been in the Prince's anteroom for four hours. Vosk the chamberlain will not let her past. There will be no warrant.

"Ash came at seven. I have nine people in those yards already and two on the water. The Pearl closed to di Teufel coin at eight -- I didn't even have to ask, Corliss's house did it first and the rest followed inside the hour. Your storyteller's version arrived here about twenty minutes before you did, which I'd call good work."

"The Prince?"

Now she does look up. "Lilura is *still in the anteroom*. Four hours. They've given her tea twice. Emeric's council have him in session about the Tourney seating and she cannot get past a chamberlain called Vosk, who I have twice paid and who is therefore either bought harder by someone else or genuinely afraid.

"So no warrant. Not by noon and probably not at all before tomorrow, which means whatever you do at that pier you do outside the law."

### Event 128 <!-- id: myth-event-44f07c99d2bc, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T00:22:49.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Crowbill, Marisette -->

THE OFFER: "We steal the boy. He steals everything else." Marisette: "*Yes.* That's the right shape." -- and two corrections that save the plan.

"You're not asking him for help -- you're bringing him a *job* and taking the least valuable thing off it. That's a trade he understands before you've finished the sentence." She taps the desk. "And better than you know: if there are forty men on that pier tonight and every one of them is carrying a chest off, then nobody watching from the yards sees a rescue. They see a **robbery**. Which is the most ordinary thing that can happen on a dock, and the one thing nobody investigates too hard when the victim is a foreign lord who's just been disgraced."

Then she holds up a finger. "Two corrections.

"**Don't give him the ring.** That ring is a demon-worker's seal on a dead heir's hand and it's the single piece of evidence that makes Emeric's chamberlain step aside. The moment it's in Crowbill's strongbox it's a *trade good*, and he will sell it to whoever pays -- possibly to the Baron. Offer him first pick of the cargo. Offer him the *whole* cargo. Never the evidence.

"**And don't say 'once he's disgraced.' Crowbill doesn't buy futures.** Tell him the ship is loading to sail tonight and that after tonight everything on it is out of this city for ever. He is a man who responds very well to *last chance*."

### Event 129 <!-- id: myth-event-641c8f357433, type: scene, at: 2026-09-07T01:11:11.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Crowbill -->

Crowbill: "Every day since it tied up." Three good reasons the DragonBarge is still sitting there with everything on it -- and he assumes she has an answer to all three.

"That's not a boast, that's my *trade*. A foreign lord brings a deep-water hull into a canal city and moors it at a private pier and lets nobody aboard -- of course I've thought about what's on it. So has every fence between here and the Shoals.

"And here's why it's still sat there with everything on it.

"**One**: ten men in scale and one way up. **Two**: it's a *ship*, so if it goes badly they don't have to win, they just have to cast off, and then I've lost people for nothing. **Three** --" he holds up the third finger and looks at her over it "-- a Baron. Not a merchant, not a guild. A man with a border tower and three hundred men and a long memory, who would spend two years and a lot of money finding out who did it, because that's what men like him are *for*."

He sits back. "Which is three good reasons, and you didn't come here without knowing them. So you've got something that answers all three, or you wouldn't have got past my door.

"So let's not do the part where you sell it to me. Just tell me the two things I actually need. **When is she sailing?** And **what do you want off her that isn't cargo?**"

### Event 130 <!-- id: myth-event-7e27eae049b1, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-07T01:14:26.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Crowbill -->

Crowbill's Insight 76 against 75 -- he misses by ONE. For the first time in that room he cannot tell what she is. "Which I don't enjoy."

He puts the bread down. "Tonight." He says it to himself, not to her. Then: "And you want the *champion*."

He's quiet for a good five seconds, and you can hear a clerk's pen scratching in the next room.

"See, now, that's a strange thing to want, and I can usually tell when someone's shading me and I can't tell with you, which I don't enjoy." He taps the desk twice with one knuckle -- the crow moves. "Because the cargo I understand. Cargo is cargo. But the fighting man is the one item on that ship the Baron will actually *chase*. Gold he replaces. A champion the day before the Lady's Tourney? That's him publicly beaten in front of every Guild in this city.

"So you're not asking me to rob a lord. You're asking me to *humiliate* one, and put my name on it, in exchange for goods I'd have to fence quietly for a year."

Then he tells her which version of her two days is running fastest, and it ends: "And the Baron's entered a champion that not one soul in Purewater has been allowed to look at. That last part's new. That went out this morning and it's *everywhere*, and it's the bit the Guildmasters are chewing on."

His eyes come up. "So here's my question, and I want the true answer, because I'm not putting forty men on a pier for a story. **What's wrong with him?** Nobody hides a swordsman. You hide a thing that would *lose you everything* if it were seen."

### Event 131 <!-- id: myth-event-4d3524130739, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T01:17:34.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Crowbill -->

"It's not a humiliation. It's a rescue." And Crowbill goes to the window and tells her the one rule of his that isn't about money.

"The champion's the ghost of a fallen general from a thousand years ago contained in the body of this woman's nineteen year old brother. I serve Hel and she wants the ledger set right and the ghost released. That clear enough of a read for you?"

"Seventeen," Gardwen says quietly.

Crowbill looks at her, and then back at Magda, and doesn't say anything for a while. Then: "A boy with a dead general in him." -- "Aye." -- "Being kept on a ship for it." -- "Bled every morning to keep him quiet. There's a laundress on that pier who's been boiling the sheets for five days and can tell you the shape of the cut."

He stands. He goes to the window, which looks at a wall, and stands there with his hands behind his back.

"I've a rule," he says to the wall. "I've a few. Most of them are about money and one of them isn't, and the one that isn't is why I've still got a city to work in.

"**Children aren't stock. You don't sell them, you don't use them, and you don't let anyone do it on my water and call it business.**" He turns round. "Your Marisette knows that rule. It's why we get on."

"So. I'd have done it for the cargo anyway. Now I'll do it *properly*, which is different, and here are my terms -- and they're not money.

"**One.** Everything on that ship that isn't the boy is mine, and nobody counts it but me.
"**Two.** My name is nowhere. Tonight was *thieves*, and this city has plenty.
"**Three** -- and this is the one I care about." He puts one finger on the desk. "When it's done, the **High Priestess of the Lake Lady** stands up and says publicly that the canal folk kept her water clean. Not me. Not names. *The canal folk.* Because I have four hundred people who get moved on and blamed and dredged out of the channel, and one sentence from that woman is worth more to them than a Baron's whole hold.

"Can you get me that?"

### Event 132 <!-- id: myth-event-e23c43809ad7, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T01:21:08.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Crowbill, Purewater -->

"You're the river men, I'm just a pig farmer." TAKE HER OFF THE PIER. Crowbill's face becomes interested for the first time: "Nobody watches a wake."

"She has to leave to sail. Once she's off that pier she's not got four men at the bollards and four at a ramp -- she's just a hull in the dark with a channel round her, and the channel's yours."

"It is." He's already pulling a scrap of paper over. "And she cannot get out to sea without going through the **Mouth**, and the Mouth on the ebb is a mile of slow water with the harbour lights behind it, and I have men who have been going up the sides of anchored hulls in that stretch since they were nine years old." He's drawing. "You don't need forty. You need *twelve*, in six punts with no lights, and you take her from three sides at once while she's making way and everyone aboard is looking forward.

"And the best of it -- a ship under way has half her people at the ropes and the rest looking where she's *going*. **Nobody watches a wake.**"

Then he stops drawing. "One problem, and it's yours to solve, not mine."

He taps the waterline on his own sketch. "That hull *steams*. Whatever your Baron's got in her, it cooks the water where it touches, and there's a dead slick round her the whole way out. I've had a man put his hand in that water off the stern two days ago on a dare and he's still got no feeling in three fingers.

"My people go up the outside of hulls. They cannot climb *that* one -- not at the waterline, and that's where you have to start.

"So. Can anybody in your party make forty feet of that hull cold enough to touch for the four minutes it takes to get twelve men over the rail?"

### Event 133 <!-- id: myth-event-8d8e0137b5d5, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T01:24:00.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Crowbill, Ravella -->

Gardwen rules herself out flat, and then names the two who could -- and what each would cost. "She'd probably do it and it'd probably cost her more than she'd tell us."

"Not me. I do bark and bones and bleeding -- I've nothing that touches water and nothing that touches *heat*. I couldn't cool a kettle."

Then she thinks properly. "**Nerissa** could try. That's her channel and that hull is wounding it. But she told us straight this morning: the Lady wouldn't tell her whether to hold the door or break it. She's already gone past what she's sure of once today. She'd be doing it from a boat, in the dark, in a moving channel, against something that has been fouling her water for five days.

"Meaning she'd probably do it and it'd probably cost her more than she'd tell us."

She glances at Crowbill and back. "And there's the other lot. The ones nobody's said out loud yet."

Crowbill, without looking up from his sketch: "The Court."

"The Mermaid's Court," Gardwen agrees. "Their chambers are *under Lost Isle*. **We've been standing on the roof of them all night and the only reason nothing came up those stairs at us is that nothing chose to.**"

Crowbill sets his pen down. "**Ravella.** Siren's Call. Half of one and half of us, and she brokers for them when they can be bothered to want anything. I've done business with her twice and both times I came off worse and knew it at the time." A shrug. "But you'd be asking the Court for a favour, mother. They don't take coin and they don't take gratitude. They take *arrangements*."

### Event 134 <!-- id: myth-event-6f84c5f68c8c, type: scene, at: 2026-09-07T01:57:01.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Ravella, The Siren's Call -->

Ravella's cup stops halfway to her mouth. "Let me tell you what 'thin' means from underneath, since nobody up here ever asks."

"There are people living in the deep channels of this city who were here before the causeway. Not many. They keep out of the way and they mind their own business and their whole lives are *water that behaves*. And for five days there has been a hot wound in the eastern channel that pushes ahead of the tide, and every one of them has had to move -- old, young, sick -- and two haven't managed it."

She says the last part very flatly. The canal outside the window has gone glassy in a strip about four feet wide.

"So yes. We mind."

Then she picks the cup up again and the broker comes back, which is somehow worse.

"But you'd have got that from a fishwife. Here's the part you'd have paid for. **They came down our stairs.**"

She lets that sit. "Under Lost Isle. Four days ago, three men with a lantern, digging out a door that has been left shut and unmarked since before this city had a name. And they *took* things off it -- the small confused ones that sit near the threshold and don't go through, which are ours to look after and nobody else's.

"The Court could not stop them. Won't say why and I've stopped asking. So we have had our water burned and our threshold robbed in the same week, and we have been able to do precisely nothing about either.

"And now an old woman walks into my house smelling of Lost Isle weed with a dead captain's axe. **How many of the taken have you seen?**"

### Event 135 <!-- id: myth-event-84ca91ca45d5, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T01:58:40.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Ravella, The Siren's Call -->

THE ARRANGEMENT: two dead men laid down properly, for one cold ship. "There isn't a version of this where a boatman walks home to his supper."

"Two." Ravella doesn't move. "Where." -- "One's dead. I put an axe through his head on Lost Isle last night. The other's roped in a cellar under the Lake Lady's temple with two Swords on the door, and he talks. Boatman. Ellick Sarn."

Something passes over her face at the name and she doesn't explain it. "Ellick. He poled the Forge Quarter run. He carried for us twice."

She's quiet a moment. "Then you already have both of them and you didn't know what you had. Here is the arrangement, and I'll speak for the Court because they'll agree and because there isn't time to go down and ask.

"**Give them back.**

"That's the whole price. Not the ship, not the Baron, not your boy -- those are your business and the Court doesn't care about a dead general or a barony. The two you've got are *ours*, and they need to go back through that threshold and be let go properly, and until they are they're neither ours nor dead nor alive, and that is an obscenity."

She looks at her very directly. "You should understand what you're agreeing to. The thing in Ellick Sarn came off our threshold and it goes back to it. What's left of Ellick is -- *thin*. **There isn't a version of this where a boatman walks home to his supper.** He said yes to a warm coat in a cold winter and he has been paying for it ever since, and the kindest end available to him is the right one.

"You'd be taking him to that stair and standing there while it's done." She turns her cup again. "And I understand that's your trade."

Then, briskly: "Do that, and I will come out to the Mouth myself tonight and put my hands on that hull and make it cold enough to climb. For as long as you need, not four minutes. And I will bring people who can be in the water beside it while it happens, which your Crowbill's boys cannot.

"Two dead men, laid down properly, for one cold ship. Do we have an arrangement?"

"Don't know where the second one is. But sure. I'll do what I can."

She takes her hand anyway. "You don't need to know where he is. He went into a drowned street off Lost Isle with an axe wound in his head and did not float. He's in our water. We'll have him on that threshold by dark and you won't have to look for him -- **you'll have to *stand* there, which is the part that matters.**

"And Magda -- when you're standing over those two tonight, say it out loud. Whatever your goddess's words are. The Court will be listening from underneath and they will want to hear that somebody up here said it."

### Event 136 <!-- id: myth-event-c8a2102d948d, type: scene, at: 2026-09-07T02:05:13.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Crowbill, High Priestess Nerissa -->

Nerissa's answer, in her own square hand, on a scrap the size of a palm: "That he had to buy it is a shame on my order, not on him."

> *Tell the crow he shall have his sentence -- from the temple steps, at the Tourney, in front of the Governor and every Guild in this city. **The canal folk kept the Lady's water clean.** Those words, that place, and I will not soften them.*
>
> *I would have said it years ago if anyone had thought to ask me. That he had to buy it is a shame on my order, not on him.*
>
> *-- V.*

The gondolier takes a second coin and goes off to Gallows Reach with the answer, and you get the impression he'd have carried that one for free.

### Event 137 <!-- id: myth-event-fca07a757b78, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T02:09:10.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Hesper -->

HESPER'S THIRTY HOURS: a removal needs three parts. The door, the thing it comes out of, and the ANCHOR. The locket is the latch. The bones are in the border tower.

"Before you ask -- yes, I'll come. Now listen, because I've spent thirty hours buying this a syllable at a time and it changes your night.

"**You cannot simply pull a shade out and let it go.** It has to have somewhere to be, even for a moment, or it goes into the nearest living thing standing over it -- which will be one of you.

"So a removal needs three parts. **The door.** The *thing it comes out of*. And the **anchor** -- something that already holds a piece of him, that he'll consent to sit in while he's between.

"The Baron carries a locket. It's the small anchor, the working one, and he's had it on his person since before they left the border. That's what keeps the daily ritual holding.

"But that's not the primary. And the thing in that room told me what is, this morning, while complaining about how much *nicer* it would be to have a proper one."

He takes a breath. "**They've got his bones.** Thuban's. In the Baron's border tower, where they've been for a hundred years, because you cannot hold a man like that with anything less."

### Event 138 <!-- id: myth-event-5d0e78f425b1, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T02:18:28.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Hesper -->

THE REVERSAL. "How the fuck are we going to do that?" -- and Hesper works out that the quiet one is thirty-six hours out and rowing the bones TO them.

Hesper doesn't laugh. He looks at her for a moment and then something happens behind his eyes.

"Wait. Say the bit about the quiet one again. The timing."

"Gone since before dawn yesterday. Ellick reckoned four hours out to the border tower and four back with a fair wind."

"And it's been thirty-six." His hands have come up. "So he is not *at* the tower. He has been there, and he is on his way *back*, and he is late because he is carrying something heavy and he will not risk it in bad water."

Gardwen gets there half a beat before Magda does. "**He's bringing the bones *here*.**"

"He has to. They cannot do a removal without the primary anchor and the primary anchor has sat in a border tower for a century because nobody dared move it. But they've run out of boy. So for the first time in a hundred years, Thuban Eta's bones are **out of the tower and on the water**, and they are coming to Purewater, and they are going to the one place the work can be done.

"Which is a stair under Lost Isle that you are already sitting on with a dozen Swords of the Lake."

Then, quietly, and it lands like a stone: "And Magda -- you were never going to be able to lay that man to rest without them. You know that. You've buried enough people."

"You don't put a man down without his bones."

"No. You don't." He almost smiles, and it's an awful smile. "**They have spent a hundred years keeping the one thing you need locked in a tower you could never reach. And tonight they are rowing it to you.**"

### Event 139 <!-- id: myth-event-1375d5ab443b, type: scene, at: 2026-09-07T02:21:21.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Crowbill, Hesper, Ravella, Thorne -->

CH.9 -- The day before the Tourney. Ellick Sarn talks: he sold himself for warmth, the binding on the ship is 'furnished' and FIGHTS, and the morning cut keeps it tired. The name is Thuban Eta. Thorne identifies him from the muster rolls -- drowned by his own company for refusing to fire a valley, held by the betrayal itself, and nobody ever came back for him. Magda accepts that Hel's command means SHE must stand as proxy. Crowbill takes the job for the cargo; Ravella will cool the steaming hull for the return of two taken men; Hesper deduces the quiet one has gone to fetch Thuban's bones -- and is rowing them to the very stair the party holds.

ELLICK: 'He said, would I like to not be cold any more... Nobody did anything to me that I didn't stand still for.'
ELLICK on the champion: 'That one FIGHTS. Two voices out of one throat having a row... You bleed a man to keep him from arguing.'
ELLICK: 'You keep saying rescue the boy. I'd think about that. There's two in there, and only one of them's been asking to get out.'
THORNE: 'You don't hold a shade with iron. You hold it with the thing it can't put down.'
THORNE: 'What holds him isn't a grudge against nine specific bastards. It's that NOBODY EVER CAME BACK FOR HIM.'
THORNE: 'Whose IS he, if he isn't hers?'
GARDWEN: 'It needs somebody willing to stand where they stood and answer for it... because they're the one who turned up.'
MAGDA: 'The old lady has her ways. OK. I'll do it.'
MAGDA to Crowbill: 'We steal the boy. He steals everything else.'
CROWBILL: 'Children aren't stock... you don't let anyone do it on my water and call it business.' Price: Nerissa to thank THE CANAL FOLK publicly from the temple steps.
RAVELLA: 'Give them back. That's the whole price.' -- and on Ellick: 'There isn't a version of this where a boatman walks home to his supper.'
NERISSA by note: 'I would have said it years ago if anyone had thought to ask me. That he had to buy it is a shame on my order, not on him.'
HESPER: 'They have spent a hundred years keeping the one thing you need locked in a tower you could never reach. And tonight they are rowing it to you.'
OFFSCREEN ROLLS: Blau Streetwise 82/66 and 87/66 -- failed twice to trace Magda; Lilura 93/55 -- never got past the chamberlain Vosk; Thorne's story 35/70 -- running well among the Guilds.

### Event 140 <!-- id: myth-event-33ffc5139deb, type: combat, at: 2026-09-07T02:21:21.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, High Priestess Nerissa, Ila -->

CH.8 -- Lost Isle. Gardwen's Detect Evil (Piety 46/44 failed from the bank; 46/69 succeeded holding Nerissa's hand in the channel, augmented by Love of Family) finds Sana alive and hiding in a tower-stub and two 'wrong' men below. Sana comes down on Magda's proof of an eleven-page letter about a heron. Magda kills one hollow man (sling to the arm, then a staff blow to the head after he had surrendered) and cripples the other; Gardwen's Hold Person grips him, which it should not. They take Ellick Sarn prisoner and uncover an ancient swept stair -- a door for laying the drowned to rest.

SANA: 'Say what she calls my mother.' MAGDA: 'I don't know but I know you sent her an eleven page letter about a heron.'
SANA on Bettan, 14, who cut her hair: 'She came back CHEERFUL... She stopped wearing her shawl. In February... And she started asking about people's families.'
HOLLOW MEN, finishing each other's sentences: 'You're not her.' '--she was meant to bring the girl.'
SANDY, arm shattered: 'That's going to make the poling difficult.' -- 'we'll manage.'
NERISSA, after Magda killed a man who had put his hands down: 'He had stopped, and he was showing me the stone.'
NERISSA, reading Magda (Insight CRITICAL 4/80): 'You're not deciding anything, are you... You're not cruel. You've simply not been given a reason to stop yet.' Then gave her one: Sana, watching from the boat.
NERISSA on the stair: 'It's a door. Not for people. For the drowned... You lay them to rest through a thing like this.'
MECHANICS: approach 38/60; Magda Stealth 20/68; sling 56/128 hit, 7 dmg right arm (-3, serious); staff 58/85, 6 dmg same arm (-9, MAJOR); underwater head blow 53/57 for 1 damage; Ruhi's Trip put both Magda and the hollow man under; Gardwen's Command (14/87 vs 89/42) broke the grip; killing blow 75/100, 8 dmg to head (-4). Rules correction mid-fight: ONE proactive action per round, remaining AP for reactions only.

### Event 141 <!-- id: myth-event-4255437a4c81, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T17:05:08.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Hesper, Lost Isle, Purewater -->

THE SPLIT: two things, two places, not at the same time. Magda picks the Mouth. "Get the boy off that ship *breathing*. I can work with almost anything else."

The DragonBarge is holding to the ebb. Blau has failed to find her a third time -- three days he's had the city's ear open and this city has told him nothing whatsoever. Thorne's *"never seen her,"* a fishmonger, forty people in a working yard, a customs man who wrote *dog* on his tally-board.

And the quiet one is not going to make the ebb. He's overdue by a day and carrying something he won't risk in bad water, which means **the bones arrive after the ship sails.**

- **The Mouth** -- Crowbill's twelve, Ravella's water-folk, a cold hull, and a boy behind a barred shutter.
- **Lost Isle** -- Nerissa, a dozen Swords, a swept stair, and at some point tonight a quiet man rowing in with a century of bones and no idea who's sitting on his doorstep.

"The Mouth. Definitely. Time to break some dragons."

Hesper takes it well. "Then I'll go to the stair. Nerissa will want telling what a removal actually needs, and somebody ought to be there when the quiet one arrives who knows what he's looking at."

He shoulders the bag. "Magda. Get the boy off that ship *breathing*. I can work with almost anything else."

### Event 142 <!-- id: myth-event-49b4d4733731, type: combat, at: 2026-09-07T18:04:20.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Ravella, Purewater -->

Ravella goes over the side and forty feet of hissing hull just STOPS, like a candle pinched out. 36/75. And 22 on the second roll -- somebody aboard feels it done.

"When I go in, that water will kill anyone who isn't mine for about a minute while it turns. After that it's cold and it's climbable and I can hold it as long as I'm conscious. So say when, and be sure. Because I only get to do this once tonight."

Magda lets the ship come. Two hundred yards. One-fifty. A hundred. Eighty. "**Now.**"

Ravella doesn't answer. She goes over the side without a splash and the water takes her like it was expecting her.

The water goes first. You don't see her do it. What you see is the white line along that hull *stop* -- forty feet of hissing steam just ceasing, mid-length, like a candle pinched out, and the dead-water slick behind it going dark and ordinary and cold.

Six punts move at once. Grapnels go up in sacking and land with a sound like a fist in a pillow. Canal men are on the lines before the hooks have set, going up the side of a moving ship barefoot with knives in their teeth, and it is the most casually competent thing Magda has ever watched anybody do.

But a ship that has hissed for five days and suddenly *doesn't* is a ship that has changed its noise -- and there's a man aft who has lived beside that sound long enough to notice its absence.

A voice on the quarterdeck. Not a shout. A question. Then a shout. Then a bell.

### Event 143 <!-- id: myth-event-af6c4b803ddd, type: combat, at: 2026-09-07T18:05:45.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Purewater -->

"Shoot anyone I see on deck with my sling." She's not going up that hull and she knows it. First stone misses (69/57). Second is a 1 -- CRITICAL, under the raised arm, and the body blocks the rail.

The first one leans out over the side with a knife, going for the grapnel lines -- which will drop three men into cold water and the ebb. The stone goes past his head close enough that he flinches back, which buys the line about three seconds and no more.

She's already loading the next one. That's the thing about a sling: you're never really out, you're only ever between.

**A 1. Critical.** He's turned inboard, half-lit by somebody's dropped lantern, side-on at forty feet -- and the stone takes him under the raised arm where the scale gapes. Three damage. It shouldn't be enough for anything. But a critical ignores armour, and a stone into the armpit at that range does what a stone does: it goes *in*.

He doesn't fall over the side. He folds sideways onto the rail with his arms over it, and the knife drops past her into the water, and he stays there hanging half over the edge with his legs on the deck -- **which is worse for everyone aboard than if he'd gone in**, because now the men behind him have to get past him and the whole rail is blocked at exactly the spot they were massing.

Two more canal men go up the line on the far side while that's happening.

### Event 144 <!-- id: myth-event-6e9665bc4786, type: combat, at: 2026-09-07T18:08:15.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Blau, Purewater -->

Blau opens a lantern shutter over the side to hunt for the boats, and Magda puts a stone through his gut from forty yards. 23/57, abdomen, no armour. He has never in his life expected to need any.

The bell stops. The shouting stops. It goes very quiet up there, very suddenly, which is the most frightening thing that has happened all night. Then a single lantern shutter opens on the quarterdeck and swings out over the side, throwing a cone of light down onto the black water -- and it sweeps along the hull, punt by punt. Looking for the boats. Looking for **her**.

The lantern makes him a target and he knows it and he does it anyway, which tells you something about him.

She hears it hit. Forty yards and dark and she *hears* it -- the flat thud of a stone going into a body that has nothing on it but wool, because the man does not wear armour, because he has apparently never in his life expected to need it.

The lantern drops. Not thrown -- *dropped*, out of a hand that stopped working, and it goes over the side and hisses out in the water and the quarterdeck goes black again.

Somewhere up there a man says something short with no breath behind it.

### Event 145 <!-- id: myth-event-a8f53ed5c5e7, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T18:09:17.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Purewater -->

"Start berserking as I climb. Use the luck." Berserk makes Brawn one grade easier -- which is what gets a seventy-year-old in mail up forty feet of wet line.

It comes up out of her on the rope. She's not let it since the woods, and it does not arrive like anger -- it arrives like *warmth*, like getting into a hot bath, everything narrowing to the next handhold and the noise above.

And it changes the roll. Hauling a mailed body up forty feet of wet line hand-over-hand isn't grace, it's **Brawn** -- and Berserk Rage makes Brawn one grade easier. Hard becomes Standard.

**95 against 71 -- failure. Luck Point: swap the digits. 95 becomes 59. She's up.**

*(And here the player caught a second sheet error: "I only get 3 luck. Check the rules -- they don't accumulate." POW 14 is the 13-18 band: 3 Luck Points, replenished each session, not banked. The 8 on the sheet came in from a Roll20 import and had been taken at face value. Corrected mid-climb. Luck: 2 of 3, and that is the whole night's allowance.)*

### Event 146 <!-- id: myth-event-2141052a43b4, type: combat, at: 2026-09-07T18:29:14.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Blau, Purewater -->

"Scream with all of Hel's fury at these violators, stealers of souls, rapists of the dead." It stops the fighting at the bow for a full second.

It isn't a war cry. It's older and uglier than that -- the sound you make over a byre full of dead stock in a bad winter, and there are words somewhere in the middle of it about **violators, stealers of souls, rapists of the dead**, and every canal man on that deck decides at once that whatever that is, it is on their side.

The man in the way has a sword and a shield. The axe comes over the top of it -- a size larger than it has any right to be, in hands that aren't feeling the weight -- and goes through the scale over his belly with a sound like someone standing on a crate. He sits down against the mast. His legs simply stop being interested.

Eight feet to Blau. He's still on one knee on the quarterdeck steps with her stone in his gut, and he has watched all of that happen, and his face has not changed.

"*That's* her. The northern woman." A pause, and something that might be a laugh with no air behind it. "**Nobody told me she was seventy.**"

### Event 147 <!-- id: myth-event-e13f8a45d068, type: combat, at: 2026-09-07T18:31:35.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Blau, Purewater -->

Both spears evaded -- and evading in Mythras puts you PRONE. Twice. Blau, not raising his voice: "Don't crowd her. She's on her back. Spears."

Berserk cuts both ways: her evade is one grade *harder*, and she takes half of everything that lands.

The spear comes in low and she isn't there -- she goes under it and inside, which at seventy involves a great deal more of the deck than she'd like. The sword comes down where she's landed and she rolls out of it, mail and all, and takes the edge of a hatch coaming in the ribs doing it.

Nothing has touched her. She's also flat on a wet deck in a hauberk with two men standing over her, and out of Action Points.

That's what evade costs. Parry keeps your feet.

And the pair of them step back a half-pace and level, which is exactly the right thing to do to a downed woman with an axe.

### Event 148 <!-- id: myth-event-b9e29fdd683d, type: combat, at: 2026-09-07T18:34:53.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Blau, Purewater -->

Blau comes down off the steps with a working knife and stands over her. Neither of them can finish the other. "Ah." And then: "*Cut the boy's throat.*"

"You're the pig woman." He draws a knife left-handed. It's a working knife, not a duelling piece. "He was a foul little animal and he'd have been the ruin of the house inside five years." Perfectly level. "You've done the Baron a service and he will hang you for it, and I'll be there."

He's an 82 with that knife and he cannot make it work, because there is a hole in his abdomen she put there from a boat forty yards away and every time he bends at the waist something inside him refuses.

He goes down onto one knee to reach her and stops halfway with his teeth locked, and the knife comes in short and slow and wide, and he has to put his other hand on the deck to keep from going over.

Neither of them touches the other. For a second the two of them are simply face to face on a wet deck, a foot apart, both bleeding, seventy years apart in age and neither able to finish the other.

Then he says, quite quietly, with what breath he has: "Ah."

And he looks over her shoulder, past her, at the barred door under the quarterdeck.

"*Cut the boy's throat.*"

### Event 149 <!-- id: myth-event-7168a9c67d3a, type: combat, at: 2026-09-07T18:38:48.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Blau, Purewater -->

A 6. CRITICAL, from flat on her back. Two special effects. "Abdomen!" -- she takes Choose Location and puts the axe into the hole she made from forty yards.

She comes up off the deck at him -- not standing, *lunging*, all of it from the hips and the one good leg, with the axe going out low and flat.

She doesn't take the leg. She takes the *hole* -- the one she put in him from a boat in the dark forty yards out, that he has been holding shut with one hand for the last five minutes because he is a man who does not believe in armour.

The axe goes in where his hand is and takes the hand with it.

He goes flat. There's a moment where he's looking at the mast, and then there isn't anybody looking at anything.

The two guards see it happen from four feet away.

**That is the second thing tonight that has stopped a fight** -- the first was the noise she made coming over the rail. Blau was the reason those men were still standing in formation on a deck they'd already lost, and now Blau is a shape on the boards with an old woman lying next to him. One of them steps back.

Then, from the bow: "**MAGDA -- DOWN!**" -- and Gardwen's **Entangle** takes the whole waist of the ship: cordage, weed off the rigging, wet rope coming alive off the pins and going round ankles and wrists and throats. It catches both guards. Three knights forward. Two canal men who will be very rude about it later.

And it catches **the knight at the door**, with the beam half out of the brackets, two feet from a boy behind it.

### Event 150 <!-- id: myth-event-8777757e76e1, type: scene, at: 2026-09-07T18:40:10.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Cailan, Thuban Eta, Purewater -->

THE CABIN. Chalk that is kept and redrawn every day. A cot bolted down, stripped to the canvas. And a wound that has been made and remade in the same place four hundred times.

Small. Low. Lamp-lit, and hot -- hotter than the deck, hot the way a byre is hot, and the air in it is *wrong*: scorched hair and something sweetish, the exact smell that came out of Emmeralda's room on the Pearl.

Chalk on the deck-boards. A worked figure, a big one, and this one isn't half-scuffed. **It's kept. Somebody redraws it every day.**

A cot, bolted down. Bedding stripped to the canvas -- because the sheets went ashore this morning in a laundress's bag.

Two men in here with him and neither is a soldier. One's already backing into the corner with his hands up and a bowl of something in them. The other has a razor in his hand and he is standing over the cot, and he has heard the order from the deck, and he has *not moved yet* -- she has caught him at the exact moment a man decides whether he is the kind of person who does that.

And on the cot: seventeen. Thin the way people are thin when someone else decides what they eat. Stripped to the waist, and the whole upper left of his chest is a long-healed, re-opened, re-healed, re-opened mess.

He's awake. He looks straight at her, and there are two people looking out of his face at once, and one of them says in a boy's voice --

"*Don't*--"

-- and the other finishes it in a voice a thousand years older:

"-- **she's not here for you.**"

### Event 151 <!-- id: myth-event-4fe835f72470, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-07T18:42:27.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Thuban Eta, Purewater -->

Trying to pull out of the rage to spare the man with the razor. Willpower, Hard, augmented by the oath to Gardwen: 72 against 52. IT DOESN'T LET GO OF HER.

Nothing in her wants to stop. That's what the rage *is* -- it does not retreat and it does not spare, and the man with the razor is standing over Gardwen's brother.

She points the axe at him and that is *all* she can make herself do -- and it costs her everything she has to do even that. The rage does not want the razor. The rage wants the man holding it, and she has got both hands locked on the haft and is standing there shaking with the effort of not closing the eight feet between them, making a noise through her teeth that isn't words in any language.

The razor-man understands exactly none of it as mercy. What he sees is a seventy-year-old woman in bloodied mail, filling a doorway, screaming, with an axe levelled at his face and a dead enforcer behind her on the deck.

He drops the razor. It hits the boards and skitters under the cot. Then he sits straight down on the deck with his hands on his head, which is the single most sensible thing anyone has done on this ship tonight, and starts saying "*I only do the dressings, I only do the dressings*" over and over.

And on the cot the older voice comes out of him again -- steady, and very tired, and speaking to her as if across a table:

"**Woman of the death-goddess.** I have been listening to you come up this ship for a quarter of an hour. I know what you are and I know who sent you, and I have been waiting for somebody to come with that particular smell on them for a *very long time*."

The boy's face contorts -- a fight, right there, two people using one throat. "-- hurts, it hurts when he talks, *please* --" -- "-- **I know. I am sorry. It will not be much longer now.**"

"Get us off this ship before the other one comes back with my bones."

### Event 152 <!-- id: myth-event-0b58ed64bee4, type: combat, at: 2026-09-07T18:46:00.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Purewater -->

Second attempt at breaking the rage: Formidable 39, rolled 61. It has her until it's finished with her. "General Thuban" -- then she goes back out and kills nine helpless entangled men.

She gets one thing out. Two words, ground through her teeth at the shape on the cot, and it is not a greeting and it is not a promise -- it's the acknowledgement one soldier gives another across a yard.

"**General Thuban.**"

The old voice says, with something like relief: "*Ah. Good.*"

Then she turns round and goes back out onto the deck, because that is where the rest of them are.

And the deck is a slaughterhouse floor by now -- Gardwen's entangle has the whole waist of the ship in wet cordage, and there are five men in it who cannot get their legs under them, and every one of them is in scale, and every one of them belongs to the house that has kept a boy in that cabin for ten years bleeding him every morning to keep a dead man quiet.

**They are helpless. She is not able to care.**

Canal men get out of her way. One of them -- a lad of maybe nineteen who has been in three knife fights tonight and enjoyed them -- takes one look at her coming up the deck and climbs the shrouds to be somewhere else.

She does not remember most of the next minute.

When it lets go of her, she's on her knees by the mainmast with the axe across her thighs and no strength in either arm, and the fatigue comes down like a dropped sack. Berserk ends: automatic 1 Fatigue level. Fresh -> Winded.

There are nine men on that deck who are not going to get up.

Gardwen is standing about ten feet away with her staff in both hands and her mouth slightly open, and she has clearly been standing there for some time, waiting to find out whether she needed to say her name.

### Event 153 <!-- id: myth-event-171fbf9682ca, type: scene, at: 2026-09-07T18:49:07.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Purewater -->

"Ooooh. Let me just sit down for a bit. Fuck me. Anyone got anything to drink?" The lad who went up the shrouds to avoid her comes down and holds a bottle out at arm's length, like a man feeding something at a fair.

She sits down on a coil of cable with her back against the mast and her legs out in front of her like a woman at the end of a market day, and makes the noise that seventy-year-old knees make when they are finally allowed to have a view.

"It's not good," he says. -- "Doesn't need to be."

It isn't. It's the kind of spirit they make out on the Shoals from whatever's left, and it goes down like a hot nail, and it is the finest thing she has ever tasted.

Around her the ship gets sorted with startling efficiency, because these people do this for a living. Three chests up on deck in the first minute. The long flat case takes four of them.

Ravella comes over the rail dripping and cold to the touch and looks at the nine on the deck and says nothing about any of it. Then she goes to the rail and speaks down at the water, and something answers.

Gardwen is not looking at the deck. She has gone straight past all of it to the cabin door and stopped in it, and she has not gone in, and she has been standing there for about a minute and a half. She has one hand on the frame.

Inside, the boy is trying to work out who she is, and the old voice is telling him.

### Event 154 <!-- id: myth-event-6ca9f5deb3d8, type: scene, at: 2026-09-07T18:51:13.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Cailan, Purewater -->

"Go on girl. It's him. He's got someone with him, but he's there." -- and Thuban Eta *steps back* and gives the boy the room.

She goes in. You can't hear it from the mast. You can see it through the doorway, in lamplight, in pieces.
