She stops two feet short of the cot, because he's a stranger -- seventeen and thin and marked and not the shape of anything she's been carrying for ten years. And he looks at her the same way, because she isn't eleven any more either.

Then the old voice says something -- you see the boy's mouth move and you see her flinch at the register of it -- and whatever it says, it's short, and afterwards the face on that cot changes completely.

*It steps back.* That's what it looks like from a doorway thirty feet off. Something in him steps back and gives the boy the room.

His mouth moves. Whatever he says is one word and it isn't her name, because he was seven and there are things a seven-year-old calls a big sister that aren't a name.

Gardwen's legs go. She catches herself on the edge of the cot and then she just puts her forehead down on his shoulder -- carefully, on the right, away from the wound -- and stays there, and the boy gets one arm up around her head, and neither of them makes any sound you can hear.

Ruhi comes over the rail from somewhere, soaked, and pours herself into that cabin and lies down under the cot without being asked.

### Event 155 <!-- id: myth-event-ae362fc81bd1, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T18:54:41.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Ravella, Purewater -->

"What do you want to do with this shitpile of a boat, Ravella? Where do you want it to go?" -- "Nobody's asked me that. Not one person in this city has ever asked what we'd like done with anything."

She says it flatly, not as a complaint -- as data.

Then she stands and looks down the length of the black hull.

"Let your crow strip her. Take every last thing out of her -- and *especially* whatever's in the aft hold making that smoke, because that's the part that's been burning my channel, and I'd like it in a box on dry land where I can be angry at it later.

"Then take her out. Past the Mouth, past the bar, out where the Lady's water stops running and the deep starts -- and open her seams and let her go down there. Not in the harbour. Not in the roads. **Out.**"

She glances at her. "And we'll see her down. That's ours to do. There'll be people under her the whole way and when she settles, someone will be there to say the water's clean again."

A pause. "Tell your crow he can have a week to get the cargo off. After that we take the hull whether he's finished or not."

### Event 156 <!-- id: myth-event-0a93bd4b2f24, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-07T18:57:43.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Ravella, Lost Isle -->

THE FIRST RITE FAILS. Lore (Hel) 87 against 52. She says the words over Ellick Sarn and the other one -- and nothing happens at all.

She walks over to them on a bad leg with the axe still in her hand because she's forgotten she's holding it, and kneels down on the wet stone, which hurts, and pulls the sheet back off their faces because she has never in her life said words over a covered face.

Behind her everyone stops moving. Nerissa takes her lamp two steps back. Under the stair the water goes very still, and it is not the wind that does it.

*"Mother of the darkness. Take these drowned back to their rest. It's time they went home."*

And nothing happens.

The water stays still. The lamps go on burning. The two men on the stone go on being two men on the stone, and Ellick Sarn's face does not change in any way at all.

She waits. She gives it a good while, because she has stood at enough gravesides to know that the words sometimes take a minute. They don't.

Somewhere down the stair, under the black water, something is *listening* and has not been given what it needs.

### Event 157 <!-- id: myth-event-1fbd283de335, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T18:58:30.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa, Ravella, Lost Isle -->

RAVELLA'S CORRECTION: "It's not the words. The words were right. You said *take them*. You're asking her to come and fetch. She won't. She never does."

She comes and crouches on the other side of the bodies and looks at her across them, and she isn't angry, which is worse.

"You said *take them*." She lays two fingers on Ellick's forehead. "You're asking her to come and fetch. She won't. She never does -- that's the entire nature of your goddess, isn't it? **She takes the ones nobody sings about, and she takes them *when somebody finally brings them*.**"

She looks up. "Thorne told you this and you were listening to the other half of it. Nobody ever came back for Thuban. Nobody has come back for **these two** either -- Ellick's wife thinks he ran off, and that one's people don't know he's dead."

She stands. "You have to do the part that costs you. Not ask her to take them. **Give them to her.** Say whose they are and say what was done to them and say that somebody came back -- and you have to mean the last one, because you're the somebody."

Nerissa, from behind, very quietly: "That is exactly how it works, Magda. In every faith I know."

And Hesper, who has been listening while unpacking his bag, and who has gone rather pale: "And if that's the shape of it for two drowned boatmen who died last week..."

He doesn't finish it. He doesn't need to.

### Event 158 <!-- id: myth-event-c641b303838d, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T19:04:52.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Ravella, Lost Isle -->

THE LITANY. "Oi. Listen up then." Devotion to Hel 70 goes on the sheet for the first time; Lore (Hel) 52 +14 = 66. Rolled 52. It takes. The water goes WARM.

First, a correction to her own sheet: she has had three passions on it and none of them is the one that walked her three hundred miles on eleven nights of dreams. **Devotion to Hel -- 70.**

And this isn't the same attempt as before. Ravella told her the shape of it; she's not repeating a failed thing, she's doing a different one.

She mutters *I'm not a fucking priest* at the stone, and then does it anyway -- kneeling on wet stone with a bad leg, in the voice you'd use across a yard.

*"Oi. Listen up then.*

*"Brothers, workers, toilers, men. You lived your lives as we all do -- with imperfect plans, a little hope, a little selfishness, likely with much love and sadness when you died. In this place. In the water. The water that takes all things.*

*"But then these bastards brought you back. To haul, and work, and toil. Because it helped them.*

*"So fuck 'em.*

*"Come with me, brothers. Rage with me at the loss and the death -- and then go back to the dark deep sea. Go to your rest. Go now, my brothers. Go."*

She is, at this point, shouting at a hole in the ground.

The water at the foot of the stair does not part or glow or do any of the things a story would have it do. **It goes *warm*.** That's all. It comes up the steps past her knees like a draught from an open door in a cold house, and every lamp on that island bends toward it at once, and Nerissa makes a small sound behind her.

And the two men on the stone let go. There's no light. There's a *slackening* -- she has seen it four hundred times in byres and beds and she would know it anywhere. Whatever was still holding the shape of Ellick Sarn's face stops holding it, and what's left is just a boatman who died, and looks it, and looks *finished*.

Ravella has both hands flat on the stone and her head down, and she stays like that a long time. When she gets up her face is wet and it is not canal water.

"They're through. The Court has them." And, with no broker anywhere in it: "**You said whose they were. Nobody has said whose anybody was down here in four hundred years.**"

Hesper is staring at her. "Magda. That was two men who died *last week*, and it took everything you had."

### Event 159 <!-- id: myth-event-657750bee9d2, type: combat, at: 2026-09-07T19:09:03.000000000, session: 1, involves: High Priestess Nerissa, Lost Isle -->

The quiet one lands, carries five feet of oiled cloth up the drowned street on his shoulder, sets it down gently -- and calls for a man who has been dead since last night.

One set of oars, unhurried, no lantern. He comes round the eastern point and along the drowned street with the confidence of someone who has done it four times before, and lands the boat exactly where Ellick landed his, and ships the oars, and steps out into the water without a sound.

He's a long time getting the thing out of the boat. It takes him three tries and a great deal of care.

**Oiled cloth, roped, about five feet long.** He carries it up the drowned street on his shoulder with his back bent under it, and sets it down on the stone at the foot of the stair -- *gently*, the way you'd set down someone sleeping -- and stands there breathing.

Then he says, to nobody, in a voice that carries beautifully across still water:

"**Ellick. Get up here and help me with him.**"

Nerissa doesn't even signal. She just says, quietly, "*Take him*," and the wall comes alive. **24 against 80, and a 100 on the second: he gets nothing off. Not a word, not a gesture.**

He doesn't struggle. He turns his head sideways against the stone, and what he does is *look*. At the shuttered lamps coming open one by one. At twelve Swords of the Lake. At Nerissa. At Hesper. And then at the oiled bundle lying eighteen inches from his face, which he set down so carefully, and which he is now not going to be allowed to touch again.

Hesper crouches over it and doesn't touch it either. He looks at the ropework a long moment and his mouth thins. "That's a *reliquary* knot. That's not how you tie cargo. **That's how you tie something you're afraid of.**"

### Event 160 <!-- id: myth-event-8e84536721d4, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T19:11:00.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Hesper, High Priestess Nerissa, Lost Isle -->

"Ask that one." The quiet one answers three questions and holds at 58/75. Then Hesper: "Why are we asking the gaoler? *Ask the prisoner.*"

He answers exactly three questions and they are all the wrong ones.

*Is that Thuban Eta in the cloth?* -- "Yes." *Will the removal work at this stair?* -- "Yes." *Will you tell us how?* -- "No."

Then he settles his shoulders against the wall and stops.

Hesper tries for a long while. He's good at it -- he goes at it from underneath, professional to professional, *you've done this and I haven't and I'd very much like to know how the seat is kept clean.* And you can see the man enjoying it, and enjoying not answering more.

At the end he says one thing unprompted, to Hesper, almost kindly: "**You'll open him at the wrong point and put a thousand-year-old knight into a priestess. I'd rather like to see that.**"

Nerissa steps back from him and looks like she wants to wash.

Hesper comes back and sits down on the stone with his bag between his knees, and rubs both hands over his face. "Well. That's that. So it's my library, my theory, and a rite I have read about twice and never--"

He stops mid-word, and his hands come away from his face, and he looks over her shoulder at the tower-stub where Gardwen is sitting with her arm round a seventeen-year-old boy.

"Magda. **We have been extraordinarily stupid.** There is a man not thirty feet from us who has been *inside* one of these for a thousand years. Who was put in by experts and has had every day since to feel exactly how the seat is fastened.

"Why are we asking the gaoler? **Ask the prisoner.**"

### Event 161 <!-- id: myth-event-eefb62d32897, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T19:11:17.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa, Lost Isle -->

"I have an idea. Start stripping off my armor." Nobody knows what she is doing. Nerissa looks at the black water where they put a man in mail with his hands tied, and arrives somewhere she doesn't like.

The kettle helm goes on the stone. Then the coif, and her hair comes out flat and grey and wet.

Then she goes at the hauberk, which is a two-person job at the best of times, and Nerissa -- who has been watching her with a deepening frown -- comes and takes the weight of it without being asked, and the two of them get it off over her head. It goes down on the stone with the noise a hauberk makes, which is like a great deal of money being dropped.

She's in a soaked wool shirt in the cold, seventy years old, with a spear-hole in her thigh, standing on a drowned island in the dark.

Hesper has gone very quiet. Nerissa says, "Magda." And then, when she doesn't answer, she looks at the black water at the foot of the swept stair -- water they put a man into, in his mail, with his hands tied -- and you watch her arrive somewhere she doesn't like.

"*Magda.*"

Gardwen has stood up at the tower-stub.

Everyone on that island is now looking at her, and not one of them knows what she is about to do.

### Event 162 <!-- id: myth-event-fb12e3c0bf49, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T19:14:17.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Cailan, Thuban Eta, Lost Isle -->

THUBAN SAYS NO. "You would beat me out of a child. I do not feel your hands, woman -- *he does*." And then he tells her what it actually needs.

She walks over to the tower-stub in a wet shirt with her hands empty and tells him what she means to do: berserk the boy, unarmed and unarmoured, and force the general out into his bones and through the veil.

The boy's face is wet and frightened and seventeen. Then it changes -- you can watch it happen now, the way you'd watch someone step forward in a doorway -- and the other one is looking out. He takes his time. He's looking at her bare arms, and the spear-hole, and the axe she's left thirty feet away.

"**No.**" A pause. "But I understand why you thought of it, and I want you to know I would have taken that offer from you gladly, if it worked.

"Understand what you are proposing. **You would beat me out of a child. I do not feel your hands, woman -- *he does*.** You would break his ribs and I would watch, and at the end of it I would still be here, because I am not held in his body by his body. They have been cutting him open every morning for ten years to do exactly what you just described. If pain could shift me I would have been shifted four hundred times."

He lets that sit.

"Now hear the rest, because you were closer than your priest is. **There is a fight.** You were right about that. But it is not yours, and it is not with the boy.

"They did not fasten me in with iron. Your storyteller told you and he was correct -- **they fastened me in with the night I died.** Every hour I have spent in here I have spent back on that boat with my hands tied, going over the side, looking up at Corran and Vey and the rest of them not doing anything. That is the room I have been kept in. Not this child. *That.*"

His voice goes thin. "And I cannot leave it, because to leave it I have to accept that it happened and that nobody came, and I have not been *able* to accept that, because it is unbearable, and I have had a thousand years of finding it unbearable.

"So. You do not need to fight me out. **You need to come and get me out of that water. Say it was wrong. Say whose I am. Say somebody came.**

"And you must mean it, and I will know if you don't, because I have had a very long time to learn what people mean.

"Open my bones and open your door. And then stand where they stood, and do not look away when I go under, because they did.

"That is all it ever needed. **It has only ever needed one person to turn up.**"

### Event 163 <!-- id: myth-event-28430a311561, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-07T19:18:15.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Lost Isle -->

"How would we honour a mighty warrior, murdered by cowards?" Customs 26/48. It comes back not as instructions but as smells -- and then she hits the thing at the bottom of it.

Woodsmoke and wet wool and somebody's aunt weeping professionally.

**You put his weapons back in his hands.** A man goes out armed. Whatever was taken off him when he died is given back, and if you haven't got it you give him yours, and that's better anyway.

**You say the name.** Out loud, first, before anything, so everyone standing there has to hear it.

**Then you tell the tale.** Not a prayer -- a *telling*. Who he was, what he did, and how he died, said in front of witnesses, in the open, at full volume.

**You settle the debt.** If a man was murdered, somebody stands up and says so, and names it, and the wrong is spoken into the air where everyone can hear it. Nobody goes into the ground with an unpaid killing on him.

**Then fire.** Or water, for a drowned man. You give him back to whatever took him, on purpose this time, with people watching.

**And you feast, and you brawl, and you get disgracefully drunk**, because a death that nobody celebrates is a death that didn't count.

And then she hits the thing at the bottom of it, and it stops her where she stands.

**Valhalla isn't his.** That's for the battle-dead. Thuban Eta went over a gunwale in the dark with his hands tied by his own people. He was *cheated* of the death that buys that hall, and he has been paying for their crime with his afterlife for a thousand years.

Which is exactly, precisely, why he's Hel's.

And the wound isn't the water and it isn't the rope. **It's that in a thousand years, nobody has ever told his tale.** Nobody said the name. Nobody said what he did or how he died or that it was wrong. There has never been a telling.

She is standing on a rock in the dark with a High Priestess, a hedge-mage, a druid, a wolf, twelve Swords of the Lake, a crime lord's boat crew somewhere off the point, a half-merfolk broker and a whole drowned Court listening from underneath.

That is the largest crowd of witnesses that has ever stood over Thuban Eta in his life or his death.

### Event 164 <!-- id: myth-event-b6b49453a482, type: scene, at: 2026-09-07T19:19:00.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Hesper, High Priestess Nerissa, Lost Isle -->

Hesper cuts the reliquary knots, naming each one as it comes off. Around the wrists: two turns of iron wire, corroded almost to nothing, still on him after a thousand years.

He does it slowly, naming each knot, and Nerissa stands at his shoulder with her hands in the water at the stair-head, and the black water there has started to move without any wind on it.

The oiled cloth comes back.

**Bones.** Long ones, brown with age, laid the way you'd lay a man -- and around the wrists, corroded almost to nothing but still there, still *on him* after a thousand years, two turns of iron wire.

They put him in the water tied and nobody ever cut it off.

Gardwen makes a sound.

Nerissa says, "*Give me the shears*," and cuts them, and drops the wire in the water, and that alone changes the temperature of the air on that stair.

### Event 165 <!-- id: myth-event-016801c6b804, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-07T19:19:30.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Thuban Eta, Lost Isle -->

THE FIRST TELLING FAILS. 47/44 by three. The Luck swap makes it worse (74). The re-roll fails too (56). She does everything right and it is NOT ENOUGH.

She says the name -- *Thuban Eta* -- out loud, first, so everyone on that rock has to hear it. She puts Vell's axe in the bones' hands, because his own went into the water with him and hers is better anyway. She stands at the head of that stair where his company stood over him, and she says the wrong out loud: *these men murdered you, and it was cowardice, and it was never answered for.*

And she tells his tale. In a market-day voice to twelve Swords of the Lake and a priestess and a druid and a wolf and a whole Court under the water -- *he would not burn a valley, so his friends drowned him for it, and no one has said his name in a thousand years.*

It is the first telling he has ever had.

And it is **not enough.**

She feels it not land. It's like putting your shoulder to a door that turns out to be a wall -- everything she has goes into it and the world does not move an inch. The water at the foot of the stair stays cold. The lamps don't lean.

The boy on the stone starts to shake, and the old voice comes out of him ragged for the first time all night: "-- *I can't* -- woman, I can't *reach* it, there's something across--"

Hesper is already up and moving. "The seat's still fastened. Something's still holding him and it is not the bones and it is not this stair--"

And the roped man against the wall, who has said nothing for twenty minutes, starts to **laugh**. Quiet, delighted, entirely genuine.

"Oh, that's *good*. That's very good. You've done all of it properly and you've done it beautifully and you have forgotten one thing. He isn't only held by the bones. **The Baron wears the rest of him around his neck.** And the Baron is at the Governor's table three miles from here, being toasted."

### Event 166 <!-- id: myth-event-aa9c7be113b2, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-07T19:23:43.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Lost Isle -->

Magda picks up the axe on the way over. Opposed Willpower 87 against his 77 -- SHE FAILS. "You're not going to. If you were, you'd have done it before you asked."

She is seventy years old, in a soaked shirt, Winded, with a spear-hole in her leg, and she has already killed nine men tonight and told a dead general's tale to a hole in the ground.

He watches her come and he is *still* smiling, and then at about six feet he stops smiling.

He does not flinch and he does not talk. What he does is look up at her with an axe six feet from his face and say, quite gently, like a man explaining something to a child at a funeral:

"**You're not going to. If you were, you'd have done it before you asked.**"

And he closes his eyes and leans his head back against the stone and is simply *done* with the conversation, and there is nothing behind that face she can reach with a weapon.

But the explanation comes anyway, from Hesper, who does not need the prisoner's help for this part:

"The bones are the *primary* anchor. They're where the weight of him sits. But the **locket** is the working one -- the piece they carry, the one the daily ritual runs through. It's not a spare. **It's the *latch*.** They cut a piece off him a century ago and put it in that locket, and every morning they use it to hold the seat shut.

"So the door is open. The bones are open. He is *willing* -- you did that, and it worked, and I want you to hear that it worked. But there is a lock on this and it is around Hanzo di Teufel's neck."

Nerissa, into the silence: "The Tourney is at noon."

And Gardwen, with her brother shaking against her: "Then that's where we go. Because that's where *he'll* be -- in the Governor's box, in front of the whole city, wearing my brother round his neck."

### Event 167 <!-- id: myth-event-76a35d6d31f7, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T19:27:05.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, High Priestess Nerissa, Ravella, Lost Isle -->

THE MORAL PIVOT. Magda waits until the strategising is finished, then beheads the bound prisoner and puts the head in a grain sack. "Might come in handy."

She doesn't join the strategising. She stands at the edge of it with the axe hanging from one hand and watches the roped man, and he knows she's watching, and after a while he opens his eyes and looks back, and the two of them hold that for a long time while the others work out how to get into a Governor's box at noon.

Nerissa says, "--then Emeric has to hear it from Sana herself, and not in an anteroom--"

And she walks over and does it.

It takes one. The sound afterwards is the sound of everyone on that island stopping at once.

She finds a grain sack by the stair, tips out what's in it, puts the head in, knots the top, and says it to nobody: "Might come in handy."

**Corvin** -- twenty-two, who took a rope out to Lost Isle for her the night before -- turns round and walks twenty feet away and is sick against a wall.

**Hesper** closes his eyes. Just for a moment. Then opens them and goes back to the bones, because he is a practical man and there is work, and he does not look at her again for some time.

**Ravella** doesn't react at all. She looks at the body, and then at the water, and says quietly to one of hers: "Not that one. He doesn't go down there."

**Gardwen** looks at her for a long moment. She doesn't say *why did you do that.* She says: "Was he still worth anything?"

And **Nerissa** comes and stands in front of her. Her face is perfectly still and her voice does not rise, and every Sword on that island can hear her.

"He was bound. On my island, under my cordon, taken by my people who had orders to hold him." She lets that sit. "And he was the only living soul who knew where **Bettan** is. Fourteen years old. Cheerful. Cutting girls' hair at a school on the Northern Isles for people who fill them up."

Her hands are shaking slightly and she is doing nothing to hide it.

"I gave you a reason to stop, once, and you took it. So I will not pretend I did not know what you were. But **you have just taken that child away from me and I do not know how to get her back.**"

She turns and walks away toward the boats. Twelve Swords of the Lake watch her go, and then look at Magda, and none of them says anything at all.

### Event 168 <!-- id: myth-event-3a5e3fb6bb63, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T19:29:29.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Lost Isle -->

"Gardwen. You know how to find people, right?" -- and Gardwen puts the sack aside because there's a job. "I don't need to track her. I have to stand somewhere near that school and ask what's *wrong* in it."

"In woods, yes. Broken ground, bent grass, where a body's lain -- I could find a child in the Ash Vale in a night. Not here. There's no *ground*. It's all stone and water and forty thousand people walking over each other."

Then she stops. "But I don't need to track her."

She looks up. "Magda -- I found those two on Lost Isle from four hundred yards across open water, and I never saw them. I felt what was *in* them. Wrong the way milk's wrong. And I found Sana in the same casting because she wasn't."

She's on her feet now. "Bettan's been filled. Sana told us -- the quiet week, the cheerfulness, the not feeling the cold. If she's carrying one of those things then she doesn't read like a girl any more, and **I don't have to find *her*. I have to stand somewhere near that school and ask what's *wrong* in it.**

"It cost me a magic point and nearly all of my legs, and I couldn't reach across the channel until the priestess put her hand in the water with me. So I'd need to be *at* Corbie's. And I'd need Nerissa."

She meets her eye and doesn't soften it. "Who has just walked off to the boats and will not want to look at you."

Then, more quietly: "Go and tell her anyway. Not sorry -- she'd smell that. Tell her you've got a way to find the girl, and let her decide what she does with you afterwards."

### Event 169 <!-- id: myth-event-64efb1a14e0a, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T19:33:18.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa, Lost Isle -->

"Oi. Priestess! Don't look for the girl. Look for the demon." -- "You've mistaken me. That isn't purity, Magda. That's **arithmetic**."

She says all of it and she doesn't apologise, because she isn't, and Nerissa would know. Nerissa listens to the whole thing without interrupting.

Then: "You've mistaken me." Very level. "I did not object because it was *unclean*. I have buried men off the tide-flats with their throats opened by people I then had to bless at Solstice. I'm sixty-one and I run a temple in a port." She takes a step closer. "**I objected because you cut up the only map to a fourteen-year-old girl and then said *might come in handy*. That isn't purity, Magda. That's arithmetic.**"

She holds her eye until she's sure it's landed, and only then does the rest of her arrive.

"--and yes. Yes, that would work. I can put my hands in the water at Corbie's and hold the channel for her the way I did last night, and if that child has something in her then it will show like a lamp through a shutter." Her jaw tightens. "It's better than anything I had, which was *ask the school again and hope*.

"We go at first light. Not before -- the girls will be at breakfast and I want them in one room and counted while it's done."

Then, as Magda turns to walk away: "Magda." Not raised. "**I will work with you until this is finished, and I will do it wholeheartedly, and I will not pretend at you afterwards. When the boy is out and the Baron is down, you and I are done, and you will not be welcome on Temple Isle.**"

A beat. "Sword-Captain Vell's axe stays with you. She'd have wanted that and I'm not petty."

She gets in the boat.

And Magda, shrugging: "No. I killed him because he deserved it. Fucker wasn't going to tell you anything anyway. I don't even know what arithmetic is."

"No," Nerissa agrees. "You don't."

### Event 170 <!-- id: myth-event-0b57a4beb78e, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-07T19:48:37.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, High Priestess Nerissa, The Northern Isles -->

Corbie's at first light. Gardwen's casting takes at 45/103 -- and then: "*No.* Wait-- There's **three**."

Mistress Corliss has forty girls in the refectory being counted, which they are treating as an outrage. Nerissa goes into the channel to her knees at the water-stair. Gardwen takes her hand and puts the other flat on the wall of the school itself.

It takes about four seconds. "There," she says.

Then, immediately: "*No.* Wait--" And her grip tightens on Nerissa's hand hard enough to make the priestess wince.

"There's **three**."

Bettan, fourteen, cheerful, in the refectory with the others -- a wrongness sitting in her like a lamp behind a shutter. And two more in that room. Girls who went quiet for a week sometime in the last month, and came back lovely, and started asking everyone about their families.

Nobody noticed. Why would they? A homesick child getting happier is the thing you *want* to see.

Gardwen opens her eyes and she's grey. "Magda -- Santo had five twists of hair. He'd used one. Four left, and Sana's was one of them." She's already doing the sum out loud and hating it. "**They weren't collecting *vessels*. They've been *filling* them.** Here. For a month. In a school full of forty girls, four hundred yards from that stair."

Nerissa comes up out of the water very slowly. "The four twists were the ones they hadn't done yet," she says.

She looks at the lit windows of the refectory, where forty girls are being counted and complaining about it.

"Then how many *have* they done?"

### Event 171 <!-- id: myth-event-723d7af5d452, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T19:55:17.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa, Temerach Nebulo -->

"That's a matter for the watch, wouldn't you say?" -- and then Magda names Temerach Nebulo, who is already inside the ground with a bow and a competitor's badge.

Nerissa takes the school off her hands without argument -- it's hers, the girls are hers, and she is very glad to have something she can do properly. "Corliss keeps them in the refectory. Nobody leaves the island. And the Watch will take the di Teufel household on High Isle *quietly*, at breakfast, because at breakfast on Tourney day there'll be four men in that house and forty at the field. That I can arrange without a warrant -- the Harbor Masters owe me and the household aren't nobles, they're servants."

Then Magda says the name, and she goes still.

"**Temerach Nebulo.** She's ours. Sworn to the Order. The *Lakelady's Javelin* -- that's not a name the temple gives out twice in a century.

"And she's entered in the **Archery**. Which means she is already inside that ground with a bow in her hand and a competitor's badge on her chest, and she is the one person in Purewater who can stand thirty yards from the Governor's box at noon holding a weapon and have nobody think anything of it whatsoever."

Hesper says quietly: "I saw her shoot at the Anminster meet, eleven years ago. Moving targets, boat to boat, cross-channel." -- "And?" -- "She put every arrow inside a handspan and then apologised to the judges for the last one, because it was *high*. I have never in my life seen anyone do anything that well. I'm not sure I've seen anyone do *anything* that well." -- "No," Nerissa agrees. "You haven't."

And Gardwen, from where she's sitting with her back against the school wall: "She's been hunting Hanzo di Teufel for years -- that's the whole reason she's in this city. So she'll help. But she's spent her whole career trying to bring that man down the *lawful* way, in front of witnesses, with everything in order." She glances at the sack by Magda's foot. "And she is not going to enjoy what's in that bag."

### Event 172 <!-- id: myth-event-4ef798f80221, type: gm-note, at: 2026-09-07T19:58:00.000000000, session: 1 -->

PLAYER DIRECTIVE: "Don't have characters talk about skill numbers in gameplay. Find another way to express it." The beat was re-rendered as Hesper's memory of watching her shoot eleven years ago.

Numbers stay in the roll line, never in anyone's mouth. Saved to memory and applied for the rest of the session.

The original version had Hesper say "Her combat style is one hundred and twenty-one." The replacement is a man describing something he watched at the Anminster meet eleven years ago and has never got over. It is strictly better, and it came from the player.

### Event 173 <!-- id: myth-event-36a40806b4d1, type: scene, at: 2026-09-07T20:01:56.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa, Temple Isle -->

Giving the gear back on the temple steps. "That's a young woman's kit." Nerissa picks the axe up herself and carries it in personally.

Hauberk, coif, kettle helm, and Vell's axe laid across the mail like a man laid out.

"I said keep it."

"Aye. And it's about four stone and I'm seventy." She gets her staff back off the Sword who's been holding it, and the weight of it in her hands is the first thing that's felt right in a day. "That's a young woman's kit. She'd want it on somebody who can carry it up a rope without spending a favour to do it."

She chuckles, and it turns into something rougher. "Besides. Fucker on that ship didn't think I was going to make it up either."

Nerissa does not smile. But she picks the axe up herself, and carries it in personally, which is not something a High Priestess does for a piece of armoury.

Four hours' sleep in a cell under the temple on a cot objectively worse than the deck of the DragonBarge, and she sleeps like the dead. Fatigue: Fresh. Luck: 1 of 3. That's what's left of the night.

### Event 174 <!-- id: myth-event-e1ca9be32519, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-07T20:04:49.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda -->

Gardwen heals the spear-hole. Two attempts (90/87 fails, then 53/58). "Don't. You've been walking on that since the ship and I've been asking since the ship."

She's been offering since the ship and Magda has been waving her off.

She takes two goes at it -- the first one slides off, and she swears in a way that startles a passing Sword -- and then she gets both hands round the thigh and leans on it the way she described at the ford. *Find the shape it's supposed to be and lean until it remembers.*

The spear-hole closes from the inside out. It itches unbearably for about four seconds and then it's a pink pucker and an old woman's ordinary aches.

### Event 175 <!-- id: myth-event-1cee2c6e8041, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T20:08:28.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa, Temerach Nebulo -->

"Why the fuck didn't anyone tell her?" -- because the competitors' enclosure is sealed by a four-hundred-year-old temple rule that Nerissa herself enforces.

"I sent Corvin at first light. He's been at the competitors' rope for four hours." She points, and he's there -- the young Sword who was sick against a wall on Lost Isle, standing at the cordon with a folded note in his hand and a face like a man failing.

"The enclosure is **sealed** from the first horn. No messages in, no messages out, nobody in or out but judges and the Lady's own. It's not a Governor's rule I can lean on, it's a *temple* rule, and it's four hundred years old, and it exists precisely so nobody can nobble a competitor with news from outside." Her jaw tightens. "I wrote it. Or my predecessors did. I *enforce* it."

"You're the High Priestess."

"I am. And if I break the seal on the sacred enclosure at the Lady's own Tourney, in front of six thousand people and every Guild in this city, then this afternoon stops being about a Baron and starts being about me. Which is exactly what the men sitting next to Emeric would like."

She lets that sit. "There is one exception and it is not much of one. **The Lady's own** may pass -- that's clergy, in vestment, on temple business.

"But Lilura has never met Temerach Nebulo, doesn't know the half of it, and cannot be trusted to say the right four things to an elf who will start asking questions."

She looks at her. Then at Gardwen. Then, for a moment, at the sack.

"You, on the other hand, are not clergy, are not in vestment, are not subtle, and are extremely memorable."

### Event 176 <!-- id: myth-event-ff50d40adb45, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T20:09:42.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, High Priestess Nerissa, Temple Isle -->

Nerissa vests the woman who beheaded a bound prisoner on her island eight hours earlier, with her own hands. "I would very much like you to appreciate what that costs me."

"I want to make you a **novice of the Order of the Lake Lady**. For about an hour. Vestment, sash, and Lilura walking you in as her attendant, which is a thing a novice does and which nobody looks at twice."

She holds up a hand before Magda can say anything.

"I am aware. Yes. I told you last night you'd not be welcome on Temple Isle again and I meant it and I will still mean it at sunset." Her mouth is very thin. "But right now the only thing standing between six thousand people and a demonologist in the Governor's box is whether one archer gets told four sentences in the next hour, and I have a rule I will not break and a robe I *can* hand you.

"So I am going to put the Lady's cloth on a woman who beheaded a bound prisoner on my island eight hours ago, and I am going to do it with my own hands, and I would very much like you to appreciate what that costs me."

She's already unpinning her own sash.

"Four sentences. Not five. She is the finest archer in the realm and she is about to shoot in front of the whole city, and if you fill her head with the wrong thing at the wrong moment you will cost her the only lawful route she has ever had at that man."

The vestment is blue-green and about a foot too long and smells of cedar. Nerissa does the sash herself, at the throat, with her mouth in a line, and she does not meet her eye once while she's doing it -- and when she's done she steps back and looks at her in it for a second and says, flatly: "Don't."

"Wasn't going to say a word."

"You were *radiating*."

### Event 177 <!-- id: myth-event-eb09628f5d3a, type: gm-note, at: 2026-09-07T20:19:48.000000000, session: 1, involves: Conall Bjornlasch, Magda, Temerach Nebulo -->

THE EPISTEMICS MECHANISM'S FIRST REAL TEST -- and the GM fails it, not the graph. Magda has never heard the name Conall. The beat is replayed.

The player caught it: *"Remember Magda doesn't know Conall. First test of the epistemology mechanisms in the game and it fails."*

The graph was unambiguous. Every fact about Conall Bjornlasch is known by Temerach alone. `CLAUDE.md` says *"`character-view --id <pc>` before speaking for anyone,"* and the GM wrote Magda's dialogue without querying it. The mechanism didn't fail -- it only catches this if it is consulted.

Replayed with what she actually knows:

"Is Conall alive?" -- "Who?"

It stops Temerach. Properly stops her -- the first thing all day that has.

"Conall Bjornlasch." She's watching her face now and getting nothing, because there's nothing there to get. "Dark. Twenty-two. Rides with me. He's been in this city three days."

"Never heard the name in my life, lass."

And something happens behind that flat elven composure that Magda can't read at all.

"...**Then you don't know,**" she says slowly. "None of you know. You've spent three days pulling this apart and nobody has mentioned him once. That is either very good news or the worst news I've had this week, and I don't yet know which."

### Event 178 <!-- id: myth-event-0fded99eb404, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T20:23:40.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Hanzo di Teufel, Temerach Nebulo -->

TEMERACH'S PLAN: not in the box. "So we don't go to him. **We make him leave.**" The water-stair behind the colonnade, twelve feet wide, wall on one side, out of sight of the ground.

She thinks about it for perhaps four seconds, which for her is a long deliberation.

"Not in the box." She says it as settled fact. "Six thousand people are watching that canopy. If he dies in it, or is *taken* in it, then by tonight the story is that the temple and the Governor murdered a foreign lord at the Lady's own Tourney, and the three men sitting beside Emeric will make sure it's the only story. I have watched that happen to better cases than this one.

"So we don't go to him. **We make him leave.**

"The Single Combat is called at the second hour. His champion is in a cell under that temple. He does not know it yet. So at the second hour a herald calls for the di Teufel man, and calls again, and *nobody comes* -- in front of every Guild in this city. He will have about a minute to decide whether he is a man whose champion is late or a man who has been robbed. And he is not stupid. He'll know.

"And then he leaves that box. He has to. He'll go for his boat, and the only water-stair from the Governor's enclosure is the one behind the colonnade, which is twelve feet wide with a wall on one side. That's where he can be taken, and it is out of sight of the ground, and everything that happens there happens in front of temple witnesses instead of the whole city."

Then, on the other thing: "A magician. Yes. I'd assumed. Which is why he does not get a minute of warning and does not get his hands free."

Finally she looks at her properly, and there's something almost like humour in it. "And you're the part I can't do. He will leave when the herald calls. Or he will leave *sooner*, and on our timing instead of his, if somebody he does not know walks up to that box and shows him something that tells him it has all already gone wrong."

A pause. "**You've been carrying that sack since you got here.**"

### Event 179 <!-- id: myth-event-9620016ca363, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T20:28:59.000000000, session: 1, involves: Conall Bjornlasch, Magda, Temerach Nebulo -->

"It's the quiet one's head. He was pissing me off." And then: "This Conall kid of yours. Is he any good with a bow?" -- and Temerach answers too fast.

Her face does not change at the word *head*. "I am not going to pretend that pleases me." Flat. "But I have seen what that man's people do to fourteen-year-olds and I am not going to stand on a field and lecture you about a demonologist's neck. Use it if it works."

Then the other question, and she answers it too fast. "No."

Magda waits.

"He's --" She's stringing something that's already strung. "He has a soldier's training, all weapons. He's competent. He's not an archer."

"That's not what I asked, lass. I asked if he was any good."

"He's *competent*."

Here is a thing about Temerach Nebulo: she is one of the most dangerous people in this realm and she is an **appalling** liar. She has no facility for it whatsoever. She has just told Magda two things she didn't mean to -- that Conall is better than competent, and that she would rather chew her own hand off than have him within a mile of that box.

She hears herself do it, too.

"He is my responsibility. He is twenty-two, he came to this city because I brought him, and there is a reason I have kept him away from the di Teufels that I am not going to discuss on a competition field with a woman I met four minutes ago. Ask me again tonight and I may tell you. Ask me now and I'll say no and mean it."

Then, briskly: "Get me one thing. **Whether he still has the locket on him.** If he's left it on a ship or in a strongbox we are all wasting the afternoon."

### Event 180 <!-- id: myth-event-8e6f90b1fcbe, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T20:35:34.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Hanzo di Teufel -->

THE BOX AND THE NOTE. A fitted merchant's sample case, bought off a chandler for more than it's worth. Gardwen, reading over her shoulder: "You've spelled *champion* wrong." -- "He'll get the sense of it."

Nerissa listens to the whole plan and then says, "I will get you the servants. I will not get you the box."

She gets the servants. Marisette does, really -- three of her girls have worked Tourney service for years, and one of them, Corliss's youngest, is pouring in the Governor's enclosure this afternoon because she always does. Ravella adds a gondolier who ferries the Guild party and a woman who's been carrying ice up from the Shoals since dawn. Nobody has to be told a story. Marisette says *look at what's round the Baron's neck and tell me* and they say *right you are*.

The box Magda sorts out herself, from a chandler off the ground, for more than it's worth. The note takes a while because she writes like a woman who learned late.

> ***YOUR SHIP IS ON THE BOTTOM. YOUR MAN IS DEAD.***
> ***I HAVE YOUR CHAMPION AND HIS BONES.***
> ***-- THE PIG WOMAN***

Gardwen reads it over her shoulder and says nothing for a while, and then: "You've spelled *champion* wrong."

"He'll get the sense of it."

### Event 181 <!-- id: myth-event-045cecbbb8f3, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-07T20:36:01.000000000, session: 1, involves: Hanzo di Teufel -->

Corliss's youngest pours at four feet for an hour. 9/70 -- beautifully. And 21 on the other roll: she is seen, and he SMILES at her.

She's eighteen, has poured at four Tourneys, and is very good at being furniture. She goes in with the wine four times over the hour and comes out with this:

**It's on him.** Gold, a flattened oval about the size of a thumbnail, on a fine chain -- worn *under* the shirt, but the chain shows at the collar and the shape of it shows through the green when he leans forward.

**And he touches it.** Not fidgeting. Every so often his hand goes flat to his chest over it, briefly, the way a man checks a purse -- and once, when a Guildmaster leaned in to toast him, he moved his shoulder to put it further away. "He does it about every quarter hour. And after he does it, he looks round."

Then she tells the rest, and her hands are not steady. "He caught me." Fourth pour. She was closer than she needed to be and she looked a half-second too long, and when she came back up he was already looking at her.

And he *smiled*. And said -- pleasantly, in front of two Guildmasters and the Prince -- "*You've very good hands, girl. Who do you serve?*" She said the Guild steward's name, because it's true. He said: "*Of course.*" And went back to his cup.

"He wasn't angry," she says. "That's what's wrong with it. **He was *pleased*.**"

Marisette has already got her off the island and onto a boat, which she did before telling Magda.

### Event 182 <!-- id: myth-event-c610794086b4, type: scene, at: 2026-09-07T20:37:59.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Temerach Nebulo, Temple Isle -->

THE ONLY QUIET HOUR IN FOUR DAYS. "Relax. Watch the tournament." She buys fried fish and finds a place on the rail and, for about an hour, is just an old woman at a fair. It is excellent.

The Archery runs in three passes. Twenty-two starters. By the third pass there are four.

**First pass -- fixed targets at eighty paces.** She hits everything and the crowd doesn't notice her, because everyone hits at eighty paces.

**Second pass -- moving targets, boats poled across the gap between the islands. A 3. Critical.**

That's when six thousand people find out who she is.

The boats go across left to right at a walking pace with the targets standing on them, and the field is allowed six shafts. The Guild man ahead of her puts four in and the crowd is very pleased with four.

Temerach shoots six.

**She does not track.** That's the thing you notice from the rail -- everyone else swings the bow with the boat, and she doesn't. She puts the bow where the boat is *going to be*, and waits, and looses, and **the target arrives at the arrow**.

Six shafts. Six hits. The last two go into the same target inside a handspan while the boat is at its furthest and fastest.

The noise the ground makes is not applause. It's the noise people make when they've been shown something. Somebody near her says "*that's the Javelin, that's her, that's the Lakelady's Javelin*" and it goes down the rail like a wave.

**Third pass -- cross-channel, two hundred and fifty paces over open water with the wind off the Mouth.** She and the Guild archer both come through it. He's genuinely good, and on any other day in any other year he wins this and dines out on it for a decade. Today he is standing next to somebody who put two arrows in the same moving target at range and then apologised to the judges for the grouping.

They call it for **Temerach Nebulo**, Knight of the Swords of the Lake. A purse, a silver bracer, and **access to the Governor's court**.

### Event 183 <!-- id: myth-event-b016c72d7cb2, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-07T20:41:51.000000000, session: 1, involves: High Priestess Nerissa, Prince Emeric, Temerach Nebulo -->

THE WARNING DOESN'T GET THROUGH. 94/80. Vosk takes the bracer out of the High Priestess's hands three steps from the dais, and she stands there with them empty.

The one window was a good one: Nerissa presents the prize. It's her Tourney, they're her Order's colours, and the High Priestess handing a Sword of the Lake her bracer in front of the city is the most ordinary thing that will happen all afternoon. Four words -- "**Box coming. He has it.**"

Nerissa is three steps from the dais with the bracer in her hands when a man in Governor's livery comes down the steps and takes it out of them.

**Vosk.** The chamberlain who kept Lilura in an anteroom for four hours.

He does it beautifully. A small bow, a murmured word about the Prince wishing to honour the champion of the Archery *personally*, and it is protocol, and it is correct, and there is not one thing the High Priestess of the Lake Lady can do about it in front of six thousand people without making a scene that the three men beside Emeric would enjoy enormously.

She stands there with empty hands.

Emeric -- who is twenty-four and does not understand what has just been done with him -- presents the bracer to Temerach himself, delightedly, and says something complimentary about the second pass. Temerach receives it, bows, and is invited to stay in the canopy.

**She has no idea what's coming.**

And the box arrives exactly on time. A boy in a chandler's apron comes up the enclosure steps with a fitted wooden case, hands it to a steward, says *for the Baron di Teufel, paid delivery*, and is gone before anybody thinks to ask anything.

### Event 184 <!-- id: myth-event-b6f5612018bc, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-07T20:44:18.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Hanzo di Teufel -->

The Baron slides the lid, looks into the box for about two seconds, and slides it back. 58/94 -- HE HOLDS IT. He says something to a Guildmaster and the Guildmaster laughs.

From a hundred and forty feet Magda sees almost nothing, and that's the point.

He slides the lid. He looks down into the box for about two seconds. He slides the lid back. He says something to the Guildmaster beside him and the Guildmaster laughs.

Then he sets the case down beside his chair, stands, and makes a small apologetic gesture to Prince Emeric -- *forgive me, a moment* -- and Emeric waves him away good-naturedly, and he steps out of the back of the canopy with his cup still in his hand.

He does not hurry. He does not look round the ground once.

He is very, very good.

Gardwen: "Did he--" -- "He read it. He's going for his boat."

Down the public rail, through the fried-fish crowd, past the ale trestles, round the back of the colonnade where the ground gives way to worked stone and the noise drops off like a door closing. Magda gets there first -- she was closer, and he was being unhurried and correct.

Then footsteps in the passage. Unhurried. One set. Then a second set, quicker, catching up -- "*My lord -- my lord, the herald's calling the Single Combat--*"

And Hanzo di Teufel says, perfectly pleasantly, from the other side of the arch:

"Yes. Tell them my champion withdraws. Injury in training."

A pause.

"And find out who delivered that box."

### Event 185 <!-- id: myth-event-5d6cecd75063, type: combat, at: 2026-09-07T20:48:14.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Hanzo di Teufel, Temerach Nebulo -->

"Spit on the ground to add to the moisture. Hel's waiting for you Baron. Time you went down." 43/85 hit; his evade 15/36 -- both succeed, and the higher roll takes it.

Close to, Baron Hanzo di Teufel is a well-made man of about fifty in dragon-green with grey at the temples and very good boots, and the cup is still in his left hand, and he has not spilled a drop of it.

He gets three steps down the stair before he registers that there is an old woman in a Lake Lady novice's vestment standing against the wall with a loaded staff sling. He stops.

"Ah," he says. And then, with what sounds like genuine warmth: "**The pig woman.**"

He hasn't put the cup down.

"You've cost me a great deal in three days and I would very much like to know how you did the ship. I'll pay for that answer, and I pay well. Or we can do the other thing, and you should know before you choose that you are standing on wet stone at the bottom of a walled passage, which is a *dreadful* place to fight a man like me."

Behind him, in the arch, unhurried, blocking it: **Temerach Nebulo**, with an arrow already on the string.

Magda spits on the step between them.

He throws himself sideways and he is quick for fifty, and the stone still finds him. It takes him high in the chest and turns him completely round, and the cup goes out of his hand and shatters on the step, and he lands badly on wet stone and comes up on one elbow with his court silk torn open and a spreading bruise the shape of a fist coming up underneath it.

And now he is not warm and he is not amused.

He gets a hand up -- palm out, fingers spread, and she has seen exactly this once before, at a ford, when a druid asked a favour of the water -- and the air in that passage goes *dry*.

*(His sheet: Arcane Casting 88. Seventeen magic points. No armour. Lightning Bolt. Wall of Fire. Hold Person. Sleep. Charm Being. Fly.)*

"**My lord Baron. Put the hand down.** You are under arrest by warrant of the Order of the Lake Lady for the profanation of the Tourney and the sacred channel. There are two Guildmasters and a Prince forty feet above us. I have been trying to say those words to you for four years."

His hand does not go down.

### Event 186 <!-- id: myth-event-9213cbd2ce55, type: combat, at: 2026-09-07T20:50:38.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Hanzo di Teufel -->

"Close fast and break his wrist with my staff." Undefended, so the differential hands her Choose Location. The spell comes apart with a sound like a held breath being let go.

He's prone, unarmoured, and he has chosen to finish the spell rather than defend himself, because he is a man who has never once been hit by anything.

She doesn't throw the staff. She goes down the three steps with it held short in both hands like a woman going at a rat in a grain store, and brings the end of it down on the wrist.

There's a crack that is mostly the staff and partly not. The hand shuts.

And whatever was gathering in that passage -- the dry, tight, wrong feeling of air being *asked* for something -- comes apart all at once with a sound like a held breath being let go, and the temperature comes back, and somewhere above them a gull starts complaining again.

Hanzo di Teufel makes a noise through his teeth. It is the first entirely honest sound he has made in three days.

He cradles the arm against his chest and looks up at her from the wet stone with his hair come loose and his silk torn and a stone-bruise spreading under it.

**Fifty years of never once being touched, undone on a service stair by an old woman with a stick.**

"You *hag*--"

### Event 187 <!-- id: myth-event-7a3e0c9883f5, type: combat, at: 2026-09-07T20:52:03.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Hanzo di Teufel, Hesper, Thuban Eta -->

THE LOCKET. Unarmed 12 against 128 -- CRITICAL. She slaps his good hand out of the air, takes a fistful of silk and chain, and holds him there eye to eye before she pulls.

His left hand -- the good one -- moves very slightly toward his own collar.

She slaps it down out of the air like a woman swatting a wasp off a pie. Then she takes a fistful of green silk and collar and *chain*, all together, and puts her face about four inches from his.

And she doesn't rip it. Not straight away.

She holds him there, eye to eye, on the wet stone, and lets him get a proper look at what has been coming up this stair for him -- which is not a knight and not a priestess and not a magistrate. **It's an old woman who keeps pigs, sent by a goddess he has never once thought about, wearing a novice's vestment she has no right to, with fried fish on her breath.**

He stares back and he is, for the first time, entirely out of arithmetic.

*Then* she pulls.

The chain doesn't break at the clasp. It breaks across the back of his neck, and it takes skin, and he cries out.

And it comes away in her fist: gold, a flattened oval about the size of a thumbnail, warm -- *properly* warm, the warmth of something that has been alive against a chest for a hundred years -- and heavier than a thing that size has any right to be.

Inside it, something moves. Not rattles. *Moves.*

Hanzo di Teufel goes very still underneath her. And then, in a voice that has lost every bit of its charm: "You have no idea what you are holding. You cannot *open* that, you stupid woman, if you open that here he will go into the nearest--"

"**I know.**"

That's Hesper. Standing in the arch at the top of the stair with his bag, entirely out of breath, having run the length of the colonnade. "She's not going to open it here."

Temerach steps down and puts a boot between the Baron's shoulders and gets his good arm behind him, and it is over, and it took about ninety seconds.

Above them, six thousand people are being told that the di Teufel champion withdraws through injury, and there is a certain amount of booing.

### Event 188 <!-- id: myth-event-12a94dd73161, type: scene, at: 2026-09-07T20:55:17.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Hesper, Temerach Nebulo -->

"That shot was one of the most beautiful things I've seen in my life and I'm a lot older than I look." The finest archer in the realm goes completely to pieces for about a second and a half.

Hesper takes the locket in a folded cloth and does not touch it with his skin, and puts it in a box within a box, and his hands are not quite steady. "Bones, door, and the latch. That's all three. For the first time in a hundred years, all three are in one place."

Temerach has the Baron's good arm behind him and a knee on him and is not even slightly out of breath.

Magda tells her about the second pass. Six shafts, six hits, two in the same target at the furthest and fastest, and the apology to the judges.

She looks up. And the finest archer in the realm, who has just arrested the man she has hunted for four years, goes completely to pieces for about a second and a half -- she gets pink to the ears, and looks at the step, and says:

"The sixth was high."

"It was in the target."

"It was *high*." Firmly. Then, quieter, and clearly having to make herself do it: "...Thank you. That's a kind thing to say and I never know what to do with them."

She hauls the Baron up onto his knees.

### Event 189 <!-- id: myth-event-47ce6bbbb501, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T20:56:00.000000000, session: 1, involves: Conall Bjornlasch, Randall, Hanzo di Teufel, Temerach Nebulo -->

TEMERACH'S OWN TRUTH: "Conall Bjornlasch is his son. Bastard-born, of an elven mage he ruined, twenty-two years ago. I have known since before I took the boy in. He does not know."

"And he has a twin brother somewhere in this city that neither of them knows about either."

She tightens the cord on the Baron's wrists.

"So what I need is for that boy to hear it from me, today, in a room of my choosing -- before he hears it shouted across a courtroom by *him*."

And when Magda asks what she needs:

"Two things, and neither of them is fighting.

"**First -- keep him off me for an hour.** Nerissa will want him questioned immediately and she'll be right to, and the moment that starts he begins buying himself time by throwing his own blood on the table. I need one hour with Conall before that room opens. You have some influence with the priestess. I have none. I've never been able to talk to people."

"And the second?"

"**Be there when I tell him.**"

That one takes her by surprise.

"I have known this boy since he was nineteen and I have lied to him every day of it, for what I thought were good reasons, and in about an hour he is going to find out that the man being led up those steps in irons is his father." Her jaw sets. "He will look at me and he will understand that I knew, and I will deserve every bit of what comes next.

"**And there needs to be somebody in that room who has nothing to do with any of it. Somebody he has never met, who has no stake, who will simply say the true thing plainly when it's needed.**

"You're extremely good at that. It's a rarer skill than shooting."

### Event 190 <!-- id: myth-event-45958b7082a2, type: scene, at: 2026-09-07T21:00:03.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Cailan, Thuban Eta, Lost Isle -->

"I have my own goddess to tend to." The tide is thin. Thuban: "You came back." -- "Aye." -- "Then I am ready, and I have been ready for a thousand years, and I would like you to be quick."

Nerissa looks at the channel as they come down the temple stair and says "*it's thin*," in the voice of a woman reporting a fact she wishes she couldn't feel.

Forty minutes to Lost Isle. The boy, carried, awake, and very frightened. Gardwen beside him the whole way with her hand in his. Ruhi in the bottom of the boat. Hesper, with a box within a box. Nerissa, who has left her own Tourney running behind her without a backward glance. Twelve Swords. Ravella, and four of hers in the water alongside the whole way, which no boatman in this city has ever had happen to them.

The drowned street. The swept stair. The bones laid out on the stone with the wire cut off the wrists and a borrowed axe across them.

Hesper sets the locket down at the head of the bones and opens the outer box and then, with a great deal of care, the inner one. The thing inside it moves.

They put the boy down on the stone at the top of the stair, and Gardwen holds him, and Thuban Eta comes up into his face for what everyone there understands is the last time. He looks at the bones. Then at the locket. Then at Magda.

"You came back," he says. -- "Aye."

"Then I am ready, and I have been ready for a thousand years, and I would like you to be quick -- **because he is very tired and I have taken enough of his life.**"

### Event 191 <!-- id: myth-event-11b118fbcd03, type: skill-roll, at: 2026-09-07T21:00:24.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Cailan, Thuban Eta, Lost Isle -->

THE RITE. 28 against 66. It takes. She goes two steps down into the black water and stands exactly where nine men stood over a bound knight, and does not look away.

She does it exactly as she did it before, because it was right before.

She says the name. **Thuban Eta.** Out loud, first, so everyone on that rock has to hear it.

She tells the tale -- the valley he wouldn't burn, the argument he won in front of men who killed him for it, the boat in the dark, the wire on the wrists, the water. In a market-day voice to a priestess and a mage and a druid and a wolf and twelve Swords and a Court listening from underneath.

She names the wrong, out loud, so it's in the air where it can be heard: *this was murder, and it was cowardice, and no one ever answered for it.*

And then the part that costs.

She goes down two steps into the black water in a novice's vestment she has no right to, up to her knees, and stands exactly where nine men stood over a bound knight a thousand years ago and did nothing -- and she does not look away.

*"You went in here and nobody came. I came. You're not theirs and you're not his and you were never Valhalla's, because they cheated you of the death that buys it.*

*"You're hers. You've always been hers. And she takes the ones nobody sings about -- but only when somebody finally brings them.*

*"**Mother of the darkness. This is Thuban Eta and I have brought him. He's yours. Take him home.**"*

**The locket opens on the stone by itself.** Not dramatically. It simply comes apart, the way a shell does when whatever was holding it shut has stopped bothering.

And the water goes *warm*. It comes up past her knees like a draught from an open door in a cold house, and every lamp on that island bends toward it at once, and under the surface something enormous and patient turns over and pays attention.

On the stone, the boy arches once against Gardwen's arms and makes a sound that is two voices and then one voice and then no voice at all.

And something goes down that stair. She doesn't see it. She *feels* it go past her in the water -- unhurried, the way a big fish goes past your legs in a river -- and there is a moment, exactly one, where she has the absolutely unmistakable sense of being **looked at** by something that is grateful and is not going to say so, because it's a soldier.

Then it's gone. The bones settle very slightly, the way bones do. And the water is just water.

Gardwen's brother is limp in her arms with his eyes open. Breathing. Then he says, in a boy's voice with nobody behind it: "...it's quiet."

### Event 192 <!-- id: myth-event-9e6ef7bde6fe, type: scene, at: 2026-09-07T21:03:00.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Cailan, High Priestess Nerissa, Lost Isle -->

THE COLLAPSE. Eleven people killed in two days, and she comes apart completely on her knees on wet stone. Nerissa turns her back and makes everyone else stop looking.

She comes up out of the water and her legs don't work properly and she doesn't much care.

She gets to them on her knees on the wet stone and puts her arms round both of them at once -- the girl who has been carrying ten years of a dead brother, and the boy who has just had a thousand-year-old man taken out of him -- and holds on.

And then, after seventy years of not doing this in front of anybody, she comes apart completely.

It's not graceful. It's the ugly kind, from the chest, the kind that hurts your throat -- a great old woman in a stolen vestment howling on a rock in a drowned street, with her face in a druid's hair and one hand knotted in a boy's shirt.

Gardwen has got hold of her arm with her free hand and is not letting go of it. The boy doesn't understand who she is at all. He puts his head against her anyway, because she's warm and because there's nothing left in him to do anything else with. Ruhi shoves her whole head under her elbow and stays there.

Nobody on that island says a word.

**Nerissa** turns her back -- not away from her; she turns and faces the others and *makes them stop looking*, which is the single kindest thing she does all week.

**Hesper** sits down heavily on the step with the empty locket in his hands and stares at nothing.

**Ravella** goes to the edge of the stair, and crouches, and says something down into the water that isn't in any language anyone knows -- and something answers, and this time everybody hears it.

**Corvin** -- twenty-two, who was sick against a wall for her -- is crying openly and doesn't bother to hide it.

When she finally gets her breath the light has moved on the water, and the boy is asleep against Gardwen with his mouth open, and Gardwen has not moved a muscle in case it wakes him.

She looks at Magda over the top of his head. Her face is absolutely wrecked and about ten years younger.

"Magda. I don't know his name." A pause. "**He was seven. We called him Bird. I don't know what his *name* is.**"

### Event 193 <!-- id: myth-event-a0308ca1101e, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T21:07:55.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Cailan -->

"Call him Bird then. It'd be a lot better than anything those bastards called him."

"And he'll pick his own when he's ready. That's how it works -- you get given one, and then one day you take one. **Nobody's given him anything since he was seven.**"

She nods, and doesn't trust herself to say anything, and puts her cheek back down on the top of his head.

### Event 194 <!-- id: myth-event-25376cd62ff9, type: scene, at: 2026-09-07T21:08:00.000000000, session: 1, involves: Magda, Ila, Marisette, The Pearl -->

Marisette takes it standing up, with her hands flat on the desk. "*Three* of them. At the school." Then she pours two glasses of something that costs more than Magda's farm and does not make a speech about it.

"And you got the girls back?" -- "Priestess has them. Hesper's working on it. He says two of them are -- *shallow*. Recent. He thinks they'll come out." -- "And the third?" -- "He didn't say."

She's quiet for a moment.

"Ann Yannick keeps her work and gets a pension out of me whether she wants one or not. Corliss's girl gets whatever she asks for and I'll be telling her that myself. And Ila--"

She stops.

"**Ila is going to want to see that boy.**"

### Event 195 <!-- id: myth-event-b4115634a652, type: scene, at: 2026-09-07T21:08:30.000000000, session: 1, involves: Crowbill, High Priestess Nerissa, Temple Isle -->

NERISSA PAYS HER DEBT FROM THE TEMPLE STEPS, in front of the Governor and every Guild in Purewater. "The canal folk kept the Lady's water clean." She says it twice. Then: "Say it back to me."

She names the desecration. She names the arrest, by warrant, of Baron Hanzo di Teufel. She names the fouling of the sacred channel and the fact that she rang the alarm and was told to stop -- and she looks directly at Prince Emeric's chamberlain when she says that part, and Vosk goes the colour of paper.

And then she says the sentence she owes.

***"The canal folk kept the Lady's water clean."***

She says it slowly. She lets it sit. And when the ground doesn't quite know what to do with it, she says it again, louder, and adds: *"Say it back to me,"* -- and six thousand people do.

Somewhere on the water, a very forgettable man with a crow on his hand hears the whole city say it.

### Event 196 <!-- id: myth-event-b04ae47b942c, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T21:13:48.000000000, session: 1, involves: Conall Bjornlasch, Magda, Temerach Nebulo -->

MAGDA TAKES THE VESTMENT OFF BEFORE SHE ANSWERS. Conall, to the only stranger in the room: "Is she telling the truth?" -- "Yeah. Course she is. Go find your brother."

Temerach doesn't sit down. She stands with her hands at her sides like a woman giving a report to a superior officer, which is the only register she has, and it turns out to be exactly the right one.

"Conall. I am going to tell you four things and then you may do whatever you like to me.

"**One.** Baron Hanzo di Teufel is your father. You were born of an elven mage he seduced and ruined, twenty-two years ago, and she fled to this city and died here.
"**Two.** You have a brother. A twin. He is somewhere in Purewater and he does not know about you either.
"**Three.** I have known both of those since before I took you in at nineteen. Every day since, I have chosen not to tell you.
"**Four.** He was arrested this afternoon, by me, on the water-stair, and he will be questioned tonight -- and he will say all of this out loud in front of a magistrate to buy himself an hour. So you were going to hear it today whatever I did. I wanted it to be from me. That is not the same as it being *right*."

Then she stops. Completely. No softening, no *I'm sorry*, nothing.

Conall is quick, so he doesn't get to have the easy reaction. He goes straight past *you're lying* and lands on all the small things at once. *That's why you found me at nineteen. That's why we came to Purewater. That's why you entered the Archery.*

"How long have I been the reason?" His voice goes. He looks up, and he isn't looking at Temerach. He's looking at the stranger, the one with no stake.

"**Is she telling the truth?**"

Magda pulls the sash off over her head and drops the whole vestment on the chair, and stands there in her own filthy shirt and ringmail, which answers his question about who's talking before she opens her mouth.

"Yeah. Course she is."

That's all. She doesn't dress it and she doesn't explain it and she doesn't tell him it'll be all right.

"Go find your brother."

He stares at her. "Where?"

"Don't know. It's your city, not mine." A shrug. "But he's got your face, so ask around. Someone'll have seen it."

### Event 197 <!-- id: myth-event-9e8c11eee2f8, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T21:14:00.000000000, session: 1, involves: Conall Bjornlasch, Randall, Hanzo di Teufel, Temerach Nebulo -->

"He's better looking than Santo. Does that make you a Baronet?" Temerach: "No. And yes. And that is the most dangerous thing in this room."

Temerach makes a small strangled noise at *better looking than Santo*. Then she answers the other thing, because she has spent four years in law books.

"Bastards do not inherit. Not in the border law and not here -- that's clean. But Santo di Teufel was the only legitimate issue, and Santo is dead, and if Hanzo is attainted for desecration and demon-craft then the barony and the tower and everything in it goes back to the Crown to dispose of.

"**And when a Crown disposes of a border holding, it looks for *blood*.** Not because bastards have a right. Because it's tidier to hand a wild border march to somebody the local men will follow than to install a stranger and pay for a garrison for twenty years."

She looks at Conall.

"So no, you are not his heir. But there are men in three counties who will hear *the di Teufel bastard* and start doing arithmetic -- and there is a border tower full of a hundred years of that family's work sitting empty tonight, and there is a movement of people who wanted a Dragon King badly enough to fund all of this, and **they have just lost their candidate**.

"You and your brother are going to be *extremely* interesting to some very bad people by the end of the month. Both of you. And neither of you has any protection at all except the Order and me."

Conall, hoarsely: "And him. He's alive. He knows I exist now?"

"He will by tonight."

### Event 198 <!-- id: myth-event-5bc3559d8d76, type: decision, at: 2026-09-07T21:21:17.000000000, session: 1, involves: Conall Bjornlasch, Magda, Randall, Marisette, Thorne, The Moist Oyster -->

"Ask Marisette to get him. Let's make it a party." And Conall and Randall look at each other across the Moist Oyster for the first time in twenty-two years.

Marisette does not need to be asked twice, and does not need an hour. "Randall." She says it the way you'd name a nephew. "Pearl-born, raised in Corliss's house and then Asphodel's, thieves like a gentleman and drinks like one too. I have known that boy since he was *four*."

Then she stops writing and looks up. "He's had a *strange* three days, that boy. Somebody put dragon-gold on the street for a face like his and he's been sleeping somewhere different every night and hasn't told anybody why."

**The Moist Oyster**, that evening. Magda picks it because Thorne is owed the true version, once, and because it is the only room in Purewater where a Guildmaster, a thief, a priestess and a wolf can all be in the same place and nobody will comment.

Constantine sends food he can't afford to send. Marisette sends wine nobody will be able to afford to drink again. Ravella comes in through the water-door with four of hers, which stops the room for a moment.

**Ila** is there because she threatened Constantine with a very specific kind of hell, and she has attached herself to **Bird** -- who is sitting in the warmest corner in three cloaks, eating steadily, saying almost nothing, with Gardwen on one side and a wolf across his feet.

**Nerissa does not come.** She sends Lilura, and a note, and a cask.

**Hesper** comes late, from the school, and says the two shallow girls are out and sleeping, and does not say anything about the third.

And **Thorne** is in his corner with his chalk, watching the door.

At about the ninth bell, Asphodel comes in with a young man behind her. Dark. Twenty-two. Well-dressed in a way that isn't his money. He is halfway through saying something to her when he sees the man standing up at the far table -- the one with his own face on him -- and the sentence just stops.

The whole room stops with it.

**Conall and Randall look at each other across the Moist Oyster for the first time in twenty-two years.**

Neither of them says anything.

And Thorne, very quietly, from his corner, to nobody at all: "*Oh*, that's good."

### Event 199 <!-- id: myth-event-3329407eca4c, type: combat, at: 2026-09-07T21:24:04.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Blau, Ravella -->

CH.10 -- The Mouth. Ravella cools forty feet of burning hull; twelve of Crowbill's canal men go up the lines. Magda stays in the punt and slings: a critical under the raised arm blocks the rail, then a stone into Blau's unarmoured gut from forty yards. She goes up the rope on Brawn (berserk making it one grade easier) and a Luck Point, comes over the rail screaming Hel's fury, and cuts through to Blau. Gardwen's Entangle takes the whole waist of the ship. Magda kills Blau with a critical placed in the wound she had already made, then -- unable to break the rage (two failed Willpower rolls) -- kills nine helpless entangled men before it lets go of her.

BLAU, dying, to his men: 'Cut the boy's throat.'
BLAU, earlier: 'That's her. The northern woman. Nobody told me she was SEVENTY.'
IN THE CABIN: chalk kept fresh daily, a cot bolted down, and a boy of seventeen whose upper left chest has been opened and reclosed four hundred times.
CAILAN and THUBAN, one throat: '--Don't--' / '--she's not here for you.'
THUBAN: 'I have been listening to you come up this ship for a quarter of an hour... Get us off this ship before the other one comes back with my bones.'
MECHANICS: Ravella 36/75 cooled the hull but 22 meant the deck noticed; Magda's slings 69/57 miss, 1/57 CRITICAL, 23/57 hit Blau's abdomen for 6 (0/6 serious); Brawn 95/71 failed, Luck spent, 59 succeeded; berserk axe 81/85 with a Bleed; two evades put her prone (Mythras evade = prone); Blau killed on a 6/57 critical, Choose Location into the abdomen, -7/6 MAJOR. Berserk ended: Fresh -> Winded.

### Event 200 <!-- id: myth-event-4575ee40c42d, type: scene, at: 2026-09-07T21:24:04.000000000, session: 1, involves: Gardwen, Magda, Hesper, High Priestess Nerissa, Ravella -->

CH.11 -- Lost Isle, the failed rite. Magda's first attempt for the two drowned men failed (87/52) -- Ravella corrected the form: do not ask Hel to take them, GIVE them to her and say somebody came back. The litany worked (52/66).
